  • The test found a shared-code bug: UObject::RemoveNames ends with GConfig->UnloadAll() (the cache is keyed by FName, whose names may be invalid afterwards), and the Flush(0) above it was compiled out. SetString only marks the ini dirty; only SaveConfig flushes. So every value set but not yet written was dropped at the next level change or at StaticExit (which runs BEFORE appExit's flush): the -ini: values, FURL::SaveURLConfig's [DefaultPlayer] values after every map load, the launcher's resolution switches. Found as 2 FAIL in the first ucc write-back check (logs/c1/c1-ini-run2.txt). The owner's report "saving doesn't seem to work" (web) is the same hole for anything held in the inis.
  • A browser tab is closed, never shut down, so nothing may wait for an exit flush.

Decisions and alternatives

  • Values are WRITTEN into the ini (SetString marks it dirty), not applied for this run only: engine-ut2004's deliberate trade (one mechanism; the web daemon keeps the ini). There is no launch-only override.
  • Log malformed tokens (deviation from the template's silence) because an unrecognised switch is otherwise invisible.
  • FLUSHCONFIG is the template's command, kept under the same name so the shared frontend daemon needs no UC2-specific code.
  • ServerName cannot be the test key and cannot be seeded by -ini:: the profile code copies the profile's own ServerName over GameReplicationInfo.default.ServerName at start-up (PlayerProfile.uc:764).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations

  • A value with a double quote inside it ends the token. The command line is limited to 1,023 characters. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 10, feature 9.)
  • The RemoveNames flush is a behaviour change on every platform: the ini files are written at every level change when dirty (not on the Xbox build). It writes nothing when nothing is dirty.
  • An override persists: a launcher that sends Name= once overwrites the ini value for later runs without the switch.
  • Not verified: the browser round trip of flushconfig and the daemon's 5-second scan by the engine side alone; the later browser saves spec (LOG.md D-3 entry) exercised it for profiles.

Status - measured: c1-ini-overrides.sh 30 PASS, 0 FAIL; coordinator's re-run on main "ALL PASS" (LOG.md 2026-10-07); c1-saves.sh 11 PASS; under wasm/Node the three -ini: tokens and the write-back work (c1-web-saves.sh 16 PASS).

Off switch - none for the overrides (they only act when the switch is on the command line). FLUSHCONFIG acts only when called.

#10. What the web build persists: flat profile files (C-2)

What the player/owner sees - on the web a profile and the inis survive closing the tab; natively the profile is a directory per profile.

Where (commit 22e9dbf Engine: the web build keeps a profile as one flat file Profiles/<name>.PRO ..., 61c9f48 for the native inventory)

  • Epic's code changed: Engine/Src/ULoadSaveManager.cpp (41 lines added): UC2_FLAT_PROFILE_FILES (defaults to 1 under __EMSCRIPTEN__, 0 elsewhere, or -DUC2_FLAT_PROFILE_FILES=1; :18-25) switches five functions - load, save, exists, delete, list (:109,134,148,186 etc.) - from Profiles/<name>/AVARIS_PROFILE.PRO to Profiles/<name>.PRO in the engine's default directory (/System/ in the browser). The header (and the Xbox subclass's virtual GetProfilePath) are untouched. The bytes inside are identical.
  • Engine/Inc/ULoadSaveManager.h (M13, 4399e22): a saved object reference is always 4 bytes (see feature 12).
  • Core/Inc/FFileManagerUnix.h:604 (M13, 0c9d5d2): Exists() is true for directories; and FFileManagerGeneric.h DeleteFiles (D-3, 10b802f). Without these no profile could be loaded or deleted outside Epic's Windows file manager.
  • UCC.cpp:335-410: ucc-bin profiletest save|load|list|exists|delete <profile> <file> writes a profile with a deterministic value in every plain property through the game's own save path, or loads one, and exports every property as text (the cross-width and cross-layout save test).
  • Frontend (per the notebooks, not read): userFiles: ["System/Profiles/*.PRO"]; network.playerName (System/User.ini, section DefaultPlayer, key Name); manifestExclude entries for System/Profiles/**, *.PRO, System/Shot*.bmp, System/AutoPatch.txt (ufrontend d6554d4).
  • Tests: tools/dev/c1-saves.sh (native file inventory by diffing a 1,231-file tree before and after a session: it added only Avaris.ini, User.ini, Avaris.log and Profiles/<name>/AVARIS_PROFILE.PRO; plus the restart check: the profile is listed and loaded by a new process, CharacterClassName comes from the file), 11 PASS; tools/dev/c1-web-saves.sh (Node, 16 PASS: flat file, list, exists, delete, byte-identical to the native file, each build loads the other's file).

How the parts fit - the web host's persistence daemon lists ONE directory per userFiles entry and allows a wildcard only in the last path segment, so System/Profiles/*/AVARIS_PROFILE.PRO is rejected by the frontend's compiler; the engine therefore writes a flat file the glob System/Profiles/*.PRO matches. The host persists the inis through its engine-file derivation and the flushconfig call. Natively the layout is unchanged. What is NOT persisted on purpose: Avaris.log, Shot*.bmp, Detected.ini/Running.ini, AutoPatch.txt, uc2.uprof, the empty Save/ and Cache/ folders.

Why - the owner asked what must persist (PLAN.md C-2). The inventory found exactly one save game: the profile (UPlayerProfile::Serialize: settings, button layout, ladder progression, unlocks, stats, ~57 KB). UT2004's web profiles are flat too (Saves/*.uvx).

Decisions and alternatives

  • Coordinator's decision: flat file on the web. Rejected/noted alternative: keep the layout and teach the frontend's compileUserFiles/ scanUserFiles one directory-wildcard segment, then revert 22e9dbf. Also noted: a fallback needing no directory at all would keep profiles directly in System/.
  • Native keeps Epic's layout (the Xbox save-game layout).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations

  • Two layouts for the same data; code that names a profile path must go through ULoadSaveManager. A profile copied between native and web needs renaming by hand.
  • A profile name that is not a legal file name on the web filesystem is not discussed in the notebooks.
  • Left to the browser: WasmFS makes /System read-only (0555) for preloaded files and refuses mkdir below it; the frontend's backend ensureDir is expected to make it writable. The notebook marked this "not verified" under Node; the later browser saves spec passed (LOG.md D-3 entry: "saves 1/1 (profile restored after closing the tab)"), so it works in the headless Chromium used.
  • Nothing is saved while the profile is the transient default ("No Profile"): every setting lives in the profile (profiles.md, other section).

Status - measured: as listed; coordinator's re-run c1-saves.sh ALL PASS; browser saves spec 1/1 on three later coordinator runs (one run stopped by the memory guard, passed on rerun).

Off switch - -DUC2_FLAT_PROFILE_FILES=0 at build time reverts the web layout.

#11. Windows: MinGW-w64 i686, run under wine (M12; windows64 in feature 12)

What the player/owner sees - uc2.exe (Windows-subsystem program, no console) and ucc.exe (console) plus OpenAL32.dll in System/. The package folder run/m12-package (made by tools/dev/package-windows.sh) is what to copy to a Windows PC. Nothing was ever run on real Windows.

Where (commits 754ce47 build, IpDrv, UC2ALAudio: every package library compiles for Windows (MinGW i686), 6aa92ca Core, Engine: compile with MinGW-w64 i686, 1e7f700 UCC, SDLLaunch, Engine: ucc.exe and uc2.exe link with MinGW-w64, 2024551 Core, tools: Windows console Ctrl+C handler (Epic's), wine wrapper, run tree with exe/dll, 558956d smoke script, bc382c1 packaging, merge 5ee457f)

  • Build: preset windows (CMakePresets.json: MINGW_TARGET_ARCH=i686, MINGW_THREAD_MODEL=posix, -fno-omit-frame-pointer), cmake/toolchain-mingw-w64.cmake; root CMakeLists.txt:99-105 (-static -static-libgcc -static-libstdc++; without -static the OpenAL DLL imported libwinpthread-1.dll), :205-216 (SDL2 with SDL2main for MinGW, no X11), :237-288 (OpenAL Soft as OpenAL32.dll, Linux backends off); Source/UCC/CMakeLists.txt:39-52 (ucc.exe, console, ws2_32 winmm); Source/SDLLaunch/CMakeLists.txt:45-52 (uc2.exe, WIN32_EXECUTABLE; SDL2main not used, MinGW's CRT calls main() for either subsystem).
  • Epic's code changed (each guarded so only MinGW sees a difference; the notebook's table, re-checked against the code):
    • Core/Inc/wordwrap.h: on !_XBOX && _WIN32 branch before the portable one - <winnt.h> types WCHAR as wchar_t, distinct from UNICHAR; file-local WW_WCHAR/WW_LPCWSTR/WW_LPWSTR are #defined over the Win32 names for the two TUs that include it (MSVC 7.1 had WCHAR == unsigned short).
    • Core/Src/UnUnix.cpp: appSystemTime64 uses GetLocalTime+SystemTimeToFileTime on _WIN32 (:3654; no tm_gmtoff in the MinGW CRT); appWinCtrlHandler (:2752, Epic's CtrlCHandler: first Ctrl+C/Break/close sets GIsRequestingExit; a second goes to Windows' default handler where Epic threw appErrorf("Aborted!") on the handler thread); crash reporter SetUnhandledExceptionFilter, std::set_terminate, SIGABRT (:2551-2713, :3063-3070) printing image base + rva for addr2line, naming the running exe; appGetSystemDllHandle/ appGetDllExport through LoadLibraryA/GetProcAddress for OpenAL32.dll (:1565-1665); engine packages answer a "linked in" sentinel on _WIN32, as Epic's static builds did (UnVcWin32.cpp returns (void*)1 under __STATIC_LINK), so no --export-all-symbols; POSIX signal handlers and setrlimit skipped.
    • Engine/Src/UnModelLight.cpp: CompressLightmaps (nvDXT) && _MSC_VER: Epic's guards disagreed, only MinGW is neither MSVC nor Linux; Engine/Src/xbstrip.cpp Win32 headers; GUI/Src/GUI.cpp <wtypes.h> only on _WIN32.
    • IpDrv/Inc/UnIpDrv.h:12-34,140: WinSock chosen by platform _WIN32, not by compiler (_MSC_VER); <ws2tcpip.h> for socklen_t; #undef MM_MAX, CreateEvent, CreateSemaphore, CreateMutex (the Win32 headers rename a script enum value and Core's FSynchronizeFactory virtuals); resolver thread by CreateThread; IpDrv/Src/UnSocket.cpp:366 setsockopt optval const char*.
    • UCC/Src/UCC.cpp:9,66,84,110: WIN32 -> _MSC_VER at four places (the MSVC file manager, UnThreadingWindows, GetCommandLineA); :94 FFileManagerLinux also for __MINGW32__; :478 unbuffered stdout on MinGW.
    • Also (other sections' files): UC2ALAudio library names OpenAL32.dll/soft_oal.dll and _putenv for setenv; SDL2InputTest.cpp clearerr + fseek(File,0,SEEK_CUR) at EOF because wine's msvcrt keeps returning EOF on a growing file (all platforms).
    • Core/Inc/FFileManagerUnix.h: mkdir without a mode, O_BINARY, USERPROFILE.
  • tools: m12-wine.sh (one program in its own prefix <WorkUC2>/.cache/wine-m12, WINEDEBUG=-all, winemenubuilder/mscoree/mshtml/winedbg disabled, the owner's display variables dropped, wineserver -k afterwards, Ctrl+C forwarded; UC2_WINE_STOP_AFTER), m12-wine-smoke.sh (checks verify, server, netjoin, null, gl, pinned, audiocheck, selftest, capture, nosound, keyboard, pad, leftovers), package-windows.sh (copies the tree, fails unless every imported DLL is a Windows system DLL or OpenAL32.dll), dwarf-layouts.py, m12/deadbolt-pinned.scen.

How the parts fit - MinGW is "Linux without the Unix identity": UnGnuG.h has a MinGW block that sets __INTEL__/ASM options but not __UNIX__/__LINUX__, so POSIX paths in the file manager and sockets are chosen by _WIN32 where needed; UnUnix.cpp is shared. SDL2 is static; OpenAL is the only runtime DLL, loaded with LoadLibrary (absent: UC2ALAudio: no OpenAL library ... sound is off, the game still boots).

Why - PLAN.md M12: MinGW i686 + x86_64 presets, "wine smoke reaches menu". Epic's MSVC layer cannot be built here; engine-ut2004 already runs on MinGW through the GCC layer. OpenAL Soft is built from the fetched source (the brief forbids downloading binaries).

Decisions and alternatives (windows.md decisions 1-5)

  • The GCC/Unix Core layer with _WIN32 branches rather than Epic's MSVC layer; UnThreadingPosix.cpp on winpthreads rather than UnThreadingWindows.cpp.
  • MinGW's default -mms-bitfields kept. verifyclasses passes with it and with -mno-ms-bitfields; a whole-engine DWARF comparison (11,781 facts each) differs only in UChannel, UControlChannel, UActorChannel, UFileChannel (+4 bytes with MS rules) and FDynamicActor, all C++-only; MS rules are what MSVC 7.1 gave Epic's Windows binary and what the Windows headers are written for.
  • -mno-align-double and -fpack-struct=4 not used (would put every Windows/SDL header struct with 8-byte members out of the platform ABI). MinGW aligns double/QWORD members to 8 where i386 Linux aligns to 4: 39 engine types differ in size between the two builds, all C++-only (UState/UClass +8/+4, ULevel, UViewport, net channels, render caches, UC2ALAudio's state, platform structs); none is mirrored by script or written as a block.
  • appBaseDir stays the working directory (as on Linux): the game must start with System\ as the working directory (Explorer double-click does that; a shortcut needs "Start in"). Clipboard: a process-local string, as on Linux (the Windows clipboard is not used).
  • Runtime DLLs: libgcc, libstdc++, winpthread static; SDL2 static; OpenAL Soft a DLL beside the exe. Imports: ucc.exe ADVAPI32, KERNEL32, msvcrt, WS2_32; uc2.exe also GDI32, IMM32, ole32, OLEAUT32, SETUPAPI, SHELL32, USER32, VERSION, WINMM; OpenAL32.dll AVRT, KERNEL32, msvcrt, ole32, SHELL32, USER32, WINMM.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations

  • Not tested on Windows. wine 10 (a WoW64 build; WINEARCH=win32 is refused) is not Windows. The GL path ran on Mesa llvmpipe through wine's WGL (reported as GL 4.3 compatibility); no NVIDIA/AMD/Intel Windows driver, no WASAPI (wine runs used the null and wave-file backends), no XInput hardware (SDL's virtual controller), no high-DPI, no non-ASCII path (TCHAR is ANSI: depends on the code page), no antivirus/SmartScreen check of an unsigned 81 MB exe. The notebook ends with a 10-item real-Windows checklist (windows.md) that nobody has run.
  • A Linux client joining a Windows server: the handshake works (netjoin check); the full join was blocked by the network defect fixed afterwards on another branch; the combined run on the fixed code is not recorded (BLOCKERS.md: "Not done: real Windows / wine server against the fixed client").
  • Linux build and wine build get different GL versions (4.5 vs 4.3 compatibility under wine): irrelevant to the driver.
  • Windows uses an unsigned uc2.exe with debug info (81 MB; UC2_PACKAGE_STRIP=1 strips copies).
  • Editor (ucc-bin make) is not built for MinGW.

Status - measured under wine (LOG.md 2026-10-05): ucc.exe verifyclasses all match; server up and clean Ctrl+C; client with null renderer then GL: 0 GL errors in 1,767 frames; keyboard 16 checks and pad 33 checks PASS; audio self-test 39 checks, 0 failed (identical to Linux to the hundredth); audiocheck 78 cue tables, 2,438 cues, 0 failures; capture NuBattle at -19.01 dB; pinned DM-Deadbolt view vs Linux: static part 100.000% within 2 levels (with -nopostprocess and the reticle masked), same position and rotation; nosound PASS; smoke 13/13 on the combined main after M9. Seen only: two engine screenshots of the main menu (the notebook says they were looked at).

Off switch - none; the Windows build is a preset.

#12. 64-bit: LP64 x86_64, windows64, arm64 preset (M13)

What the player/owner sees - native64 (Linux x86_64) plays like the 32-bit build; windows64 builds uc2.exe/ucc.exe for x86_64; the arm64 preset exists but has never been configured. Saved profiles written by a 32-bit and a 64-bit build are byte-identical and load in each other.

Where (commits 56298d5 Core: 64-bit bytecode object slots hold object indices; registration stashes in pointer-sized fields, f60b44e Core, Engine, GUI, UCGameplay, UWeb, IpDrv, UCC: 64-bit layout contract and the verifyclasses 64-bit mode (M13), 3afff92 Core: a None context clears its whole 64-bit destination; switch compares a pointer's width, 555078d Core: the GC copies a whole object pointer, 2a0d111 Engine, build, tools: two punned pointer ints; arm64 and native64-uchar presets, 4399e22 Engine: a save game's object reference is 4 bytes on every width, 0c9d5d2 Core: the POSIX file manager reports directories as existing, 58cf932 build, UCC: -Werror=int-to-pointer-cast at 64-bit, sanitizer preset, ucc profiletest, c67b332, a6c8edc, merge 064dd20; notebook analysis/64bit.md)

  • Epic's code changed (the notebook's site table, re-checked against the snapshot):
    • Bytecode object slots (SERIAL_POINTER_INDEX). Core/Inc/Core.h:101 PLATFORM_64BITS (x86_64, _WIN64, aarch64, any __LP64__); Core/Inc/UnObjBas.h:15 turns SERIAL_POINTER_INDEX on for every 64-bit target (Epic had it for __x86_64__ only, with Ryan Gordon's 64K-entry side table GSerializedPointers searched linearly at every load and GC pass; removed). A bytecode slot stays 4 bytes (every jump and skip offset of the shipped 32-bit .u and every script patch counts those slots) and holds Obj->Index, INDEX_NONE for None: UObject::SerialPointerIndex/SerialIndexPointer (:847-849, O(1)). Readers: FFrame::ReadObject (Core/Inc/UnScript.h), execBoolVariable (UnCorSc.cpp), XFERPTR (UnClass.cpp).
    • Layout. Object properties align to 4, not sizeof(UObject*), at link (Core/Src/UnProp.cpp, same value on every 32-bit target); the C++ mirrors are #pragma pack(4): whole-file in UnMaterial.h, UnForceFeedbackWaveform.h, UnCanvas.h, UnPlayer.h, UnTerrain.h, UnFluidSurface.h, UnCameraEffects.h, UnGame.h, UnEngine.h, UnURL.h, UnCorObj.h, UCCameraEffects.h, UWeb.h, and around FLiveUser (UnObjBas.h), FOrientation (Engine.h), UUIXInteraction (UnUIXInteraction.h). None has an 8-byte scalar member, so no 32-bit target's layout changes. PTRINT replaces INT in generated headers where native code casts to and from a pointer (EngineClasses.h 13 lines: OctreeNodes, FStateInfo, LogAr, five MovieMaterial fields, Particles, ScriptedTexture.RenderTarget, ShadowBitmapMaterial.TextureInterfaces[3]; GUIClasses.h Designer; IpDrvClasses.h PrivateResolveInfo, LinkPtr, MSLinkPtr).
    • Native placeholders (Core/Src/UnClass.cpp:453 table, 50 entries: 46 members, 4 array strides; lookup FindNativePlaceholder): a script int that stands in for C++ data script never touches (the object header ObjectInternal[7], vtable stand-ins Subsystem.ExecVtbl, Player.vfOut/vfExec, render-cache pointers, MovieMaterial fields, post-process render targets, Xbox Live UI pointers, IpDrv resolver/link pointers, UWeb maps) advances the layout by the C++ size, the property itself stays a 4-byte int. UStruct::Link applies it (:568); the C++ sizeof of every class registered by DECLARE_CLASS is recorded in both UStruct native constructors (:373-411) and compared with the linked script size; a mismatch is appErrorf (fatal), or a VERIFY: [layout] warning while ucc verifyclasses runs (GLayoutWarnOnly, set from argv before appInit, UCC.cpp:526-528).
    • Registration stashes. UnObj.cpp native constructors parked the const TCHAR* name in the 4-byte FName Name and the autoregister chain in _LinkerIndex; at 64-bit the name goes to StateFrame and the chain to _Linker (pointer-sized, NULL again after Register()); ClassConfigName (an FName inside pack(1) UClass) is stashed in a side table (UnClass.cpp:1454), because the 8-byte pointer overwrote half of ClassConstructor (SIGSEGV 0x0000555500005555 on the first object of Engine).
    • Casts and puns. Align(), FMemStack::PushBytes, STRUCT_OFFSET via PTRINT; the outer-hash input (DWORD)(PTRINT); the GC copied 4 bytes of each object pointer in FArchiveTagUsed (UnObj.cpp:5221, now sizeof(UObject*); SIGSEGV at the first garbage collection); UInput's alias struct was described by arithmetic sizeof(FName)+sizeof(FString) and now by sizeof(FAlias)/STRUCT_OFFSET (Engine/Src/UnIn.cpp:63); UnAudio.cpp RIFF bound, UnStats.cpp memset length, UnOctree.cpp, UnParticleEmitter.cpp, AFileLog.cpp, UnScript.cpp, UnMaterial.cpp, IpDrv MasterServerClient/Uplink.cpp: (INT)p becomes (PTRINT)p. Cast census of a full 64-bit build: 29 pointer-to-int + 90 int-to-pointer = 119 diagnostic lines before, 0 + 0 after (tools/dev/m13-cast-census.sh).
    • VM. UnCorSc.cpp (#if PLATFORM_64BITS): X = None.Y zero-fills the destination with the compiler's 32-bit byte count; where X is bigger in memory (a pointer, a struct holding one) the high half of the old pointer survived; execLet now zeroes the whole ElementSize when exactly that short fill landed on it, and execSwitch compares at least sizeof(UObject*) bytes (engine-ut99 253aaf2).
    • Saved data. Engine/Inc/ULoadSaveManager.h: an object reference in a save game was sizeof(UObject*) bytes (4 or 8); now always an INT 0 of 4 bytes on save and 4 bytes read on load, byte-for-byte the old 32-bit format.
    • File manager. FFileManagerUnix::Exists also answers for directories; before, ULoadSaveManager::ExistsGameObject never found a saved profile on Linux/MinGW/web and every load failed with "Unable to load profile" - a width-independent bug found by the cross-width save test.
  • new code of ours: UCC/Src/UCC.cpp:164-285 the 64-bit mode of verifyclasses (VerifyNativeLayouts64): (1) every class with a C++ size: C++ vs script (400 classes); (2) every placeholder table entry exists and is linked at its C++ size; (3) no bytecode reads or writes a placeholder - every UStruct's script is walked with SerializeExpr on a collecting archive (6,578 scripts) and each UProperty it references is tested; DUMP=<file> (any width, opt-in) writes the linked layout of every class, script struct and function for tools/dev/m13-layout-check.py, which compares it with the DWARF of the same binary member by member (5,697 properties, 400 classes, 122 structs, 202 event parameter structs). ucc-bin profiletest.
  • build: CMakeLists.txt:146-151 (-Werror=int-to-pointer-cast at 64-bit), :132-136 sanitizer, :137-143 unsigned-char audit, presets native64, native64-uchar, native64-asan, arm64, windows64; cmake/toolchain-aarch64-linux-gnu.cmake.
  • tools: tools/dev/m13-64bit.sh (checks verify census controls audio movies glselftest effects pinned psh2glsl saves soak leftovers, plus opt-in asan, base32; M13_TREE/M13_BUILD select the trees), m13-layout-check.py, dwarf-layouts.py, m13-cast-census.sh, pinned-compare.py, map-census.sh (CENSUS_NAME, CENSUS_PORT), m12-wine-smoke.sh (M12_TREE, M12_REF, M12_OUT: windows64 under wine).

How the parts fit - the shipped scripts see a 32-bit world: object references in bytecode are 4-byte indices, properties are laid out with 4-byte slots; C++ sees 8-byte pointers. The two meet at three places: the bytecode readers (index <-> pointer), the property linker (placeholders widen the script layout where C++ keeps a pointer in an int), and the guard that refuses to start when a native class's C++ size and script size differ. Without the placeholder table the guard stops the game at the first mismatching class; without the guard a wrong layout is silent corruption. Scripts that read a placeholder int as a value would see only its low 4 bytes - the bytecode scan proves none does.

Why - Owner decision (PLAN.md): arm64 "later / build-only: keep code 64-bit clean, add preset, no device chase before web ships"; 64-bit x86 is the check that code is clean. The playbook is the frontend's UT99/Unreal 1 64-bit work (ufront 2.83/2.84) and engine-ut2004's (Ryan Gordon's) amd64 port; UC2 already had SERIAL_POINTER_INDEX for __x86_64__ in the Epic tree.

Decisions and alternatives

  • Keep the shipped .u unchanged and widen on the C++ side (placeholders, index slots), because rebuilding the scripts (UT2004 recompiled Object.uc with a pointer type) was "not open before M14".
  • Object index instead of a side table: same scheme as ufront 2.83's UT99 decision; O(1), no 64K limit.
  • Fatal at link time on any mismatch, as ufront 2.83's decision 5, except while verifyclasses lists them all.
  • BITFIELD stays unsigned int (4 bytes), never long.
  • arm64 keeps -fsigned-char like every target (arm's char is unsigned); the x86_64 -funsigned-char audit build shows the code does not depend on it (below). Windows 64-bit is LLP64: PTRINT is unsigned long long there (UnGnuG.h:270-276).
  • Editor (script compiler) stays 32-bit-only (feature 14).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations

  • The placeholder table is hand-maintained: a new native pointer kept in a script int needs an entry, and the failure mode is a startup appErrorf naming the class (good) - but only if the class is registered by DECLARE_CLASS.
  • The 64-bit proof is "layout agrees and no bytecode reads a placeholder". What it does not prove is that the C++ code behind each pointer-in-int site works at 64 bits. The site table marks these as exercised by no check: Orientation.MA (matinee/cinematics - the SceneManager is in no check), StateInfo Node/StateNode/Code (PushState/PopState in AI scripts, not asserted), FileLog.LogAr, MovieMaterial in-memory decode thread, ScriptedTexture.RenderTarget and ShadowBitmapMaterial.TextureInterfaces[3] (punned pointers, effects runs "only if in view; not asserted"), GUIController.Designer, UIXInteraction (Xbox Live), IpDrv resolver/master-server links (no DNS or master server in the tests), UWeb maps, wave import's RIFF bound, UnOctree.cpp CompareArray (debug), None-context fill ("any run; not asserted").
  • Engine/Src/palette.cpp (unbuilt, no caller) reads 32-bit pixels as unsigned long*: wrong at LP64, left as Epic wrote it.
  • UT2004's 32-byte padding of every allocation on 64-bit is not adopted (no padding in FMallocAnsi.h); the guard replaces it.
  • -fpermissive keeps pointer-to-int narrowing a warning; only the census script finds it, and only in a full clean build log.
  • arm64 is unverified. The preset and toolchain file exist; no notebook records a configure or a build; BLOCKERS.md says the compiler and qemu-user were installed by the owner on 2026-10-06 and in the same entry lists sudo apt install g++-aarch64-linux-gnu qemu-user ... as still missing, so the entry contradicts itself. By reading (64bit.md, step 6 and 11): every x86 asm/intrinsic site is gated __i386__, __x86_64__ || __i386__, _XBOX, __PSX2_EE__ or __HAS_SSE__, so arm64 takes the C paths that x86_64 and wasm take; bytecode operands are read as *(INT*)Code at unaligned addresses, fine for AArch64 normal loads (not for armv7, which is not a target); no atomics on pack(4) members. The client additionally needs the arm64 X11/GL development packages through multiarch because SDL2 is built from source.
  • The unsigned-char audit found one real difference: Core.h:214 ToAnsi/FromUnicode ((_WORD)In<0x100 ? In : MAXSBYTE) writes a Latin-1 character >= 0x80 as 0x7F with signed char (every build here and Epic's MSVC) and keeps it with unsigned char. Recorded, not changed (Epic's shipped behaviour); it means accented text in saved inis can be mangled on every platform.
  • A 64-bit uc2.bin is 66 MB and ucc-bin 54 MB at first link.

Status - measured (LOG.md, analysis/64bit.md): ucc-bin verifyclasses at 64 bits: 400 classes, 0 mismatches while linking, 50 placeholders found, 6,578 scripts scanned, exit 0; m13-layout-check.py: 2 mismatches (Texture.__LastUpdateTime/MemUsage declared in opposite order in C++ and script), identical at 32 bits, pre-existing in Epic's shipped PC/Xbox builds, script never reads either; 32-bit unaffected: DWARF of main's 32-bit ucc-bin vs the branch's differs only in 3-4 new template instantiations. Census 58/58 at 64-bit and 58/58 at 32-bit, same per-map result. 64-bit GL client, DM-Deadbolt with 4 bots, 70 s: max RSS 227 MB, 0 GL errors in 1,124 frames. Pinned DM-Deadbolt view 64-bit vs 32-bit: static part 99.997-100.000% within 2 levels (coordinator 99.997%). Soak 1,200 s: RSS +1.5% (agent +3.2%), 0 Critical. Saves: profiles byte-identical (845 bytes), cross loads equal. Full run 2: every check PASS; coordinator's re-run on merged main (logs/coord/a1-m13.log): verify, census 58/58, controls 5/5, audio, movies, glselftest 10/10, effects 14/14, pinned, psh2glsl, saves, soak, leftovers: all PASS. windows64: 0 errors; under wine verify and server PASS and a 10-check smoke 10 PASS 0 FAIL (the agent's report; the coordinator logged it "per the agent"). native64-uchar builds (435 commands carry -funsigned-char). Two failures in the first full run were load (pad 30/33 while a build ran; passed 33/33 three times alone) and harness (soak time limit), both fixed in the script.

Off switch - build with the native preset (default); -DUC2_ALLOW_64BIT=OFF.

#13. The ini structure: Default.ini, DefUser.ini, the web inis

What the player/owner sees - the first run creates System/Avaris.ini and System/User.ini from the shipped defaults; settings persist there. The browser build has its own boot inis.

Where

  • Engine/Config/ (commit 7633696 baseline, then edited by many): Default.ini (568 lines, 33 sections), DefUser.ini (355 lines, 10 sections), DefaultXbox.ini and DefUserXbox.ini (Epic's, unchanged, reference), KeyBinds.ini (unchanged), README.md (every change from Epic's file with its reason: 46 table rows), web/Default.ini (587 lines, 35 sections), web/DefUser.ini (355 lines).

  • Derivation: Core/Src/UnMisc.cpp:2118-2146 appInit (Epic's code): GIni = <module>.ini (module Avaris; INI= on the command line overrides), User.ini (USERINI=); each is created by copying Default.ini / DefUser.ini when absent. tools/dev/mkrun-uc2.sh copies Engine/Config/*.ini over the asset tree's into every run tree; a native player's System/ is therefore seeded from the repo's files, and the file in the asset tree (assets/uc2/System, the leak's copy) is overridden.

  • Platform-relevant changes to Epic's Default.ini (rows for the other keys are in the README and in the other sections):

    Section / key Epic Ours Why
    [URL] Port 1000 7777 1000 is the Xbox system-link port, privileged on Linux
    [Engine.Engine] ViewportManager WinDrv.WindowsClient SDL2Drv.SDL2Client WinDrv is Win32-only; Epic's line kept as a comment
    [Engine.Engine] RenderDevice D3DDrv.D3DRenderDevice UC2GLDrv.UC2GLRenderDevice (web: UC2GL4ESDrv.UC2GL4ESRenderDevice) D3DDrv is not built; -nullrenderer selects the null device
    [Engine.Engine] AudioDevice Engine.NullAudioDevice UC2ALAudio.UC2ALAudioSubsystem M8; -nosound selects the null device
    [Engine.Engine] NetworkDevice IpDrv.TcpNetDriver web: WebRTCDrv.WebRTCNetDriver a browser has no sockets
    [Engine.Engine] ScriptSaveConfig (none) True script SaveConfig() writes on PC (saveconfig section)
    [SDL2Drv.SDL2Client] (none) copy of [WinDrv.WindowsClient] plus later keys the client reads its own class section; also the compiled defaults
    [UC2GLDrv.UC2GLRenderDevice], [UC2GL4ESDrv.UC2GL4ESRenderDevice], [UC2ALAudio.UC2ALAudioSubsystem], [SDL2Drv.PadMap], [GUI.EmulatedJoypad], [ufront.AutoFOV], [Engine.Console] ConsoleKey (none / 9) see the README renderer, audio, input sections

  • Web inis: Engine/Config/web/Default.ini and DefUser.ini are GENERATED by tools/dev/sync-web-ini.py (--check fails when they are not what the script would write): the native file with the DELTAS (RenderDevice, [SDL2Drv.SDL2Client] UseJoystick=False, NetworkDevice), plus WEB_KEYS (AudioDevice) and WEB_SECTIONS ([UC2ALAudio.UC2ALAudioSubsystem] with the web-only Backends=, [UC2Movie], [WebRTCDrv.WebRTCNetDriver]) kept as the web file has them. Measured against the native file today the only differences are those: RenderDevice, NetworkDevice, UseJoystick, Backends=, the [UC2Movie] and [WebRTCDrv.WebRTCNetDriver] sections (WebRTCDrv timeouts 60 s and 300 s against 30 s and 20 s because a browser streams a map's files on demand and a guest that loads sends nothing). They are preloaded into the module as /System/Default.ini and /System/DefUser.ini (Web.cmake). A host must not serve the asset tree's copies of those four names.

  • Commits: c66d275 config: web inis generated from the native ones (they had fallen behind M7 and M10), ef34e6c, 941326a; many README commits.

How the parts fit - Default.ini/DefUser.ini are templates, read once when the working ini does not exist. Afterwards Avaris.ini/User.ini own the values; a new key in Default.ini reaches an existing player's ini only through the compiled default of the class that reads it (the README says "also the compiled defaults" for most new keys), through a migration in code (UC2InputMigrate.cpp for old input values; the ConsoleKey Tab -> tilde mapping at UnGame.cpp:284; ReplaceMissingDrivers), or not at all. On the web the host keeps Avaris.ini/User.ini (persisted engine files) and flushconfig.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 10, feature 13.

Why - Default.ini is Epic's PC ini; the retail disc's DefaultXbox.ini is a slightly later variant (net rates 7000 -> 10000/20000, four log suppressions on). The owner's rule (2026-10-06): "canonical config and localisation = the leak (unless a difference changes behaviour)".

Decisions and alternatives

  • Generate the web inis instead of hand-maintaining a second copy: at M11 the web copies were still the M4w ones, without M7's MaxDistortion and without M10's key table, pad map and bindings (the menus would not have answered the keyboard).
  • Keep Epic's other-platform sections ([WinDrv.WindowsClient], [D3DDrv...], [XboxDrv...]) in the file as dead text: the file stays diffable against Epic's.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations

  • Line endings drifted. Epic's Default.ini was CRLF (367 CR lines at the baseline; client-boot.md says "The file keeps its CRLF line endings (it is a byte copy of Epic's)"). Since the M7 merge (225b0d5, hand-resolved conflict) Default.ini and web/Default.ini are LF, while DefUser.ini and web/DefUser.ini are CRLF. git diff against Epic's Default.ini is therefore 935 changed lines in the diff stat for about 213 real ones. The engine parses both; sync-web-ini.py keeps whichever the native file uses. The code wins over the notebook. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 10, feature 13.)
  • An existing Avaris.ini is never refreshed; stale values (an Engine.NullRenderDevice from an early run, ConsoleKey=9) stay unless a migration exists. A player who ran an older build keeps old defaults; the web persists the ini for ever (Core/Src/UnObj.cpp comments say a stale baked default shadows the next build's).
  • Default.ini suppresses the DevUI/DevGUI log names (Epic's setting), so menu activity is not in the log by default.
  • The README's table has grown to be the only index of config deviations; a change made without a row is invisible.

Status - I did not run sync-web-ini.py --check (no tests); a manual diff of the native and web files in the snapshot shows only the differences listed above, so they are in step at 4813dd9. Not verified: that a long-lived Avaris.ini from the first M4 build still boots (the driver replacement logic is tested with Epic's original Default.ini as Avaris.ini: WinDrv.WindowsClient is not in this build: using SDL2Drv.SDL2Client, D3DDrv.D3DRenderDevice ...: using Engine.NullRenderDevice).

Off switch - INI=<name>/USERINI=<name> on the command line pick other files; -ini: overrides single keys (feature 9).

#14. The Editor package as a porting target (M14, platform side)

What the player/owner sees - nothing in the game. ucc-bin make rebuilds all 17 script packages from Source/<Pkg>/Classes on 32-bit Linux (the scripts section covers what that gives); this feature is what made Epic's Editor package compile, link statically and run.

Where (commits 93686c0 Editor: builds natively and ucc-bin make compiles all 17 script packages (M14 S1), 586600b Core, Editor, tools: script comparison tool, stripsource stage, backslash package names (M14 S2), c3c7397 ...script reproduction matches the shipped packages, 434ac53 Editor, UCC: Editor layouts match (GCC bitfield tagging), verifyclasses checks them; Editor 32-bit Linux only, e2b3a8c Core: a conformed package save sorts only the new imports)

  • Build: Source/Editor/CMakeLists.txt (every Src/*.cpp; UC2_STATIC_CLASS_ACCESSORS=1); CMakeLists.txt:433-435 removes it for 64-bit and MinGW; Source/UCC/CMakeLists.txt:34-38 links it and defines UC2_WITH_EDITOR.
  • new code of ours: Editor/Src/UC2EditorRegistrants.cpp (255 lines: static-link registration of the Editor package's classes through per-class C accessors, because most commandlets are declared inside their .cpp and AUTO_INITIALIZE_REGISTRANTS_EDITOR cannot name them), Editor/Src/UC2EpicClassOrder.h (495 lines, generated by tools/dev/epic-class-order.py: the order in which Epic's DLL build registered native classes, which is the order ucc make walked sibling classes in), Core/Inc/UnObjBas.h:475-485 UC2_STATIC_CLASS_ACCESSOR.
  • Epic's code changed (porting fixes): Editor/Inc/Editor.h (GCC_PACK(4) on AutosaveTimeMinutes: after a bitfield run MSVC and script start a new DWORD; a forward declaration of FObjectExport), EditorClasses.h (three GCC_PACK(4) tags), Perlin.h (register INT), UnVisi.cpp (pointer-to-member syntax), UnParams.cpp (five parsers duplicated in Engine/Src/UnParams.cpp collide when both are linked into one program: Editor's copies #if !__STATIC_LINK), TTFontImport.cpp (GDI rasterising only under _MSC_VER; elsewhere the factory exists but importing a font reports an error), UAnalyzeMeshesCommandlet.cpp (glibc's MAXFLOAT macro), UnEdSrv.cpp (#exec lines name files with \; PATH_SEPARATOR is /), UnEdExp.cpp (the header exporter: tag only the first bool of a run, and tag a class's first member, the same two rules as in feature 6, so a regeneration agrees with the hand-fixed headers).
  • Also for reproducing Epic's compile (shared with the scripts section): Editor/Src/UMakeCommandlet.cpp (.uc files sorted as NTFS listed them; -CONFORMNOCONFIG), UStripSourceCommandlet.cpp (-CONFORM), UnScrCom.cpp (class walk in Epic's registration order, -NOEPICORDER keeps object-table order); in Core UnObj.cpp:3629 (\ in #exec OBJ LOAD FILE=..\Textures\T_HUD.utx - without it the package was named "" and every texture of T_HUD.utx was saved as an import from a transient package), UnClass.cpp (StructFlags and ScriptTextCRC were uninitialised for native classes constructed on the heap, which a DLL build's zero-initialised static storage hid: ucc make wrote garbage into dependency records), UnAnsi.cpp (appAtoi wraps like MSVC's: INPUT_All = 4294967295 compiled to -1 in the shipped scripts and 2147483647 with glibc's saturating atoi), UnObj.cpp:4490 (D-9: the import sort started from 0 instead of the first new import and reordered existing imports; names are sorted from SortStartPosition at :4411).
  • tools: script-build.sh, script-diff.py, upkg151.py, epic-class-order.py, m14-scripts-check.sh (scripts section).

How the parts fit - Editor is linked into ucc-bin only (not uc2.bin); the launcher registers it through UC2InitializeEditorRegistrants (UCC.cpp:52,563), and verifyclasses checks its layouts too (UC2VerifyEditorClasses).

Why - the owner's decision (2026-10-04): boot with the shipped .u, later recompile from source to fix keyboard/mouse and drop the patches.

Decisions and alternatives - build Editor but not UnrealEd/Window (no GUI editor); keep it 32-bit Linux only: its hand-written UEditorEngine mirrors script's int Pad[8] with four pointers; the MinGW build "has not been tried".

Disadvantages, risks, deviations - the web build and the asset tree keep the shipped .u except GUI.u, UCGameplay.u and UCMenu.u, which are rebuilt (asset tree v2, tools/dev/mkassets-uc2.sh, System/SCRIPTS.txt says which); UCGameplay.u is a server package, so every peer must run it. Two shipped-vs-source behaviour differences are listed in BLOCKERS.md (CTFFlag.home patch defective in the shipped scripts; bUnlockCharacters/bShowAllMuts config in the shipped GUI.u only). A static-link consequence nobody has fixed: UHTTPDownload and UPrefab say DECLARE_CLASS(...,Engine) but live in IpDrv/Editor, which as DLLs registered them under their own package; our static build registers them as Engine.HTTPDownload/Engine.Prefab, so Default.ini's DownloadManagers=IpDrv.HTTPDownload names a class that does not exist and package download has never been exercised.

Status - measured (LOG.md 2026-10-07): ucc-bin make builds all 17 packages (10 s per full build, well under 3 GB); script-diff.py: 0 unexplained differences, 9,621 of 10,456 functions byte-identical, 813 differ only in line positions in 17 classes changed after retail; 29 of Epic's 30 patches already in the source.

Off switch - not building Editor (remove it from UC2_PACKAGES); -NOEPICORDER, -CONFORMNOCONFIG.

#Inventory: tools/dev/*, tools/*, bin/*

All repo scripts are bash or python3 (Pillow only, no numpy on this machine), print one PASS/FAIL line per check, and run the game, wine, node or ffmpeg inside bin/capped. "Section" says which other section of the report explains what the script checks.

#tools/dev/ (run-tree and build helpers, platform checks: mine)

Script What it runs or measures Milestone
import-epic-sources.py one-shot import of the pristine tree into this repo's layout (refuses to overwrite) M0
mkassets-uc2.sh builds the canonical asset tree <WorkUC2>/assets/uc2 from the pristine tree: v0 = Epic's PC-format content (Maps 58 + 9 ComboMatinees, Textures, StaticMeshes, Animations, System .u/.ini/Localization, Ladders), v1 (2026-10-06) hard-links the cooked Sounds/Music/Movies/Textures/Movies, v2 (2026-10-07) overlays rebuilt GUI.u, UCGameplay.u, UCMenu.u (shipped copies kept in assets/uc2-shipped/System, System/SCRIPTS.txt lists them) and writes MANIFEST.sha256; real copies, never symlinks, never deletes M0, M8, M9, M14
mkrun-uc2.sh <build-dir> [tree] [more-build-dirs] assembles <WorkUC2>/run/<tree>: hard links of the asset tree's content folders, a real copy of System/ (the engine rewrites inis and logs there), Engine/Config/*.ini copied over, optional UC2_SCRIPTS=<dir> rebuilt packages, the build's ucc-bin, uc2*, libopenal.so.1, ucc.exe, uc2.exe, OpenAL32.dll, ucc-bin.js/.wasm; it only ever removes the tree it created M3 on
map-census.sh <build> [secs] [workers] loads every Maps/*.unr on a headless Engine.ServerCommandlet (own run tree and port per worker); PASS = "up for play", no Critical:, still ticking at the timeout; logs/census/summary.tsv; kills servers that hang on exit M3

#bin/ (in /home/ion/WorkUC2/bin, outside the repo and not under version control; read only here)

Script What it does
capped <mem> <secs> -- cmd... (44 lines) runs the command and its whole process tree in one systemd-run --user --scope with MemoryMax, MemorySwapMax=0, TasksMax=2000, timeout -s TERM -k 5, core dumps off, ALSOFT_DRIVERS=null unless the caller set it; afterwards asks systemd why it ended (exit 137 = memory ceiling, 124 = time limit), kills and stops the scope so nothing survives
mem-watchdog [warn=16] [kill=24] [hours=12] (30 lines) every 10 s logs machine memory in use to logs/mem-watchdog.log; above 16 GB a WARN line; above 24 GB kills every uc2-capped-* scope (and nothing else). The owner's memory policy of 2026-10-04
publish-preview <engine artifact dir> (25 lines) snapshots SDLLaunch.{js,wasm,data} + meta.json, refuses if the wasm hash differs from meta.json (build still linking), runs the frontend yarn build --target uc2 --engine-artifact ... --out serve/uc2 inside capped 4G 300, appends to serve/PUBLISHED.txt; the server unit uc2-preview (port 8096) serves the directory
play-uc2 [WxH] [uc2.bin args] (25 lines) for the owner: rebuilds the run tree run/play from engine-uc2/build-native every time (inis and Save/ of the previous tree kept in run/play-saved), edits the window size into the tree's Default.ini, runs uc2.bin ... -log on the owner's display and speakers
oracle-shot.sh <name> [map] [secs] (282 lines) runs Epic's original avaris.exe (Direct3D 8) under wine with software GL under Xvfb, optionally injects keys and console commands, grabs a screenshot to logs/oracle/: the PC-look reference for the renderer. Writes only under run/oracle, .cache/wine-oracle, logs/oracle

yarn, yarnpkg symlinks to corepack's yarn for the frontend build

#Open issues in this area

  1. Real Windows has never run it. Everything in feature 11 is wine 10 on Mesa llvmpipe, null/wave-file audio and a virtual pad. The 10-item checklist in docs/porting/analysis/windows.md (double-click start, real GPU driver, WASAPI, XInput pad, mouse/fullscreen/DPI, paths with spaces and non-ASCII, SmartScreen, cmd server with the firewall prompt, a Linux client joining a Windows server, clean quit) is open. A Windows server against the fixed network client is not recorded.
  2. arm64 has never been configured or built. Contradictory BLOCKERS entry (installed / still missing). The client needs arm64 X11/GL development packages through multiarch.
  3. Exit-time crash/hang of a dedicated server after SIGTERM (BLOCKERS.md "Open (M13)": StaticExit -> PurgeGarbage -> ULinkerLoad::DetachExport, about 2 maps in 58 per census, then a hang in FFileStream::Destroy waiting for a thread that is not there). Related use-after-frees were fixed on the network branch; whether this one is gone is not recorded. map-census.sh still reports PASS for it (its timeout has no -k; the 64-bit script added a hang watcher).
  4. Fetch refs are not pinned (SDL2 release-2.32.x, OpenAL Soft ufront/web, GL4ES fix/cubemap-bind-active-unit), the first configure needs network, and the web meta.json does not record the fetched libraries; its buildFlags and header comment are stale.
  5. Documentation drift. README.md ("Status: M0") and the top of BUILD.md ("Status: M0") are stale; BUILD.md has no web section; UC2_ASSETS_DIR is documented and unused; client-boot.md says Default.ini keeps CRLF (it has been LF since 225b0d5); the wasm preset description says DWARF.
  6. Null-this tests in UObject::IsValid/GetFullName (UnObj.cpp:235) can be deleted by GCC/clang at -O2; -fno-delete-null-pointer-checks is not set. No failure seen.
  7. Suspected real bugs left as Epic wrote them (feature 4 list), and UnParticleEmitter.cpp MIN_ATTEN_DIFF = 0.
  8. The FColor -> FPlane conversion chosen at three Engine sites and one UCElements site is an assumption about MSVC 7.1; if lighting alpha looks wrong, look there first.
  9. Placeholder sites exercised by no check (feature 12 list) and the hand-maintained 50-entry table.
  10. Asyncify + JavaScript exceptions performance in a browser is unmeasured; the 2 GB wasm maximum has no recorded headroom measurement; pool size 4 is tied to OpenAL Soft's threads.
  11. ucc help blank descriptions for native commandlets (cosmetic; possible effect on class'X'.default.<localized> in menus not followed up).
  12. Static-link registration: UHTTPDownload/UPrefab are registered as Engine.HTTPDownload/Engine.Prefab; DownloadManagers=IpDrv.HTTPDownload names a class that does not exist; package download has never been exercised.
  13. ToAnsi/FromUnicode write 0x7F for Latin-1 characters >= 0x80 (signed char), so accented text in saved inis or log files may be mangled on every platform (Epic's behaviour; unchanged).
  14. Editor (ucc-bin make) exists on 32-bit Linux only. Everything else uses the shipped .u plus three rebuilt packages.
  15. Owner questions still open here: split-screen on PC (the SDL2 client creates one window per viewport as WinDrv did, nothing else was done), which arm64 hardware (M13), whether to pin the fetch refs, and the flat-file versus directory-wildcard choice for web profiles (coordinator decided flat; 22e9dbf can be reverted if the frontend learns directory wildcards).
  16. The PipeWire package (libpipewire-0.3-modules:i386) for the native 32-bit game's PipeWire backend (BLOCKERS.md).

#Places where we changed Epic's shared code

All 136 files that are modified relative to 7633696 (git diff --name-status 7633696 4813dd9 -- Source, status M), paths relative to Source/. Class: P = a porting fix only (compile, portability, 64-bit, a bug found while porting) - explained in this section; P+ = a porting fix and a change that belongs to another section (named); O = another section's change only (named; not explained here). Counts: P 95, P+ 18, O 23.

#20. The renderer (OpenGL, GLES, WebGL)

Scope: everything that makes UC2 draw on OpenGL / OpenGL ES / WebGL: the new driver Source/UC2GLDrv, its GL4ES binding Source/UC2GL4ESDrv, the Xbox shader translation, render targets and post-processing, projectors, shadows and three-layer terrain, dynamic gamma, the GL side of movie textures, anti-aliasing and anisotropy, the web renderer, and the render-related ini keys. Baseline 7633696, code read at main 4813dd9 (snapshot engine-uc2-report). Line numbers are the snapshot's. Features are numbered R-1..R-22; owner items (O-n) and milestones (Mn) are named where they apply.

#The things to know first

  1. One new driver, built from three sources. Source/UC2GLDrv has 127 files: 25 C++ sources, headers and CMake files (about 19,700 lines) plus 102 generated shader files. It is a three-way merge: engine-ut2004's OpenGLDrv gave the GL structure, UC2's D3DDrv (Direct3D 8, in the tree, never built) is the specification. The material compiler is UC2's D3DMaterialState.cpp carried over function for function, stages kept in Direct3D vocabulary and translated to GL in one place: after renaming the types it still differs from Epic's file by only 56 removed / 168 added lines, so it can be diffed against Epic.
  2. The Xbox look, not the PC look, is the target. Epic's PC build drew every Xbox-only material with its first texture, made distortion opaque (the flat periwinkle DM-Praxis panes) and had no post-processing at all. We translated all 100 Xbox pixel shaders (.psh) to GLSL with a tool that is checked against Microsoft's assembler output and on the GPU, ported the Xbox vertex-shader fragments the PC data can use, and lifted Epic's _XBOX gates through named render capabilities (FRenderCaps::XboxPostProcess, XboxProjectors, XboxShadows, MaxSimultaneousTerrainLayers, PostProcessReferenceHeight). _XBOX is never defined; both branches of every lifted gate stay in the source.
  3. There is no Xbox reference picture for most of it. The evidence is numeric (shader translator against the assembler's definition words, GLSL against a reference interpreter, a driver self-test of every post-process primitive), plus pictures compared with Epic's PC binary under wine where PC drew the same thing, plus the owner's xemu screenshots for a few owner reports. "Looks like the Xbox" is unverified for bloom/glow strength, dynamic gamma, distortion size, the shadows and the projector look.
  4. One driver for desktop, GLES and WebGL. The web build is the same driver behind GL4ES (desktop GL calls translated to OpenGL ES 2 = WebGL 1; no WebGL 2), chosen at run time by a subclass (UC2GL4ESDrv). Linux builds it too as a "native twin" so every renderer test runs on the web code path without a browser. Fallbacks for weak WebGL devices exist except one: without depth textures, depth of field and the distortion occlusion are lost.
  5. Owner-driven tuning on top of Epic's values, each one setting away from the Xbox value: depth-of-field alpha = the shipped 65 (O-3), distortion fade distance 15000 instead of the Xbox 5000 (O-7), post-process blurs sized for a 480-line picture at any resolution (O-10), fixed-function stages as generated GLSL so NVIDIA draws what Mesa draws (O-9), AA 4x and anisotropy 16x by default (O-8). Default.ini and the web ini carry the same render keys.

Costs in one line: post-processing is expensive (3.3x-3.8x frame time on software GL; on the owner's Quadro P620 single views measured 9-12 ms a frame from 720p to 4K with everything on, never broken down per effect); twelve of Epic's shared files are changed (listed at the end); the driver carries about 2,500 lines of test and debug code; 12 of the 25 shaders M6 left for post-processing were never seen drawn (5 of them unreachable or unused by design).

#Features

#R-1 The OpenGL render device UC2GLDrv (M5, fixed-function renderer)

What the player/owner sees. The game draws with OpenGL instead of Direct3D 8: menus, maps, lightmaps, meshes, fog, projectors, the HUD. It is the default device on every platform (RenderDevice=UC2GLDrv.UC2GLRenderDevice).

Where.

  • New code, Source/UC2GLDrv (127 files; 19,660 lines in Src/ + Inc/, plus 102 generated files in Shaders/). Grouped by job:

    Group Files What it does
    Package, build CMakeLists.txt, Src/UC2GLDrv.cpp, Inc/UC2GLDrv.h static library GPackage=GUC2GLDrvPackage, links Engine + SDL2, no libGL (SDL loads it); Shaders/ on the include path
    GL binding and entry points Inc/UC2GLBinding.h, Inc/UC2GLFuncs.h (X-macro list of the GL entry points + extension flags), Inc/UC2GLRawFuncs.h (M11: GLES2 calls that must bypass GL4ES) every GL call goes through the device's pointer table; prototypes are deliberately not declared, so a stray direct call fails to link
    Direct3D vocabulary Inc/UC2GLD3DTypes.h (D3D8 fixed-function enums with d3d8types.h values, D3DMATRIX), Src/UC2GLD3DXCompat.h (D3DX matrix helpers on FMatrix; M7b added the projector matrices) lets the material compiler keep Epic's text
    Device Inc/UC2GLRenderDevice.h, Src/UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp (2,438) UUC2GLRenderDevice: proc table, Init (:532), SetRes (:693), main framebuffer (CreateMainFramebuffer :828), present pass and gamma (UpdateGamma :1857, Present :2226), console commands (Exec :1878), ReadPixels (:2316, top row first, BGRA as D3D), GetRenderCaps (:2368), all test switches
    Render interface Inc/UC2GLRenderInterface.h, Src/UC2GLRenderInterface.cpp (2,318) UC2's FRenderInterface: saved-state / render-target / viewport / stencil stacks, lights (SetLight :590, UC2's directional-only selection), fog, transforms and the D3D-to-GL clip fix-up (GetGLMatrices :1763), the one place where D3D stage state becomes GL (SetMaterialBlending :1354, TranslateStageOp, CommitTexCoords :1958), streams, DrawPrimitive (:2066), occlusion queries (:2287-2310)
    Material compiler Src/UC2GLMaterialState.cpp (4,898), Src/UC2GLPSMaterialState.cpp (1,002) UC2's D3DMaterialState.cpp (4,786) and D3DPSMaterialState.cpp (986) carried over; after renaming FD3D/UD3D the differences are 56/168 and 20/36 lines (removed/added, measured with diff -w for this report); every deliberate change is marked UC2GLDrv:
    Resources Inc/UC2GLResource.h, Src/UC2GLResource.cpp (1,253) UC2's 16-bit cache ids per ECacheHint, textures (RGBA8, P8, G16, DXT1/3/5, L8, A8, R5G6B5, cube maps, composite lightmaps), render targets (texture + FBO + own or shared depth), VBO vertex/index buffers, D3DCOLOR-to-RGBA swizzle on upload
    Programmable path (R-3/R-4) Src/UC2GLShaders.cpp (1,327), Shaders/* see R-3, R-4
    Post-processing (R-6..R-13) Src/UC2GLPostProcess.cpp (1,429) see R-6
    Fixed function as GLSL (R-5) Src/UC2GLFFProgram.cpp (360) see R-5
    Movies (R-17) Src/UC2GLMovie.cpp (116) see R-17
    Tests and diagnostics (R-21) Src/UC2GLSelfTest.cpp (1,195), UC2GLCombinerTest.cpp (652), UC2GLPixelTrace.cpp (289), UC2GLSceneCensus.cpp (220), UC2GLShadowCheck.cpp (147) numeric self-tests and console probes compiled into the shipping driver

  • Outside the driver (our own port modules, not Epic's): Source/SDL2Drv/Src/SDL2Viewport.cpp:681 EnsureGLContext (re-creates the plain window as SDL_WINDOW_OPENGL with the same title/position/size and creates the context when the device asks for BLIT_OpenGL; DestroyGLContext :722; member GLContext in Inc/SDL2Drv.h:166); Source/SDLLaunch/Src/SDLLaunch.cpp:305 switch -opengl (and -gl4es :306), registration of the package (:39-42, :518); Source/SDLLaunch/CMakeLists.txt:21-28; root CMakeLists.txt:412-426 (UC2GLDrv in UC2_PACKAGES, built on every target since M11).

  • Epic's code: none changed at M5 (later features change some; see the list at the end). Epic's D3DDrv stays in the tree, unbuilt.

  • ini: Engine/Config/Default.ini:19 [Engine.Engine] RenderDevice=UC2GLDrv.UC2GLRenderDevice (was D3DDrv.D3DRenderDevice; the null device is a commented line); new section [UC2GLDrv.UC2GLRenderDevice] (Default.ini:377-406, keys in R-22).

  • Commits: 536073e (decision: one GL driver source), f107ffb (driver R1), 1700520 (SDL2Drv GL context), 34dd131 (launcher, default device), b0aac24 (gate evidence); merged as 18224d9.

How the parts fit. The engine talks to the device through UC2's FRenderInterface exactly as it talked to D3DDrv. The material compiler (Epic's text) fills a list of passes in Direct3D terms (stage colour/alpha ops and arguments, blend, address, filter, texcoord index and texture-transform flags); SetMaterialBlending and CommitTexCoords translate that to GL texture environment, blend and texture state at draw time. Since O-9 (R-5) the same D3D stage list is normally turned into a generated GLSL fragment program instead. GIsOpenGL stays 0 (UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:540): the engine keeps D3D colour and pixel conventions and the driver converts (the sibling UT2004 driver set it to 1). SDL2Drv only creates the window and context; the device draws into an offscreen main framebuffer and presents it (R-2).

Why. The owner's decision of 2026-10-04 (PLAN.md "Owner decisions"): "New GL driver by merge (UT2004 OpenGLDrv/GL4ESDrv + UC2 D3DDrv as spec)". The exploration found UC2's D3DDrv a strict superset of UT2004's (design.md F2: RenderInterface 36/36, MaterialState 26/26 functions, plus about 44 UC2-only interface functions and about 1,400 added material-compiler lines), and UT2004's OpenGLDrv a mirror of UT2004's D3DDrv. ANGLE, DXGL and WineD3D were rejected at planning (PLAN.md "What the exploration settled").

Decisions and alternatives.

  • One driver source for desktop, GLES and WebGL (LOG.md 2026-10-04) instead of engine-ut2004's two mirrored directories (OpenGLDrv + GL4ESDrv). The binding is chosen at run time by class (R-19).
  • Keep the material compiler in D3D vocabulary and translate in one place, instead of hand-translating the ~1,400 UC2-only lines into inline GL calls as the sibling driver does. Reason: the file stays diffable against Epic's, and the same stage description is what the GLSL generator (R-5) and GL4ES consume.
  • Dropped from the sibling: NV_VAR / ATI_VAO / APPLE_VAO / ATI element arrays, ARB_fp terrain shaders, DE's dynamic-quad helpers, WGL (gl-renderer.md "Merge table").
  • Push buffers, DrawPrimitiveUP, fences, real occlusion queries are _XBOX-only at every call site: left at PC behaviour (design.md F3).
  • Never define XBOX_ASSET_FORMAT (it changes serialized layouts): skeletal meshes stay CPU-skinned as on PC.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • A large body of code to maintain (about 20k lines + generated shaders), only partly shared with the UT2004 sibling: fixes there do not flow here automatically and vice versa.
  • Occlusion queries always answer "visible" (GetOcclusionQueryResults writes 0x7fffffff, UC2GLRenderInterface.cpp:2305): no occlusion culling benefit. Epic's PC stub never wrote the count at all (undefined behaviour); ours is defined but does no culling. Since M11 the engine's query boxes are not drawn while a query is open (:2072-2078).
  • Deliberate differences from Epic's D3DDrv found while porting: UC2's TResourceCache::Flush could unlink the wrong chain entry (ours unlinks exactly the id); Epic's ambient-light colours are added as packed DWORDs with wrap-around (reproduced on purpose). The sibling's glPolygonMode constant condition, switch fall-throughs and texcoord-by-order binding were not carried over (gl-renderer.md "Defects found").
  • The texture-matrix padding rules and the clip fix-up (z' = 2z-w, half-pixel shift, flip for render-target textures) were reasoned from D3D's rules, then confirmed indirectly by the M7 numeric self-test (R-6).

Status. Measured at the M5 gate (LOG.md 2026-10-04, Xvfb + Mesa llvmpipe): menu 1,200 frames and DM-Deadbolt 2,400 frames with 0 GL errors and 0 Critical; census 58/58 maps; verifyclasses, null renderer still boot. Seen: pictures in <WorkUC2>/logs/m5/ (DM-Praxis from the same spawn as Epic's PC binary under wine agrees in geometry, textures, lighting, HUD). On the owner's NVIDIA Quadro P620: run since O-5/O-9 (headless EGL). Real Windows: never (wine only, M12).

Off switch. -nullrenderer or RenderDevice=Engine.NullRenderDevice. Note: a device chosen on the command line persists into the user's ini (gl-renderer.md "Untested / unsure"), so a later run without the switch keeps it.

#R-2 Window, main framebuffer, present pass and static gamma (M5)

What the player/owner sees. The picture appears in an SDL window; Brightness/Contrast/Gamma from the client settings are applied to it, also in a window (PC applied the ramp only in full screen).

Where.

  • Src/UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp: CreateMainFramebuffer (:828): an RGBA8 texture + depth-stencil (a texture since M7, so it can be sampled; multisampled renderbuffers since O-8, R-18); the present pass, shader GPresentFragmentShader (:1486; GLSL 1.20 written so it is also ESSL 1.00), D3D's gamma ramp formula, a 256x1 ramp texture for dynamic gamma (R-9) and FXAA (R-18); UpdateGamma (:1857) reads UClient::Gamma/ Brightness/Contrast; RestoreGamma does nothing (the display's own ramp is never touched); BindingSwap / BindingSetSwapInterval (:462-469; UseVSync).
  • SDL2Drv: R-1.
  • Commits: f107ffb, 1700520; present pass extended by 47d3b6d (M7), 6921897 (O-8).

How the parts fit. The engine draws into "the back buffer", which is our main framebuffer object (drawn the GL way, bottom row first). Present (:2226) runs the present pass into the window's framebuffer and swaps. Every reader that needs the back buffer as a texture gets a flipped copy (R-6); screenshots (ReadPixels) are read from the main framebuffer before the present pass, so they do not contain the gamma ramp unless -glshotramp is given.

Why. D3D's hardware gamma ramp has no portable GL equivalent and WebGL has none at all; applying it in a shader works everywhere and leaves the desktop's ramp alone (design.md "GL4ES/WebGL limits": "no hardware gamma (do it in the present shader)").

Decisions and alternatives. Offscreen main framebuffer + present shader instead of drawing into the window's framebuffer: costs one full-screen pass per frame, gives gamma, FXAA and a sampleable depth buffer.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. The ramp is applied in windowed mode too (PC: full screen only). One extra full-screen pass per frame. -NOGAMMA turns the static ramp off.

Status. Measured: menu and maps with 0 GL errors (R-1). The gamma formula is Epic's; seen, not compared with a calibrated PC.

Off switch. -NOGAMMA (static ramp off). The present pass itself cannot be turned off.

#R-3 The Xbox pixel-shader translator tools/psh2glsl.py and the generated GLSL (M6, step S1)

What the player/owner sees. Nothing directly: it produces the GLSL that R-4, R-6 and R-14..R-16 draw with. All 100 of Epic's Xbox pixel shaders exist as GLSL.

Where.

  • tools/psh2glsl.py (1,564 lines, Python standard library only; commit 8289fbf, never changed since):
    • parser of Epic's .psh text (parse_psh :108; xps.1.1 and three ps.1.1 files; + pairing, xmma, xdd, xfc, modifiers, masks);
    • path A, a reference interpreter of the text from the XDK's instruction pages (run_text :507);
    • path B, an independent evaluator of the 60 definition words of the compiled .h Epic ships next to each source (D3DPIXELSHADERDEF, the register-combiner hardware model; load_def :561, run_def :633);
    • the GLSL generator (to_glsl :918): one fragment body per .psh, GLSL 1.20 that is also GLSL ES 1.00, no fixed-function built-ins (inputs u_tex0..3, u_c0..7, u_fogColor, v_color0/1, v_fog, v_tex0..3);
    • commands --selftest (A against B, 64 seeded cases per shader + 7 synthetic shaders worked out by hand), --emit, --check-emitted (fails if the checked-in files differ from the tool's output), --gputest, --dump NAME (main :1530).
  • tools/pshgpu/pshgpu.c (406 lines; X11 + GLX + GL 2.0 + FBO): draws every test case of every shader as a quad and compares the framebuffer with path A; built by the tool into build-pshgpu/.
  • Generated, checked in: Source/UC2GLDrv/Shaders/*.glsl (100 files, one per .psh, including the 32 DualDiffuse_* variants), Shaders/EPixelShader.tsv (enum value, name, file; derived by the tool from the _XBOX switch of UD3DRenderDevice::GetPixelShader, D3DRenderDevice.cpp 4110-4320), Shaders/ UC2GLPixelShaders.inc (7,930 lines: the same sources as C string literals plus the table GUC2GLPixelShaders[PS_MAX] with per-stage texture operation, constants read, fog use), so a run tree needs no shader files.
  • Input, unchanged: epic-sources/ShaderData/PixelShaders (100 .psh + their compiled .h).
  • Tools: tools/dev/ps-census.py (which pixel-shader material classes the packages contain, per map).

How the parts fit. The tool is offline. The driver only sees UC2GLPixelShaders.inc; it prepends #version 120 (desktop) or #version 100 + precision (GLES) and per-variant defines (UC2_TEXn_CUBE for a bound cube map, the Xbox's TEXMODE_ADJUST; UC2_SPECULARENABLE; UC2_ALPHATEST, used on GLES only).

Why. Epic's PC build drew Xbox-only pixel-shader materials with texture stage 0 (D3DMaterialState.cpp 1468-1507, design.md F1); the brief asks for the Xbox look. The shaders are readable text, so translating them is possible; a hand translation of 100 shaders would not be checkable.

Decisions and alternatives.

  • One generated file per .psh, including the 32 DualDiffuse variants (design.md had suggested collapsing them into one parametrised source): a second mechanism would be one more thing to keep right.
  • Two independent readings (text interpreter vs Microsoft's assembler output) instead of one; floats instead of modelling the NV2A's 9-bit fixed-point combiners.
  • GLSL 1.20 written ESSL-1.00-compatible from day one, so GL4ES/WebGL could take the same text (it did, R-19).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • 8-bit-plus-sign combiner precision is not modelled: the Xbox had banding we do not, notably in the xmma_x4 "all or nothing" masks.
  • Two semantic rules are not exercised by any of Epic's sources (default blue source of the xfc alpha argument; paired halves reading each other's destination); covered only by the hand-worked synthetic shaders.
  • ColorRemapGrayScale.h is missing from Epic's tree (Epic's own Xbox build would need xsasm first): that one shader has only path A and the GPU check as evidence.
  • Several inferences with no document: fog factor 1 when fog is off, opaque black for a stage a material leaves empty, initial values of unwritten registers (shaders.md "Unsure / inferred").
  • Evidence is "our reading of the text equals the assembler's output" and "the GLSL computes what the reading computes"; never a comparison with an Xbox.

Status. Measured (LOG.md M6, re-run by the coordinator): --selftest 100 shaders, 99 compared with their definition words, 6,336 cases, maximum difference 0, 0 failed; --gputest (Mesa llvmpipe) 107 shaders, 6,848 cases, 0 outside one 8-bit step (worst 0.5), 420 program variants linked, all 107 compile as GLSL ES 1.00. The tests have teeth: deliberately wrong semantics (_x4 as _x2, alpha half ignoring .b) fail 12-29 shaders. Re-run at every later renderer gate (M7, M7b, M13 m13-64bit.sh).

Off switch. Not applicable (offline tool). UsePixelShaders=False stops the driver using the output (R-4).

#R-4 Programmable materials: Xbox vertex fragments, GLSL programs, pixel-shader families (M6, steps S2-S4)

What the player/owner sees. Materials that PC drew flat or with the wrong colour now have their Xbox look: player skins with specular highlights, team skins where only the masked markings glow in team colour, emissive and team shaders on static meshes, the specular-mask glass. Visible difference to M5: the player's helmet and shoulders gain metal highlights (as the original PC binary shows); the CTF team skins are no longer tinted orange-red all over.

Where.

  • Source/UC2GLDrv/Src/UC2GLShaders.cpp (1,327 lines; commit cf8330e, extended by M7/M7b/M11):
    • UC2GL_IsM7PixelShader (:110), UC2GL_IsEngineGatedPixelShader (:154): which shader families wait for post-processing or an engine capability;
    • BuildVertexShaderSource (:187): writes one GLSL 1.20 vertex shader per 64-bit VertexShaderEnum, from GLSL snippets hand-ported from the Xbox .vsh fragments, emitted in the order FD3DCombinedVertexShader (D3DResource.cpp 2230-2620) splices them, each under a comment naming its .vsh. Ported: 19 of the 67 files (DefaultPosition, NotBoned, InitRGB, LightAmbient, LightHardDirectional5, MulVertexSpec, AddVertexRGB, OutLightRGB, the Tex* source and transform fragments, Fog); M7 added Distortion, DistortionAccum, ColorRemap; M7b the projector fragments (R-14);
    • InitPixelShaders (:619): compiles the base variant of every usable shader at start-up and precompiles the vertex shaders listed in Epic's VertexShaderConfigs.ini (:663-669);
    • PixelShaderFamilyReady (:732): a family = the enum values sharing one state handler; ready only if all of them compiled;
    • GetStateVertexShaderEnum (:769): builds Epic's enum, same bit layout, from the render state (lighting, vertex colour, stage sources/transforms, fog);
    • BeginProgramPass (:835) / EndProgramPass (:1114): per pass, streams as generic attributes, program, uniforms; UC2_RunProgramTest (:1147, M11's 420-program comparison).
  • Src/UC2GLMaterialState.cpp:1502 SetPixelShaderMaterial: PC's test IsXboxOnly() == 0 replaced by RenDev->PixelShaderFamilyReady(type) (:1554); the Xbox branch (all four stages, then the state handler) is taken for Xbox-only materials too. Test switch -gldualdiffuse=<n> (:1512-1537) turns PSEmissiveShader materials into a PSDualDiffuse to exercise the 32 DualDiffuse shaders no package uses.
  • Src/UC2GLPSMaterialState.cpp: Epic's 24 state handlers, unchanged in behaviour; constants go to FOpenGLRenderInterface::SetPixelShaderConstant (u_c0..7) instead of D3D's deferred state.
  • Src/UC2GLRenderInterface.cpp DrawPrimitive -> BeginProgramPass; blending, depth, alpha test, culling, colour mask, texture binding stay in SetMaterialBlending, shared by both paths.
  • ini: [UC2GLDrv.UC2GLRenderDevice] UsePixelShaders=True, PrecompileVertexShaders=True (Default.ini:398-399; commit bd6a4e2); README row Engine/Config/README.md:15.
  • Tools: tools/dev/run-uc2-shot.sh (screenshots from a pinned spawn, A/B in one run with -glpstoggle=), tools/dev/ps-census.py.
  • Epic's Engine/Core: none (shaders.md "Engine-side and configuration changes").
  • Commits: 8289fbf, cf8330e, bd6a4e2, 5cad7fd; merged 787c8c7.

How the parts fit. The engine and Epic's material classes are unchanged: a pixel-shader material's PreRender and Epic's state handler choose the EPixelShader and constants as on Xbox. The driver turns the pass's render state into Epic's vertex-shader enum and compiles a matching GLSL vertex shader, links it with the translated pixel shader (R-3) and draws. Everything that is not a pixel-shader material stays on the fixed- function path of R-1/R-5. Lighting for these passes is computed by the GLSL port of the Xbox fragments (up to 5 directional lights plus ambient, the selection SetLight already does on PC), not by GL's T&L.

Why. The M6 gate in PLAN.md: "Xbox-only materials rendered for real; team skins, emissive skins, dual-diffuse visibly correct".

Decisions and alternatives.

  • Vertex side generated per enum from Epic's fragments (the Xbox has no fixed-function vertex path at all), not GL fixed-function T&L: GL's fixed path cannot take diffuse/ambient material from the secondary colour, which static meshes need.
  • Static-mesh vertex colour detected from the bound streams (FVF_Specular stream present), because the PC engine passes VS_FixedFunction where the Xbox engine passes VS_StaticMeshVertexColor (an _XBOX branch of UnRenderStaticMesh.cpp, left untouched).
  • Where Xbox and PC differ (unlit, uncoloured geometry: ambient vs white), the Xbox formula wins (the brief); not seen on any content.
  • Not ported: GPU skinning and packed positions/terrain fragments (need XBOX_ASSET_FORMAT), the non-hard-coded light path Epic compiled out.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • 37 of the 59 shaders integrated at M6 are unreachable from shipped content (no package contains a PSDualDiffuse, PSShenuSkin, PSEmissiveConstColor or PSTeamSkinShaderOpacity): numeric tests are the only evidence for them.
  • Epic's own defects are kept: a one-call lag in the skin handlers (the first draw of a PSSkinShader with bUseLightingForSpecular uses the non-SpecLight shader); stage compaction in SetPixelShaderMaterial (a material with a NULL middle stage gets later textures one stage early); the "crazy constant" 16.0 clamped to 1.0 as on hardware (shaders.md "Defects found in Epic's shaders").
  • VertexShaderConfigs.ini precompiles most but not all vertex shaders used (2-4 per map still compiled on first use); programs are linked on first use: possible first-use hitches.
  • No state caching: program bound and uniforms uploaded per draw (shaders.md "Deferred: Performance").
  • Pixel-shader constants are device-global as in D3D; with UsePixelShaders=False the team skin is untinted while PC's fallback tinted it (M5's fallback is not exactly PC's).
  • No Xbox picture exists for any Xbox-only material (team skin, emissive skin, specular-mask glass).

Status. Measured (LOG.md M6): DM-Deadbolt 59 fragment shaders compiled, 0 failed; about 20,000-70,000 program draws, 0 GL errors over 1,800-2,400 frames. Compared with the original PC binary under wine where PC had its own DX8 shader: player skin mean difference over the character 6.3/255 with shaders, 16.7 without; CTF-Octane door frame 0.39 with, 5.76 without (shaders.md S4). Seen only: team skin, specular-mask glass, emissive specular, emissive skin ("runs clean, could not tell which object carries it").

Off switch. UsePixelShaders=False, -nopixelshaders (-pixelshaders to force on), console PIXELSHADERS 0|1, -glpstoggle=<s>; PrecompileVertexShaders=False skips the start-up precompile.

#R-5 Fixed-function texture stages as generated GLSL, combine4, four stages (O-9, O-9b)

What the player/owner sees. On the owner's NVIDIA GPU (Quadro P620) the maps look as on Mesa: DM-Azure's wooden pillars are wood (were orange metal), the cobra statue is golden, and in DM-AcidRain / DM-Tempest whole walls and floor gratings that were nearly black are textured. On every GPU and the web, 15 materials (the Raiden beam body, the cobra waterfall, some banners, floor panels, the Horizon and ViperPit waters) are now split into passes as Epic's 4-stage compiler split them.

Where.

  • New: Source/UC2GLDrv/Src/UC2GLFFProgram.cpp (360 lines): a pass's D3D texture stages become a GLSL fragment shader, generated per combination (ops, used arguments, 2D/cube target, colour sum, fog) and cached (FFMakeKey :100, FFBuildSource :189, FFGetProgram :237, BeginFixedFunctionProgram :303). It is linked without a vertex shader, so GL's fixed-function vertex stage (transform, lighting, texgen, texture matrices, fog coordinate) stays. D3D8 operation definitions, every stage saturated; colour sum and linear fog done in the shader; fog uses abs(gl_FogFragCoord) (:19, :232) because NVIDIA hands the signed eye-space z.
  • Src/UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp: UpdateTextureCaps (:315): stages = min(path's units, MaxTextureUnits, MaterialStages); TextureOpCaps reports MODULATEALPHA_ADDCOLOR | MULTIPLYADD when the program path is on or combine3/combine4 exists; on GLES the program path is never used (GL4ES generates its own shaders). Switches :565-578; console FFPROGRAMS :2028, GLMATCENSUS :2042, COMBINERTEST :2103; property MaterialStages :197, default 4 (:233).
  • Src/UC2GLRenderInterface.cpp: TranslateStageOp (:1196) + ToCombine4 (:1324): with GL_NV_texture_env_combine4 and no combine3, a three-operand op becomes Arg0*Arg1 + Arg2*Arg3; before O-9 the added term was dropped (a*b+c became a*b). DrawPrimitive binds the program after CommitTexCoords.
  • New test: Src/UC2GLCombinerTest.cpp (652 lines): 35 cases, every op the compiler emits, a 3-stage chain, 8 stages, colour sum, fog in front and behind, projected lookup, cube faces, LOD bias; GPU result against a CPU model written from the D3D8 SDK, tolerance 2/255. Census: Src/UC2GLPixelTrace.cpp:166-221 (-glmatcensus, GLMATCENSUS: every loaded material compiled with three cap sets, nothing drawn).
  • ini (Default.ini:393-394 and the GL4ES section, web ini too): MaterialStages=4, FixedFunctionPrograms=True; README row Engine/Config/README.md:21.
  • Tools: tools/dev/o9-nvidia.sh (220 lines: combiner tests on Mesa, the NVIDIA GPU headless through SDL's offscreen/EGL driver, and the twin; the "before" behaviour reproduced; caps; pictures), tools/dev/o9-census.sh, scenarios tools/dev/o9/{azure,acidrain,tempest,praxis-panes,perf,viperpit-*}.scen.
  • O-9b: Source/Engine/Src/UC2NetTest.cpp (NETPLACE LocalPlayer, a test command of ours) and tools/dev/m7-effects.sh (the DM-Praxis reference check placed at the oracle's camera, one run).
  • Commits: 51bb18e (step 0), c1df38f, dfe7030, e43d667, 05ec567, 28b7136, 638017e, faf3f1e; merged f2d7f25. O-9b: fcceed4, 5caec2b, merged 81e9b05.

How the parts fit. Epic's material compiler (R-1) is told how many stages it has and which three-operand ops exist; it then emits D3D stage lists. Those are drawn either by the generated program (default on desktop GL) or by GL's texture environment (comparison switch, and the only path on GLES/WebGL, where GL4ES emulates combine3 itself). Pixel-shader materials (R-4) are not affected. Without the ini keys the compiled defaults (4 stages, programs on) apply.

Why. Found by the O-5 work on the owner's GPU and raised by the owner's review (PLAN.md O-9): the NVIDIA compatibility profile reports 4 fixed-function texture units and no GL_ATI_texture_env_combine3. Epic's compiler then took its GeForce1-era fallbacks (MODULATE2X for the specular add: the "orange metal" pillar) and our translation dropped the added term of every unconditional MULTIPLYADD / "add to current" stage. Census over all 50 DM/CTF/OD maps: 327 distinct materials compiled differently on NVIDIA. Measured on NVIDIA against Mesa (mean difference of 255): wooden pillar 59.8 before, 0.76 after; cobra 5.34 / 0.29; DM-AcidRain 16.1 / 0.70; DM-Tempest 13.9 / 0.57.

Decisions and alternatives.

  • (b) GLSL for the stages by default: one path for every vendor, no dependence on combine3 (AMD/Mesa only) or combine4 (NVIDIA/Mesa only) or GL_MAX_TEXTURE_UNITS; about 300 lines.
  • (a) combine4 added to the texture-environment path too, so the comparison switch compares two correct paths.
  • 4 stages, Epic's number (MaterialStages=4): UC2's D3DRenderDevice.cpp 418-422 clamps both stage counts to 4 on PC and Xbox and refuses other cards; the old 8 came from Epic's UT2003-era OpenGLDrv ini section. With 8 the compiler folded 15 materials into one pass with a different formula.
  • (c) Epic's multipass answer was rejected: for a missing op Epic has only the GeForce1 substitutions.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Changes the picture on Mesa and the web for the 15 split materials (towards Epic's split).
  • AMD and Intel GPUs were never tried; the one vendor difference met (signed gl_FogFragCoord) is handled.
  • "4 stages is the Xbox picture" rests on Epic's code, not on an Xbox picture.
  • The web keeps GL4ES' own fixed-function emulation (checked numerically on the twin, not with this generator).
  • The coverage of combine3/combine4 across drivers could not be read (opengl.gpuinfo.org is filled by script; BLOCKERS.md "O-9").
  • Seen on the way, not fixed (BLOCKERS.md): POSTPROCESS 0 at run time leaves DM-Azure's cobra waterfall as an opaque violet stream (the distortion material's fallback; see R-7).

Status.
