Measured (LOG.md 2026-10-07; o9-nvidia.md gate): o9-nvidia.sh 12 PASS, 0 FAIL; combiner test 35/35 with programs on Mesa (worst 0.50/255) and NVIDIA (0.56), texture environment combine4 33 + 2 skipped on NVIDIA, the "before" path fails exactly the 11 three-operand cases, twin 33 + 2 skipped (no LOD bias on ES). Pinned DM-Deadbolt -nopostprocess unchanged on Mesa (99.997% within 2 levels). Performance on NVIDIA (DM-Azure, 1280x720, AA 4x, post-processing on): programs 11.4-12.4 ms vs texture environment 11.4-12.2 ms, no difference beyond noise (loaded machine). O-9b: the "swirled Praxis panes" signal was a test artefact (a close camera against the oracle's distant one; panes drawn exactly as before O-9); the check now places the camera at the oracle's PlayerStart0 and matches the wine reference at 2.58 (Mesa) / 2.65-2.72 (NVIDIA) mean difference.

Off switch. FixedFunctionPrograms=False, -glnoffprograms (-glffprograms to force), console FFPROGRAMS 0|1, -glfftoggle=<s>; -glstages=8 restores the old split; test masks -glnocombine3, -glnocombine4, -glunits=N; -glffsource logs the generated sources.

#R-6 Render targets, the back buffer and the depth buffer as textures (M7, step P1; driver side)

What the player/owner sees. Nothing by itself: it is what every post-process effect (R-8) reads from. With post-processing off the frame is pixel-identical to M6's in its static part.

Where.

  • New: Source/UC2GLDrv/Src/UC2GLPostProcess.cpp (1,429 lines; commit 47d3b6d, extended by 4ed20bc, f5fbcae, 6921897, f8dfb79, 7f58c55). Its header comment states the five coordinate conventions (textures top row first as D3D; the main framebuffer the one exception, flipped only in the copy passes; GL window depth = D3D z; the half-texel convention of Epic's screen tiles; distortion's screen position).
    • small programs: copy (GPostCopyFragmentShader :74), depth encode (:90), depth copy (:103, writes gl_FragDepth), reference copy (:119, O-10);
    • GetSampledTexture (:367) / UpdateSampledTexture (:439) / PrepareSampledTargets (:565): a stage that samples the default render target binds a snapshot of the main framebuffer, refreshed before a draw only if something was drawn since (MainColorVersion);
    • depth as a texture: the main depth-stencil attachment is a texture; in "depth" usage a pass re-encodes it into RGBA8 with the byte layout of the Xbox's B8G8R8A8 view of D24S8 (blue = bits 23..16, green 15..8, red 7..0), which DOF.psh reads with texreg2gb;
    • Epic's Xbox bodies carried over with their comments: GetBackBufferMaterial (:614), GetBackBufferCopyTarget (:638), UpdateBackBufferCopyTarget (:689), GetBackBufferCopyMaterial (:806), CopyDepthBuffer (:841, a quad that writes the sampled depth as gl_FragDepth; source must be the default target), UC2GL_UpdateDOFRampLookupTexture (:940), FOpenGLDynamicGamma (:1075, R-9).
  • Hooks: UC2GLRenderInterface.cpp (DrawPrimitive, SetMaterialBlending, Clear, GetGLMatrices: the 1/32-texel PointSampleNudge for point-sampled render-target stages), UC2GLShaders.cpp (vertex fragments Distortion.vsh, DistortionAccum.vsh, ColorRemap.vsh), UC2GLMaterialState.cpp (HandlePSVertexShader :1390, Epic's _XBOX body, now run when post-processing is on; UpdateDOFRampLookupTexture :1235), UC2GLPSMaterialState.cpp:367 (SetMatState_PS_Distortion: #ifdef _XBOX became RenDev->PostProcessActive()), UC2GLResource.cpp (render targets), UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp.
  • Epic's shared code: this commit (47d3b6d) already added FRenderCaps::XboxPostProcess and RenDevHasXboxPostProcess() (UnRenDev.h, UnRenderResource.cpp); their users came in P2, see R-7.
  • Tests: Src/UC2GLSelfTest.cpp (console POSTSELFTEST, -glselftest; R-21); tools/dev/m7-effects.sh.

How the parts fit. The engine (through the lifted gates of R-7) asks the interface for the back-buffer material, the back-buffer copy target, a depth copy, a DOF ramp; Epic's Xbox driver code does the rest, drawing through the engine's own materials (UPSBlurMaterial, PS_LuminanceTexIndex) so the same programs as the game's effects are exercised. The driver substitutes copies where the Xbox sampled the framebuffer it was drawing into (GL forbids that).

Why. Every post-process effect of the Xbox reads the back buffer or its depth while drawing (design.md F1: FPostProcessRender::Render and the camera effects are _XBOX-only; PC's driver returned early).

Decisions and alternatives.

  • Snapshot on demand (one full-screen copy per draw that reads the back buffer after it changed) instead of rendering the scene into a sampleable second colour buffer: simplest correct substitute; not optimised.
  • Depth: re-encode the depth texture into the Xbox's byte layout, so Epic's DOF shader runs unchanged.
  • Epic's SetMatState_PS_Distortion writes InShader->PixelShaderType = NULL in its "draw nothing" branches, which permanently changes the material object: not carried over (the only place in P1 where Epic's behaviour is not kept; UC2GLPSMaterialState.cpp:455-459).
  • The render-target pool's destructor asserts no references are left; at exit the camera effects still hold some: UUC2GLRenderDevice::Destroy hands them to their holders (leaked at exit) (dd3f362).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Cost: three to four full-screen snapshots per frame with the default effects, plus the depth encode and about a dozen half/quarter-size passes. Measured under software GL at 1280x720 (M7 gate): DM-Deadbolt 30.1 fps off, 7.9 on (3.8x); DM-Praxis 21.5 / 6.6 (3.3x). On the NVIDIA GPU the frame times are small (O-9: ~12 ms at 720p with AA and post-processing on; O-10: 4K about 11.3 ms after 7f58c55), not measured per effect.
  • DOF reads the encoded depth through a bilinear filter (Epic's stage state): where the middle byte wraps between neighbours the average is not a depth; estimated ~2% alpha error along thin lines, never seen in a picture.
  • That NV2A resolves a point-sampled tie to the texel right/below is an assumption (used for CopyDepthBuffer and the nudge).
  • Split-screen (more than one viewport) was never run.

Status. Measured: self-test (M7) snapshot exact over 921,600 pixels, depth-color max 2 units of 2^24, depth-copy 1 unit, blur taps within 1.0/255, bb-copy 1.44, dof-ramp 0.72, remap 0.5, plus luminance; 10 tests at M7, 11 with M11's fog, 15 with O-10 (LOG.md 2026-10-08: 15/15). The tests caught a real bug (depth 1.0 encoded as 0.9961: the sky would have been blurred at 80% alpha) and the author's own wrong half-texel model.

Off switch. UsePostProcess=False, -nopostprocess / -postprocess, console POSTPROCESS 0|1, -glpptoggle=<s>.

#R-7 The Xbox post-process paths lifted in Epic's engine and game code (XboxPostProcess, M7 step P2)

What the player/owner sees. Distortion is see-through (the DM-Praxis panes show the sea instead of flat periwinkle; DM-Decay icicles are translucent ice instead of dark-blue shapes), emissive glow, bloom, an always-on depth of field, screen and radial blur, colour remaps (berserk, pause, Liandri).

Where. Epic's shared code, all in commit 94654e2 unless said:

  • Engine/Inc/UnRenDev.h:770-819 FRenderCaps::XboxPostProcess (default 0; commit 47d3b6d) and ShippedDOFBlurAlpha (R-10); declaration RenDevHasXboxPostProcess() (:894). FRenderCaps is a plain C++ struct, not mirrored in script.
  • Engine/Src/UnRenderResource.cpp:48 RenDevHasXboxPostProcess() (47d3b6d): false without a client or device (dedicated server, commandlets), for the null device and any device leaving the field 0.
  • Engine/Src/UnRender.cpp:3256-3262 FPostProcessRender::Render: the body after the draw-list test was #ifdef _XBOX; now #ifndef _XBOX if( !RenDevHasXboxPostProcess() ) return; #endif (gate lifted).
  • Engine/Classes/PSDistortionBaseMaterial.uc (cpptext) and Engine/Inc/EngineClasses.h:7678-7686: IsPostProcess()/RequiresSorting() declared instead of PC's inline POSTPROCESS_NONE/1; defined in Engine/Src/UnPixelShaderMaterial.cpp:408-421 (Xbox answer with the capability, PC's otherwise). Only the C++ side changes; no package was rebuilt for this.
  • Engine/Src/UnPixelShaderMaterial.cpp: #ifdef _XBOX/#else became if( RenDevHasXboxPostProcess() ) {Xbox} else {PC} in UPSDistortionMaterial::PreRender (:249), UPSBlendedDistortionMaterial::PreRender (:300), UPSDistortionBaseMaterial::PreRender (:349), UPSBackBufferEffect::PreRender (:841), UPSPlayerCamMaterial::PreRender (:885), UPSColorRemapMaterial::PreRender (:942).
  • UCGameplay/Src/UCCameraEffects.cpp: Epic's #ifndef _XBOX return; #endif at the top of 16 functions became #ifndef _XBOX if( !RenDevHasXboxPostProcess() ) return; #endif (UUCScreenBlur::PostRender :89; Pre/PostRender of UUCScreenDOF :237/:312, MotionBlur :559/:587, HeatShimmer :709/:725, RadialBlur :775/:790, Bloom :934/:1014, Emissive :1210/:1315; PostRender of Berserk, MenuPause, Liandri); whole-body #ifdef _XBOX of UUCScreenSniperOverlay and UUCScreenTeleportOverlay Pre/PostRender became if( RenDevHasXboxPostProcess() ) (:1959, :2004, :2197, :2249); UUCScreenDOF constructor/Destroy (:153-200): the Xbox constructor body moved into InitSharedDOFResources() (:170), called from the constructor if the capability is there, else on first PreRender; Destroy releases only what this object took. UCGameplay/Inc/UCCameraEffects.h: one non-virtual member function declared (no layout change).
  • Engine/Src/UnRenderUtil.cpp:1462-1476 FScreenPrimUtil::DrawTile (commit 6fa7758): bug fixed: Epic's PC branch queued quad-ordered corners as a triangle strip, so a quarter of every tile (left edge to centre) was never drawn; now a triangle fan, one tile per draw. Only Xbox-only paths call it.
  • Driver: UUC2GLRenderDevice::GetRenderCaps (UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:2368) sets the capability when PostProcessActive() (ini switch on, pixel shaders on, copy programs built); SetPostProcess (UC2GLPostProcess.cpp:275) marks every actor's skins changed and clears the emitters' cached post-process flag when switched at run time; Lock (:2160-2183) applies the Xbox client defaults once (R-9, R-11).
  • ini: UsePostProcess=True (Default.ini:400, 6fa7758), [SDL2Drv.SDL2Client] MaxDistortion=35.0 (:178).
  • Tests: tools/dev/m7-effects.sh (one capped run per effect, A/B pictures, a line per effect with Critical, GL errors, failed programs, changed pixels; a DM-Praxis check against the wine reference); POSTSELFTEST.
  • Commits: 47d3b6d, 94654e2, 4be1bb8, 6fa7758, dd3f362, 4ed20bc, d546816, 0b1fa88, f4d1026, 9a181ef, efd22ef; merged 225b0d5.

How the parts fit. The native engine side decides what to draw (Epic's Xbox code, unchanged except the gate); the driver implements the interface it calls (R-6) and the 25 shaders (R-3). The .uc side is unchanged: UCPlayerController.CreateOverlays already creates the emissive+bloom and always-on DOF overlays for every player; the script defaults are the shipped UCGameplay.u's. Without the capability (null device, UsePostProcess=False, a dedicated server) every site takes Epic's PC branch exactly as before.

Why. The brief's M7 gate ("distortion no longer shows normal maps"); design.md F1 (PC distortion opaque, all post-processing _XBOX-gated in engine and driver). CLAUDE.md rule: lift Xbox gates through a named capability, keep both branches.

Decisions and alternatives. One capability and one test function for all sites instead of defining a macro per site or deleting the PC branches. UClient::Init's _XBOX lines (DynamicGamma = 1; LuminosityBias = 1) were left alone (the device does not exist yet there); the device applies them at its first frame instead.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Touches Epic's shared code in nine files (listed at the end); every site keeps the PC branch.
  • Switching post-processing off at run time does not restore the exact pre-M7 state of distortion materials (the PC branch writes only stage 1): DM-Azure's cobra waterfall becomes an opaque violet stream after POSTPROCESS 0 (BLOCKERS.md, O-9; starting with -nopostprocess was not checked). Off-to-on is clean.
  • Epic's HasPostProcessMaterial decides an actor's draw list from its first material only (kept).
  • Screen-space distortion reads whatever pixel is displaced into place, foreground included (the Praxis pillar pulled into the panes; inherent to Epic's method, kept).

Status. Measured (LOG.md M7, coordinator's run): self-test 10/0; 14 effect pairs with 0 Critical, 0 GL errors, 0 failed programs; post-processing-off picture pixel-identical to M6 in the static part (0 differing pixels for x >= 760). Latest: m7-effects.sh self-test 15/15 and 14/14 effect lines clean (LOG.md 2026-10-08). Seen only, no reference: every "on" picture (Epic's PC build never had these effects; no Xbox picture except the owner's few xemu shots).

Off switch. UsePostProcess=False / -nopostprocess / POSTPROCESS 0 (then every Epic site takes its PC branch).

#R-8 The effects themselves (distortion, glow, bloom, depth of field, blurs, colour remaps, player cam)

What the player/owner sees. See the table; "N+S" = numerically checked + seen, "S" = seen only (plausible, no reference), "H" = handler ported, never observed in content.

Effect Shaders Entry point (Epic's code, now running) Status
Distortion, opaque (glass, energy fields, torch heat shimmer) PS_DistortionAccum, PS_Distortion; DistortionAccum.vsh, Distortion.vsh FPostProcessRender::Render, UPSDistortion*::PreRender S (DM-Praxis panes, DM-Decay torch, DM-Undertow column)
Distortion, blended PS_ModulatedDistortion (+ Additive, Subtractive) same, PP_STAGE_RENDER_COLOR modulated S (DM-Decay icicles); additive/subtractive H
Emissive glow PS_EmissiveSelectBlur, PS_Blur UUCScreenEmissive (always on) S
Bloom PS_BloomSelect, PS_Blur UUCScreenBloom (always on) S
Depth of field, always on PS_DepthOfField, PS_Blur UUCScreenDOF, UpdateDOFRampLookupTexture N+S (only beyond z = 0.98: far mountains, sky edges)
Depth of field, focus on actor/distance (matinee, sniper) same USubActionDepthOfField, UUCScreenSniperOverlay H
Screen blur (concussion, BlurMe) PS_Blur UUCScreenBlur S
Radial/speed blur (sprint, teleport, sniper) PS_MotionBlur UUCScreenRadialBlur S (held on by console)
Motion blur with accumulation PS_MotionBlur, PS_Mask UUCScreenMotionBlur H (no script creates one)
Heat shimmer camera effect PS_Distortion UUCScreenHeatShimmer H (creation commented out in UCPlayerController)
Colour remap: berserk, pause, combo flash, sun flare, teleport PS_ColorRemap, ColorRemap.vsh UUCScreenBerserk, MenuPause (+2 subclasses), TeleportOverlay berserk, pause N+S; others H
Colour remap masked: sniper overlay PS_ColorRemapMasked UUCScreenSniperOverlay H (sniper rifle not reachable in tests)
Liandri overlay PS_ColorRemapGrayScale UUCScreenLiandri S (random noise: not comparable between runs)
Player cam screens PS_PlayerCam* UPSPlayerCamMaterial::PreRender H (program links in CTF-Recoil, surface not identified)
Back-buffer effect, refraction, texture-index materials, emissive select without blur PS_MaskedLuminance, PS_Refraction, PS_UseTexIndex*, PS_EmissiveSelect various H; refraction and texture-index unreachable (no object in any package); PS_EmissiveSelect compiled out by Epic (#if 0)
Depth swizzle PS_DepthSwizzleColor Xbox CopyDepthBuffer only not used (replaced by the driver's depth copy)

Of the 25 shaders M6 left for M7, 12 were drawn in a run at M7 and M7b added PS_ShadowBlur (R-15); 12 were never drawn: Mask, AdditiveDistortion, SubtractiveDistortion, MaskedLuminance, PlayerCamNoise, PlayerCamTwoNoise, ColorRemapMasked, and five that are unreachable or unused by design (Refraction, UseTexIndex, UseTexIndexMasked, EmissiveSelect, DepthSwizzleColor) (postprocess.md "Per-effect status").

Where. As R-6/R-7; the effect-specific lines are in Epic's files listed there. Effect triggers for testing: console BlurMe, set UCScreenBerserk Enabled 1, set UCScreenLiandri Enabled 1, set UCScreenMenuPause Enabled 1 (also enables its two subclasses: a trap for testers), radial blur needs set UCPlayerController RadialBlurSpeed 0 etc. (postprocess.md P3 table).

How the parts fit / Why. As R-7.

Decisions and alternatives. All of Epic's effects are enabled as the Xbox script creates them; none was removed or tuned except the owner items R-10, R-11, R-13.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • No reference picture for any "on" state: strength of glow and bloom, the berserk overlay on pawn attachments (the shoulder orb is remapped, the pawn body is stencilled out), the pause screen's teal negative (owner: "matches the Xbox", PLAN.md owner review), the Praxis pillar artefact.
  • The red pawn's halo in CTF-Recoil is strong (emissive team markings through emissive select and bloom); unknown whether the Xbox halo is that strong.
  • Never run: split-screen, cinematics with focus DOF, the sniper overlay.

Status. As R-7. The owner reviewed the preview on 2026-10-06: pause overlay and dynamic gamma "correct"; DM-Praxis panes close up "spot on"; other distortions needed work (O-5..O-7).

Off switch. POSTPROCESS 0 turns all of them off; individual camera effects through set UCScreen<Name> Enabled 0 (script properties), DYNAMICGAMMA 0.

#R-9 Dynamic gamma (M7)

What the player/owner sees. The picture's brightness adapts to the scene, as on the Xbox: dark maps are brightened (DM-Deadbolt noticeably; bright highlights clip). Owner (2026-10-06): "it works as it should".

Where.

  • Src/UC2GLPostProcess.cpp:1075-1405: FOpenGLDynamicGamma = Epic's FD3DDynamicGamma: every 0.05 s the back buffer is drawn into a 16x16 target through PS_LuminanceTexIndex (since O-10 from the 480-line reference copy), read back with glReadPixels (the Xbox read texture memory), turned into a histogram, and a 256-entry ramp is computed from its 10%/90% points, LevelGamma and LevelContrast; SetGammaRamp (:519) uploads it to the present pass's 256x1 ramp texture (R-2). GetAverageSceneLuminosity (:1391) also feeds the bloom threshold.
  • Src/UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:2160-2183 (Lock, commit 4be1bb8): with post-processing on, the first frame sets UClient::DynamicGamma = 1 and LuminosityBias = 1.0 (what UClient::Init does under _XBOX); a LuminosityBias of 0 is treated as 1.
  • Console DYNAMICGAMMA 0|1, LUMINOSITYBIAS; -glshotramp puts the ramp into screenshots.

How the parts fit. The engine already calls RI->UpdateDynamicGamma() every frame on every platform (UnRender.cpp:2404-2408, FPlayerSceneNode::Render); Epic's PC driver returned early. No engine change for this feature.

Why. Xbox default behaviour; part of M7's scope ("dynamic gamma" in the M7 notes).

Decisions and alternatives. On by default because the Xbox has it on; confirmed by the owner.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • A user cannot turn it off persistently through the ini: the device sets DynamicGamma = 1 again at the first frame of every session (only the console command works for that session).
  • UClient::DynamicGamma already read True on PC builds with nothing setting it (not understood; suspected shared storage with the next bool property; postprocess.md defect 5).
  • glReadPixels of the 16x16 target every 0.05 s is a GPU-CPU sync point (not measured as a cost).
  • It adapts per frame update, so at low frame rates it takes tens of seconds to settle; this made picture comparisons time-dependent (pp-scale.md step 3) and is why tests freeze or disable it.
  • The histogram debug overlay (ShowDebug, bDebugShowGamma) is not ported.

Status. Measured: self-test luminance (the luminance pass). Seen: fx-dyngamma pair. Whether the Xbox looked this bright on dark maps: not verified (owner's opinion only).

Off switch. Console DYNAMICGAMMA 0 (per session); UsePostProcess=False (all post-processing).

#R-10 Depth-of-field blur alpha: the shipped value (O-3)

What the player/owner sees. The always-on depth of field softens far geometry with alpha 65 in both camera modes, as the shipped Xbox binary did. With the script's intended value it would be 100 in third person (stronger).

Where.

  • Epic's shared code: UCGameplay/Inc/UCCameraEffects.h (UUCScreenDOF): the header had fallen behind UCScreenDOF.uc and lacked FirstPersonAlpha/ThirdPersonAlpha before AbsoluteBlurAlpha; added (layout fix, commit 44f81c6, M3; runtime-port.md item 5). With Epic's stale header the shipped binary read FirstPersonAlpha's byte (65) where it meant AbsoluteBlurAlpha.
  • UCGameplay/Src/UCCameraEffects.cpp:343-352 (UUCScreenDOF::PostRender, #ifndef _XBOX): ModulateBlurColor.A = ShippedDOFBlurAlpha ? FirstPersonAlpha : AbsoluteBlurAlpha, every frame (commit 94654e2).
  • Engine/Inc/UnRenDev.h FRenderCaps::ShippedDOFBlurAlpha; set by GetRenderCaps from the device's DOFShippedBlurAlpha.
  • ini: DOFShippedBlurAlpha=True (Default.ini:401; was False until commit 2208566, the owner's O-3 decision); README row 18. Console DOFSHIPPEDALPHA 0|1.

How the parts fit. Script chooses the alpha per camera mode; native code copies it; the device flag picks which byte native reads. Without the ini key the compiled default is 0 (UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:214), i.e. the script's value 100 in third person.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-10.

Why. Owner's review O-3 (PLAN.md): the owner could not see the DOF; decision "default DOFShippedBlurAlpha to the shipped value (65) so the look matches the Xbox binary".

Decisions and alternatives. The shipped behaviour (a header bug of Epic's) wins over the script's intent, consistent with "keep the Xbox behaviour". The alternative (100 in third person) is one setting away.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Ini and compiled default disagree (ini True, code 0): an ini without the key (an old user ini) gets the non-shipped value. Worth aligning. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-10.)
  • O-3 also asked for an explanatory picture pair: fx-dof-alpha and fx-azure exist (postprocess.md P5b); the effect is subtle (mean difference 1.4/255 in the far region between 65 and 100).

Status. Seen: fx-dof-alpha pair (DM-Azure, far region less softened with 65). Measured as part of O-10: DOF blur sigma 3.10 reference pixels, alpha 0.228 = 65/255 x the ramp. LOG.md has no O-3 line (PLAN.md says "see O-3 in LOG.md when done"): the change is recorded only in commit 2208566 and BLOCKERS.md "Decided by the owner".

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-10.

Off switch. DOFShippedBlurAlpha=False or DOFSHIPPEDALPHA 0 (script value); set UCScreenDOF Enabled 0 removes the DOF.

#R-11 Distortion amount and range: MaxDistortion, resolution scaling, DistortionCullDistance (M7, O-7)

What the player/owner sees. Distortion displaces the picture by the Xbox's amount at any resolution, and distant panes (DM-Praxis) keep their distortion farther away than on the Xbox (O-7: "figure out what tuneable is there AND THEN increase it").

Where.

  • [SDL2Drv.SDL2Client] MaxDistortion=35.0 (Default.ini:178, web :197; commit 6fa7758): PC's ini had no key, which reads 0 (no displacement and a division by zero in the accumulation pass); 35 is DefaultXbox.ini's value; Lock falls back to 35 when it reads 0 (UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:2176-2180).
  • Resolution scaling: Src/UC2GLShaders.cpp:1075-1076: the distortion amounts (pixels of a 640x480 back buffer on Xbox) are multiplied by height / PostProcessReferenceHeight (480 when the setting is 0).
  • Range: Src/UC2GLMaterialState.cpp:1381-1419 (HandlePSVertexShader): Epic's static FLOAT DISTORTION_CULL_DISTANCE = 5000.f (D3DMaterialState.cpp 1377) became GUC2GLDistortionCullDistance, read at the first distortion draw from [UC2GLDrv.UC2GLRenderDevice] DistortionCullDistance (missing or not positive = 5000, the Xbox value). DistortionAccum.vsh scales each vertex's distortion by 1 - saturate(z/D)^2. Console DISTORTIONCULLDISTANCE [<units>] (UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:2013-2016). (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-11.)
  • ini: DistortionCullDistance=15000 (Default.ini:402, commit 7463d9c; ported into the M11 driver by 93c2f27); README row 22.
  • Tools: tools/dev/fx-owner.sh (scenarios praxis-far, praxis-near; UC2_GPU=1 runs on the NVIDIA GPU headless), console GLPIXTRACE (statistics of the distortion accumulation target).

How the parts fit. Driver-only (the knob is in Epic's D3D driver code, carried over); the client setting is Epic's UClient::MaxDistortion.

Why. M7: without MaxDistortion nothing was displaced. Scaling: the Xbox amounts are pixels of a 480-line picture. O-7: owner's picture PraxisWindowPanesDistance.png (distortion gone at a distance).

Decisions and alternatives. Scaling by height/480 (M7's own decision, later tied to PostProcessReferenceHeight by O-10) instead of Epic's fixed pixels. O-7: the existing constant made a setting and raised; the effect itself was not restructured; the mip LOD bias of -1.8 (a second, smaller factor) left alone.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • 15000 is a deviation from the Xbox's 5000 by the owner's request: 97% of the distortion is left at 2650 units (Xbox 72%), 89% at 5000 (Xbox 0).
  • The GL4ES subclass reads this key from the [UC2GLDrv.UC2GLRenderDevice] section (hard-coded section name), not its own; the web ini has both sections, so it works, but the GL4ES section's own copy of the key is ignored. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-11.)
  • The size of the effect (height/480 scaling) is unverified against an Xbox; the owner called the close-up Praxis panes "spot on".
  • The splash distortion fade (O-5's second sentence) was analysed as fading with particle alpha exactly as on the Xbox; inferred from the code, not measured.

Status. Measured (fx-fixes.md step 4): mean accumulated offset over the panes at 2650 units: 5000 -> 6.32/9.97, 15000 -> 8.51/13.33, no fade -> 8.81/13.78. Seen: fx-praxis-far-* pictures (difference modest at that camera).

Off switch. DistortionCullDistance=5000 (or remove the key) for the Xbox range; MaxDistortion in the client section for the amount.

#R-12 Cube maps with a missing face (DM-Azure water pool, O-5); the waterfall report (O-6)

What the player/owner sees. DM-Azure's centre pool has a water surface with golden reflections (was flat transparent black/grey). The owner's waterfall report (O-6) is not reproduced and not changed.

Where.

  • Src/UC2GLResource.cpp (FOpenGLTexture::Cache, cube branch; UploadMip accepts a NULL face): every cube face is uploaded, missing ones zeroed (DXT1 zero blocks = opaque black); on GLES the face's mip chain is completed too. Commit 2238100 (main), ported into the M11 driver by 93c2f27.
  • Debug tools from the same work: Src/UC2GLPixelTrace.cpp (console GLPIXTRACE <x> <y> [all]: what the next frame draws over a pixel, material, blend, stages, LOD bias, and the distortion accumulation target's statistics, as a PPM); Engine/Src/UC2NetTest.cpp NETPLACE takes a pitch (our test file).
  • Tools: tools/dev/fx-owner.sh (scenarios pool, pool-splash, waterfall), UC2_BIN= before/after binaries.
  • Commits: bff872f, 2238100, 456dea3, 7463d9c, 8f0dafc, 7f2a383; merged d3e88e7; M11 port 93c2f27.

How the parts fit. Driver-only. The pool is FluidSurfaceInfo0, its shader uses the cube map T_Liquid_Decay_Water_C_CM, which has five faces (the -Z face is None in the package). GL treats an incomplete cube as unusable and fixed-function GL switches the stage off, so only the vertex colour at almost zero alpha was drawn. D3D (D3DResource.cpp 575-643, Xbox and PC) allocates all six faces and fills those that exist.

Why. Owner's review O-5 with xemu pictures (temp/AzureWaterDistortion.png vs AzureWaterFlat.png).

Decisions and alternatives. Reproduce D3D's allocation instead of skipping the material or fixing the package.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. The missing face's content is an assumption (zero = black, never seen from above). O-6 (the waterfall distortion disappearing in first person while standing still): drawn every frame on Mesa and on the owner's NVIDIA GPU in our runs; needs the owner's exact spot (BLOCKERS.md "Owner review O-6"). "The waterfall drawn over the character": explained as Epic's half-resolution depth mask and screen-space read (the Xbox's design too); inferred, not changed.

Status. Seen: matches the owner's Xbox screenshot in kind (water layer, golden reflections), not pixel-close (cameras differ), on Mesa and NVIDIA (logs/fx/o5-pool-gpu-before-after.png). Gate: m7-effects 10/10 + 14 lines clean, psh2glsl tests, verifyclasses, pinned DM-Deadbolt unchanged (LOG.md 2026-10-07).

Off switch. None (a loader fix).

#R-13 Post-process blurs sized for a 480-line reference picture (O-10)

What the player/owner sees. Bloom, emissive glow, depth of field and radial blur cover the same share of the picture at any resolution as on the Xbox at 640x480. Before: at 1440x1080 the glow was half as wide, at 4K a third. 4K is about 10% faster than before.

Where.

  • Epic's shared code (commit f8dfb79):
    • Engine/Inc/UnRenDev.h:791-799 FRenderCaps::PostProcessReferenceHeight (default 0 = Epic's behaviour); Engine/Src/UnRenderResource.cpp:93 RenDevPostProcessReferenceHeight().
    • Engine/Inc/UnRenderUtil.h:676-692 FPostProcessReference + GetPostProcessReference(); Engine/Src/UnRenderUtil.cpp:2363 (the reference picture: R lines, W*R/H columns, PixelScale = H/R) and FilterCopyRenderTarget (:2469-2484): when the source is the back buffer, its taps are in reference pixels.
    • UCGameplay/Src/UCCameraEffects.cpp: UUCScreenDOF::PreRender (:259-300), UUCScreenBloom::PreRender (:949-1004), UUCScreenEmissive::PreRender (:1226-1283) size their targets from the reference picture; UUCScreenRadialBlur::PostRender (:839-846) scales the insets by W/(R*4/3) across and H/R down; UCPP_TargetSize (:60) compares sizes as the whole numbers they are created with. Bug fixed on the way: Epic compared the INT target size with the FLOAT product (GetWidth() != BlurSizeX), so at sizes like 1366x768 (341.5) the pooled targets were released and re-created every frame and the emissive streak history was lost. One log line per (re)created target.
  • Driver: Src/UC2GLPostProcess.cpp: the reference copy (GPostReferenceFragmentShader :119, UC2GL_BuildReferenceProgram :136, UC2GL_UpdateReferenceCopy :391): an area-averaging downsample of the main framebuffer to R lines (N x N bilinear taps, N = ceil(H/R) <= 8, a compile-time constant since 7f58c55; the uniform-bounded loop cost ~4 ms a frame at 4K); PrepareSampledTargets routes every pass that samples the back buffer into a target no taller than 3/4 of it (DOF pass 0, emissive select, bloom select, luminance) to that copy; UpdateBackBufferCopyTarget (:747-750) taps x PixelScale, target stays half the back buffer. Src/UC2GLShaders.cpp:1075 distortion scale = H/R. Property PostProcessReferenceHeight (compiled default 480), console PPREFERENCE [h].
  • Self-test: UC2GLSelfTest.cpp new tests pp-sizes, ref-copy, ref-taps, bb-copy-ref (:812-912); the older eleven run with reference 0.
  • ini: PostProcessReferenceHeight=480 in both device sections of Default.ini (:406) and web/Default.ini (commit 2aad748); README row 24.
  • Tools: tools/dev/pp-scale.sh (347 lines: selftest, glow, dof, blur, radial, identity at 640x480, aspect, GPU perf, sheets; freezes game time and guards frame counts), tools/dev/pp-measure.py (319 lines: glow width/peak, Gaussian sigma fit, radial zoom model), run-uc2-shot.sh UC2_CAP.
  • Commits: 3f4a237 (plan), eacf619, f8dfb79, 2aad748, 940796b, f08371b, 082ad26, d034f97, 9a2e8c7, 5520a20, 7f58c55, 720a7a6; merged e42eafb (seven conflicts with M7b resolved by hand).

How the parts fit. The engine side (Epic's camera effects) decides target sizes and tap distances from the reference; the driver makes the reference copy so the first pass of each chain reads a well-filtered 480-line picture texel for texel as the Xbox did. Without the driver's copy the engine change alone would alias (four taps over a footprint k times larger: small lamps flickering in and out of the glow). Without the capability (PostProcessReferenceHeight=0, other devices) Epic's sizes apply.

Why. Owner (2026-10-07): "I want to see if the bloom and blur radious is resolution dependant"; measured yes (glow 8.3% of the height at 640x480, 4.2% at 1440x1080). Owner's guidance: "prefer the game looking beautiful than being correct".

Decisions and alternatives. Size targets AND the first pass's taps for a reference picture, with a dedicated area-averaged copy, instead of only resizing targets (rejected: aliasing) or only scaling taps. The back-buffer copy target stays half size (it is also the distortion accumulation target; a finer field keeps distortion edges sharp). Concussion blur, colour remaps, emissive falloff per frame, heat shimmer: unchanged (texture-coordinate units or time constants; full audit table in pp-scale.md).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Deviates from Epic's PC code path deliberately (Epic's code at high resolution gives narrow blurs); equals the Xbox only by construction (same share of a 640x480 picture), not by an Xbox comparison.
  • The emissive streak falloff (0.4 per frame) stays frame-rate dependent (not a distance; not this task).
  • At 16:9 the concussion blur's horizontal wobble is Epic's material data in texture units, left as is.
  • Several measurements needed re-runs because of machine load and the dynamic gamma's slow settling; the final run was on a quiet machine.

Status. Measured (pp-scale.md step 11, final binary, quiet machine, pp-scale.sh 10 PASS, 0 FAIL): glow width 8.54% (640x480), 8.54% (1440x1080; before 4.17), 8.33% (2880x2160; before 3.12); DOF sigma 3.10 / 3.15 / 3.10 reference pixels (before 1.45 / 0.70); radial blur equivalent inset 9.10 / 9.20 / 9.20 (before 4.60 / 2.30); 640x480 identical to main outside the spinning pickups; self-test 15/15. GPU (Quadro P620, frozen view): 3840x2160 about 11.3 ms against 12.5 ms before; 1920x1080 unchanged. LOG.md 2026-10-08: pp-scale.sh selftest/glow/dof/radial 6/0 on main.

Off switch. PostProcessReferenceHeight=0 or console PPREFERENCE 0 (Epic's sizes in back-buffer pixels).

#R-14 Xbox static-projector batching and the 12 projector shaders (M7b item 1, XboxProjectors)

What the player/owner sees. Map projectors (light patterns through grates and wire meshes, light pools) look the same as before except the 19 projectors that blend with the surface's own material (MaterialBlendingOp): on CTF-Recoil the corridor floors no longer turn dark and dirty under the wire-mesh grids; the grid is a clear pattern of light. Six maps are affected (CTF-Recoil 8, DM-Meridian 4, OD-Cancer 2, DM-Legacy 2, ACT-SelketCheats 2, OD-Prism 1); only CTF-Recoil was pictured. Fewer draw calls (DM-DeepCore 85 -> 81, CTF-Recoil 81 -> 75).

Where.

  • Epic's shared code (commit 784596a): Engine/Src/UnGame.cpp:2681-2715 UGameEngine::LoadMap: the _XBOX block that re-packs every level-static projector into one DT_StaticProjectorBatch actor (AProjector::RebuildStaticPrimsBatch) runs if( RenDevHasXboxProjectors() ); the emptying of static-mesh index buffers that follows stays _XBOX (PC draws static meshes from them). Engine/Inc/UnRenDev.h FRenderCaps::XboxProjectors; Engine/Src/UnRenderResource.cpp:61 RenDevHasXboxProjectors() (returns 1 under _XBOX). RebuildStaticPrimsBatch, RenderStaticPrimsBatch and the batch draw list were never gated.
  • Driver: Src/UC2GLMaterialState.cpp:3444 SetProjectorMultiMaterial: Epic's Xbox body (was #ifdef _XBOX, PC empty); TS_WORLD/TS_WORLDINV = the interface's LocalToWorld (:3594); the 13 VSC_PROJ_* registers to a u_proj[13] uniform (:3738); an Epic debugf with two %s and no arguments fixed (:3688); one log line per projector (:3843). Src/UC2GLD3DXCompat.h: D3DX vector helpers. Src/UC2GLShaders.cpp:193-333: ProjectorAtten.vsh and TexSourceTexKill3.vsh ported register for register; the 12 PS_Projector_* no longer engine-gated. Src/UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp: ProjectorShadersReady/XboxProjectorsActive (:2392-2407), switches (:599-602), console XBOXPROJECTORS (:2069, takes effect at the next map load); ShaderStateKnown: until InitPixelShaders has run the caps report what the switches ask for (the command-line map loads before the GL context exists, so the first map was batched the PC way).
  • Driver defect fixed on the way: Src/UC2GLRenderInterface.cpp:2072-2078: M11 had made DrawPrimitive skip every draw between BeginOcclusionQuery/EndOcclusionQuery; deferred queries wrap an actor's real draw (Projector.uc bUseDeferredHWOcclusionTests), so most batches vanished. Now only passes with colour and depth writes off are skipped.
  • Diagnostics: Src/UC2GLSceneCensus.cpp (console GLSCENECENSUS: every projector and terrain of the level, frustum-plane signs, leaf lists), -glprojnoclip, GLSTATS [RESET].
  • ini: UseXboxProjectors=True (Default.ini:404, both device sections, web too); README row 23.
  • Tools: tools/dev/m7b.sh (171 lines: shadow, projector-*, terrain, twin-match), tools/dev/m7b-census.sh (47 maps), tools/dev/m11-twin.sh (expects 97 fragment shaders and 485 programs since M7b).
  • Commits: 458eebf, a891d63, d979b3b, bec8014, 784596a, f7478bf, afb9914; merged 1b33254.

How the parts fit. The engine (Epic's code, gate lifted) builds the batch actor at map load; the driver draws it with Epic's Xbox projector material: attenuation per vertex in ProjectorAtten.vsh, clipping per pixel by texkill. Dynamic projectors (player shadows, decals, projectors following actors) stay on the PC path: on the Xbox they go through push-buffer renderers whose per-pixel result is the PC path's (about 400 lines not ported for no visible difference). No .uc change.

Why. M6/M7 had left the 12 projector shaders translated but unused (BLOCKERS.md "Open technical questions"). Rule for M7b: implement only where the Xbox path draws something visibly different, found on real content.

Decisions and alternatives. Only level-static projectors batched; dynamic ones not ported. Switching takes effect at the next map load (switching a loaded level was tried and dropped: the static actor leaf lists do not follow a re-attached projector).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Deliberate change of look on six maps (towards the Xbox's formula, which ignores MaterialBlendingOp, base opacity of masked surfaces, and does not darken whole unclipped BSP nodes); no Xbox reference picture.
  • A comment in UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp near the compiled default says "projectors on skeletal meshes were drawn with nothing"; the notebook's pixel trace shows PC's software-skinned branch draws FProjectorRenderInfo's material, so that comment overstates the reason (the code's behaviour is unaffected).
  • BLOCKERS.md still lists the projector shaders and PS_Terrain3Layer as "not drawn yet" (stale since M7b).

Status. Measured: DM-DeepCore, where the formulas coincide, PC path vs Xbox batch mean 0.136/255, largest 4 levels (control 0.002); CTF-Recoil grid area mean 9.0 levels (deliberate). Twin: progtest 485 programs per side, largest difference 1/255. LOG.md 2026-10-08: m7b.sh 11/0.

Off switch. UseXboxProjectors=False, -noxboxprojectors, console XBOXPROJECTORS 0 (next map load).

#R-15 Xbox shadow textures (M7b item 3, XboxShadows)

What the player/owner sees. Player and bot blob shadows are lighter in the core and a little sharper: the floor under the shadow keeps 43% of its light instead of 21%; PC's whole projection square was also 1.6% darker than its surroundings, the Xbox's is not.

Where.

  • Epic's shared code (commit bec8014): Engine/Src/UnShadowProjector.cpp: two _XBOX && NEW_SHADOW_BLEND sites now chosen at run time: the caster colour in FShadowBitmapRenderInterface::SetMaterial (:242-271, caster drawn in ShadowDarkness grey with alpha 255) and the filter in UShadowBitmapMaterial::Get (:611-761: one PS_ShadowBlur tile, four horizontal taps at -1, -1/3, +1/3, +1 texel, weights 1/6, 2/6, 2/6, 1/6, negated, instead of PC's nine canvas tiles). Both bodies unchanged; RI->InsertFence() stays _XBOX; the PC-only 256x256 size override left alone. UnRenDev.h FRenderCaps::XboxShadows, UnRenderResource.cpp:77 RenDevHasXboxShadows().
  • Driver: XboxShadowsActive() (UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:2430): UseXboxShadows and the PS_Blur family drawable (i.e. post-processing on); switches -noxboxshadows/-xboxshadows (:589), console XBOXSHADOWS (:2048). Numeric check Src/UC2GLShadowCheck.cpp (console GLSHADOWCHECK: both render targets of every shadow material read back and the shadow recomputed on the CPU with the formula of the branch the engine took).
  • ini: UseXboxShadows=True (Default.ini:403); README row 23. Tools: tools/dev/m7b.sh shadow, tools/dev/m7b-darker.py (pixel-by-pixel darkness comparison).

How the parts fit. Engine-side branch choice, drawn through the existing PS_Blur handler with BLUR_SHADOW. Without post-processing the capability is off, so -nopostprocess also gives PC shadows.

Why. Reading Epic's code showed a visible difference by arithmetic (projectors.md step 0); M7 had left it because it "would change every shadow and is not post-processing".

Decisions and alternatives. On by default (it differs visibly and is the Xbox behaviour). Tied to post-processing rather than independent.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Changes every player shadow. No Xbox reference (the owner's xemu shots show no shadow that can be made out on those dark floors). The shadow texture is stored RGBA8 where the Xbox used L8.

Status. Measured: GLSHADOWCHECK on DM-DeepCore, DM-Praxis, DM-Azure: PC branch recomputed exactly (0.00/255), Xbox branch 0.56/255; core 55 vs 27, neutral 128 vs 126. Seen: DM-DeepCore pair, mean difference 0.305 (control 0.023).

Off switch. UseXboxShadows=False, -noxboxshadows, console XBOXSHADOWS 0, or -nopostprocess.

#R-16 Three-layer terrain pass, PS_Terrain3Layer (M7b item 2), off by default

What the player/owner sees. Nothing (by default off; when on, no visible difference: largest 2 levels of 255 on DM-Ascension, a few draw calls fewer).

Where. Driver only (the engine's PC branch already reads the caps): GetRenderCaps (UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:2377) reports MaxSimultaneousTerrainLayers = 3 when Terrain3LayerActive() (:2409); SetTerrainMaterial (UC2GLMaterialState.cpp:2945) sets the _XBOX mask constants c0..c2 and draws the combined pass with the program; RefreshTerrainPasses (:2418) re-runs ATerrainInfo::Update on every loaded terrain; switches -terrain3layer/-noterrain3layer (:594-598), console TERRAIN3LAYER (:2080). ini UseTerrain3Layer=False (Default.ini:405). Commits bec8014, 3966bdb.

How the parts fit / Why. The engine builds RM_CombinedWeightMap passes when the caps say 3 (the Xbox hard-codes 3). The difference by arithmetic is one clamp/rounding instead of three; it only shows where a layer saturates under bright vertex light.

Decisions and alternatives. Implemented, measured, left off (the rule: implement where the Xbox looks different; here it does not). Kept as the exact Xbox structure and a draw-call saving.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Untested in game play with the switch on beyond three views; more code paths.

Status. Measured: DM-Ascension mean 0.039, largest 2; CTF-Judgment mean 0.147, largest 21 on 0.034% of pixels; DM-Apex 0.050.

Off switch. It is off (UseTerrain3Layer=False).

#R-17 Movie textures on the GL side (MovieMaterial, M9; "never green", O-1)

What the player/owner sees. Animated movie textures in the menus (the arena page's game-type portraits) and in levels play; a movie that cannot be opened shows its loading fallback or black, never Epic's bright-green default texture. (The movie player, cooking and full-screen movies belong to the video section; only the GL side is here.)

Where.

  • New: Source/UC2GLDrv/Src/UC2GLMovie.cpp (116 lines; commit dbbf486): the PC branch of FOpenGLRenderInterface::SetMovieMaterial (#if !defined(_XBOX)). The movie logic (reset, open on first use, advance once per level time step, OnDonePlaying, LoadingFallback) is the engine's appUpdateMovieMaterial (Engine/Src/UnMovie.cpp, shared by every device); this file uploads the current BGRA frame into the cache entry's GL texture only when the material is drawn and the picture revision changed (kept in the material's transient LastD3DTexture), and sets the Xbox branch's state (SELECTARG1, SRCALPHA/INVSRCALPHA or ONE/INVSRCCOLOR when bAdditive, the mask's alpha on stage 1). No linear-texture matrix (the Xbox's LIN_ textures were addressed in texels).
  • Src/UC2GLMaterialState.cpp:4647-4895: Epic's Xbox body stays under #ifdef _XBOX; the PC stub that returned 0 was removed (the only line range removed from that file).
  • The "green" fix is in the engine, not the driver: Engine/Src/UnMovie.cpp appUpdateMovieMaterial returns a 4x4 opaque black picture (FBlackMoviePicture) or the LoadingFallback while a material has no picture (web-audio.md step 1 (c); the video section owns it).
  • Web: frames are uploaded as GL_BGRA, which WebGL 1 lacks; GL4ES converts to RGBA on the CPU.

How the parts fit. Engine: decoding and timing (UC2Movie player behind Epic's movie interface). Driver: upload and blend state only. Without the engine fix a missing movie drew Engine.DefaultTexture (green); the web build had no movies in its manifest at first, so every movie texture there was green.

Why. Epic's PC driver returned 0 from SetMovieMaterial (default material); M9 scope. Owner's O-1: "videos play green".

Decisions and alternatives. Keep the driver part in its own file so UC2GLMaterialState.cpp stays diffable; upload on draw, not on decode.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. The CPU BGRA-to-RGBA conversion on the web costs time per frame upload (not measured). The menu-movie screenshot check is timing-sensitive and fails on loaded machines (m11-twin.sh known FAIL m9-menu-movie-shots).

Status. Measured (web-renderer.md G5 step 1): ES 2.0 twin with GL4ES forced to convert BGRA (LIBGL_NOBGRA=1, the WebGL case): logos match their cooked frames at 82.4 / 65.8 dB PSNR (a red/blue swap would not), menu movie advancing, 0 GL errors. Native: m9-movies.py 19/0, movie self-test 96-99/0 (LOG.md M9, M11 audio).

Off switch. None in the driver; movies themselves through the [UC2Movie] keys (video section).

#R-18 Anti-aliasing (4x MSAA on desktop, FXAA on GLES/WebGL) and 16x anisotropic filtering (O-8)

What the player/owner sees. Smoother edges and sharper floors at grazing angles by default: desktop GL draws the scene 4x multisampled; the browser (and the GLES twin) applies FXAA in the present pass; textures are filtered with 16x anisotropy wherever the hardware allows.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-18.

Where.

  • Src/UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp: SUPPORTS_MSAA (:359, GL 3.0 or ARB_framebuffer_object); the choice (:764-769: PresentFXAA = AntiAliasing > 0 && (IsGLES || !SUPPORTS_MSAA)); CreateMainFramebuffer (:880-893: multisampled RGBA8 and D24S8 renderbuffers); ResolveMain (:949; glBlitFramebuffer :963) resolves into the existing colour and depth textures only when something reads them and they changed: the back-buffer snapshot and depth-as-colour copy, CopyDepthBuffer and its fallback, the present pass, ReadPixels, render targets that borrow the main depth. GPresentFragmentShader (:1480-1520): "FXAA PC simple" (Timothy Lottes' public reduced form) inside the present pass, uFXAA uniform. Switches -glaa=N, -glnoaa (:607-609), -glshotpresented (screenshot after the present pass). (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-18.)
  • Inc/UC2GLFuncs.h:144-147 the two optional entry points.
  • Anisotropy: Src/UC2GLRenderInterface.cpp:1449: a linear minifying filter on a mipmapped texture gets GL_TEXTURE_MAX_ANISOTROPY_EXT = LevelOfAnisotropy (clamped to the hardware's maximum); point-filtered post-process reads and render targets stay at 1. Before, anisotropy was applied only to an explicit ANISOTROPIC filter, i.e. never (D3DDrv made every stage's default minifying filter anisotropic when LevelOfAnisotropy > 1, D3DRenderState.cpp).
  • Src/UC2GLSelfTest.cpp:1128-1132: the self-test refuses to run with AA on (it compares exactly).
  • ini: LevelOfAnisotropy=16, AntiAliasing=4 (Default.ini:390-391, both device sections, web too; compiled defaults the same, UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:227-228); README row 14.
  • Tools: tools/dev/run-uc2-shot.sh adds -glnoaa to every run unless UC2_AA=1; m10-controls.sh runs with -glnoaa (its key timings predate AA; with 4x MSAA on software GL menu steps came late).
  • Commits: 6921897, 21cbe5f, 6e542ac, 518b5f5, bce3377.

How the parts fit. Driver-only; the engine is unaware. On WebGL the context's own antialias attribute is off because the scene is drawn into the driver's framebuffer, not the canvas.

Why. Owner decision O-8 (PLAN.md): "anisotropic filtering 16x; anti-aliasing 4x (multisampled main framebuffer; WebGL 1 has no multisampled framebuffer objects: WebGL 2 or a post-process AA on the web, decided with evidence)".

Decisions and alternatives. Desktop: MSAA with resolve-on-read. WebGL 1: the context antialias attribute rejected (it would multisample only the canvas, which receives one full-screen quad); WebGL 2 rejected for now (GL4ES emits ESSL 1.00 only; WebGL 2 gives ESSL 1.00 shaders no EXT_frag_depth and rejects GL4ES' unsized depth formats: it would move the web renderer to an untested context class); FXAA in the existing present pass chosen (no extra pass or target).

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-18.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • FXAA also smooths the HUD and menus (drawn into the same frame); 9 texture reads per pixel in the present pass.
  • MSAA costs memory and resolve blits per read (3-4 per frame with post-processing); on software GL it slowed menus enough to break timing-based tests.
  • A player's saved Avaris.ini (or the browser's saved settings in IndexedDB) from before O-8 keeps its LevelOfAnisotropy=1 until the saved settings are cleared (the engine derives the user ini from Default.ini once); a missing AntiAliasing key gets the compiled default 4.
  • Desktop and web anti-aliasing differ in method and look.

Status. Measured: desktop MSAA vs off mean 1.13 (0.99% of pixels above 32 levels: the edges); twin FXAA vs off 1.06 (1.19%); anisotropy before/after mean 2.33 (logs/m11/aniso-crops.png); 0 GL errors and 0 Mesa errors. Browser: anisotropy 1 of 16 was reported with the old ini; with the new default the extension is used (not re-logged in the notebook).

Off switch. AntiAliasing=0 or -glnoaa; -glaa=N; LevelOfAnisotropy=1.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-18.

#R-19 The GL4ES binding (UC2GL4ESDrv) and the native GLES "twin" (M11)

What the player/owner sees. On Linux, -gl4es runs the game through the same code path the browser uses (an OpenGL ES 2 context behind GL4ES). In the browser this is the renderer.

Where.

  • New package Source/UC2GL4ESDrv (3 files, 329 lines): UUC2GL4ESRenderDevice, a subclass of the desktop device that overrides only the Binding* virtuals (UC2GL4ESDrv.cpp: context attributes :70, context creation :86, drawable size :164, swap interval :180, swap :187, GL4ES init :210 with the LIBGL_* environment of the engine-ut2004 template plus LIBGL_NOPSA=1, proc lookups :250-262). It is the only code that links GL4ES; GL4ES' headers are deliberately not on the include path. CMakeLists.txt: skipped where GL4ES is not fetched (Windows).
  • Driver changes for the run-time binding (commits aad99a0, 5122c93, 6c6c049, c111785, 0d7cf34, 209d8ad, 74e34d0, a4975f2, c66d275, f5fbcae, 89204c7):
    • Inc/UC2GLBinding.h rewritten (the binding is a property of the device object); Inc/UC2GLRawFuncs.h: a second, "raw" table of GLES calls looked up under GL4ES;
    • Src/UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp: Binding* desktop implementations (:396-470), ShaderPrologue (:479; #version 120 or #version 100 + precision, UC2_HIGHP for depth samplers), FindRawExt (:380), DetectCapabilities (:1415, the extension flags and the mask switches), depth-stencil storage and attachment around GL4ES (:1027-1215: GL4ES would give WebGL 1 a 16-bit depth + separate stencil, which WebGL rejects; one #ifdef __EMSCRIPTEN__ for DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT), engine texture storage around GL4ES (TexImage2D :1258, CompressedTexImage2D :1216: GL4ES would regenerate every mip chain with a box filter and always decode DXT in software), NewBuffer/FreeBuffer (:1306-1330: on GLES buffer names are reused, never deleted, because GL4ES caches attribute bindings by name), PrepareGL4ESTextureZero (:1380: a raw 1x1 black texture under GL4ES' "texture 0"; WebGL has no default texture, GL4ES set its parameters with null bound (GL_INVALID_OPERATION) and called glGenerateMipmap on whatever DXT texture was active);
    • Src/UC2GLResource.cpp: mip chains completed by the driver on GLES (CompleteChain :320, CompleteCompressedChain :473: no GL_TEXTURE_MAX_LEVEL in GLES 2), D3DCOLOR reordered to RGBA on GLES, targets smaller than the main framebuffer get no shared depth on GLES (WebGL: incomplete dimensions);
    • Src/UC2GLRenderInterface.cpp: wireframe drawn as line lists on GLES (DrawWireframe :2195; GL4ES' wireframe path crashed with real buffer objects; "written, never drawn by content"), query boxes not drawn;
    • shaders: no float literal with more than six significant digits (GL4ES reprints literals: 16777215.0 became 16777200), highp depth samplers (lowp default computed 11 bits of depth).
  • CheckGLError also drains the raw library's glGetError (GL4ES answered from its own record only, so the twin's first "0 GL errors" meant nothing); -glerrorsdraw checks after every draw.
  • Build: root CMakeLists.txt:356-399 fetches https://github.com/10-p/gl4es.git at branch fix/cubemap-bind-active-unit (static, GLES2, no X11/EGL, -O2 forced); Source/SDLLaunch/CMakeLists.txt:26-28, SDLLaunch.cpp:41-42, 306, 518-519 (-gl4es).
  • ini: [UC2GL4ESDrv.UC2GL4ESRenderDevice] in Default.ini:410-440 (same values as the desktop section).
  • Tools: tools/dev/m11-twin.sh (255 lines: shaders, gputest, boot, selftest, progtest, pinned, effects, m6, fallbacks, movies, each desktop against twin; M11_SERIAL=1 one binding at a time; the twin runs an ES 2.0 context with MESA_GLES_VERSION_OVERRIDE=2.0 and MESA_DEBUG=1), tools/dev/m11-progcmp.py, tools/dev/imgdiff.py.

How the parts fit. One object code for both bindings: everything else asks IsGLES and the Cap* flags at run time, so a renderer fix reaches the web by itself. Through GL4ES go all fixed-function state (its emulation is why GL4ES is there), all GLSL programs (already ES 1.00 subset, GL4ES swaps the header), buffers, colour attachments; around it go depth-stencil storage/attachment and engine texture storage.

Why. PLAN.md M11 ("UC2GL4ESDrv on WebGL"); the owner's decision to reuse engine-ut2004's GL4ES approach; the twin exists so the renderer tests run on the web code path without a browser (the memory incident of 2026-10-04 made browser runs expensive and rare).

Decisions and alternatives. Run-time binding by subclass instead of compiling the driver twice or mirroring directories (the template's way); WebGL 1 instead of WebGL 2 (GL4ES emits ESSL 1.00 only); depth-stencil and texture storage around GL4ES (each forced by a measured GL4ES behaviour).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Depends on a fork branch of GL4ES (10-p/gl4es, branch fix/cubemap-bind-active-unit, unpinned): an upstream change can break the build or behaviour.
  • Calling GLES behind GL4ES' back is fragile: GL4ES caches state; the rules that keep it consistent are in comments only.
  • No texture LOD bias on GLES (GL4ES stores and never applies it): the desktop uses DefaultTexMipBias=-0.5, so far textures are slightly softer on the web (pinned DM-Deadbolt desktop vs twin mean 1.415; 0.374 without the bias). Open.
  • The DM-Azure fogged sky zone is hazier on desktop than on the twin (fog share 0.88-0.91 vs 0.54-0.57; the fog formula itself is equal on both, self-test fog); open, cause suspected in GL4ES' fixed-function emulation.
  • A small unexplained residue (mean 0.38/255, nothing above 32 levels) on DM-Deadbolt desktop vs twin.
  • Mesa answers an ES 2.0 request with ES 3.2 unless overridden: ES-2-only violations are caught only by the override or the browser.
  • GL4ES draws one pixel for GL_POINTS with a user program (the engine's point lists would be wrong; none seen).
  • GL4ES keeps a CPU copy of every buffer: vertex/index data exists two or three times in memory.

Status. Measured (LOG.md M11; web-renderer.md gate): m11-twin.sh 31 PASS, 3 FAIL on main (the depth-texture fallback x2, menu-movie timing); self-test 11/11 then 15/15 on both bindings with the desktop's tolerances; program test 420 (now 485) complete programs desktop vs twin, largest difference 1/255; effect pairs within the noise floor (liandri random). Every GL4ES defect listed was found by these tests.

Off switch. Native: do not pass -gl4es (the desktop device is the default). Web: none (the only renderer).

#R-20 The web renderer: WebGL 1 context, fallbacks, frame presentation (M11)

What the player/owner sees. The game draws in the browser at the canvas's device-pixel size (4K full screen works), with post-processing, effects, movies; in the harness it runs on the real GPU (ANGLE/Vulkan on the Quadro P620).

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-20.

Where.

  • Source/UC2GL4ESDrv/Src/UC2GL4ESDrv.cpp (__EMSCRIPTEN__): the WebGL 1 context is created by the device on the engine's worker (emscripten_webgl_create_context("#canvas"), :118-147: majorVersion 1, alpha/depth/ stencil/antialias 0, explicit swap control, renderViaOffscreenBackBuffer); SDL keeps the window and input (Emscripten issue 24792: SDL's EGL path is proxied to the main thread, which no longer owns the canvas); the device draws at the canvas's size in device pixels whatever the ini says (:89-103, commit c66d275); present = emscripten_webgl_commit_frame() (:187-191); swap interval ignored on the web (:180-185).

  • Source/SDLLaunch/cmake/Web.cmake:88-104: -sGL_ENABLE_GET_PROC_ADDRESS=1, -sMIN_WEBGL_VERSION=1 -sMAX_WEBGL_VERSION=1 (no WebGL 2), -sFULL_ES2=1, -sOFFSCREEN_FRAMEBUFFER (without it commit_frame is a stub and presentation depends on the compositor: about 1 frame/s on Windows in the sibling ports). Commit 34b4ed2. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-20.)

  • Frame pacing (launcher, not the driver; Source/SDLLaunch/Src/SDLLaunchWeb.cpp:25-42, M4w 45f7301): one Asyncify yield per frame through setTimeout(0); a standalone client's max tick rate is 0, so there is no frame cap and no requestAnimationFrame pacing: the browser composites whatever was committed.

  • Source/SDLLaunch/Src/SDLLaunchWeb.cpp ue_exec_command passes what the engine does not take to the first viewport, so device console commands (GLPROBE, POSTPROCESS ...) work from the page (a860ac8).

  • Fallbacks (DetectCapabilities, masks for testing on any binding):

    Capability (WebGL 1 extension) Without it Status
    S3TC (WEBGL_compressed_texture_s3tc; -glnos3tc) the driver decodes DXT1/3/5 itself (UC2GL_DecodeDXT, UC2GLResource.cpp:139) implemented; it first crashed on every level (BSP lightmaps have no UTexture), fixed 89204c7; before that any phone without S3TC would have crashed at the first level; measured mean 0.019 vs S3TC
    GL_TEXTURE_MAX_LEVEL (none in GLES 2; -glnomaxlevel on desktop) chains completed by the driver implemented, always on GLES; DXT completion fixed for non-square levels (a4975f2, a WebGL INVALID_VALUE)
    EXT_frag_depth (-glnofragdepth) the accumulation target's depth is cleared and every program pass into it tests the scene depth in the shader (PSV_SceneDepthTest: generated main() renamed, discard) implemented (f5fbcae), self-test PASS (3 units of 2^24); a picture with distortion under the mask still pending
    OES_element_index_uint (-glnouintindex) 32-bit indices narrowed to 16 bits on upload implemented, self-test index-narrow; never reached by content (the engine makes no 32-bit index buffers)
    WEBGL_depth_texture (-glnodepthtexture) depth of field off (picture stays sharp, log line) and distortion drawn over nearer geometry NOT implemented (needs a second draw of every depth-writing primitive; about doubles draw calls on exactly the weak devices); every desktop ANGLE back end has the extension
    fragment highp not handled (the depth encode needs it); the log says so open

    Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-20.

  • ini: Engine/Config/web/Default.ini:19 RenderDevice=UC2GL4ESDrv.UC2GL4ESRenderDevice; otherwise the render sections are identical to native (generated by tools/dev/sync-web-ini.py, which keeps only listed web deltas: render device, UseJoystick=False, network device, and the web-owned audio/movie sections).

  • Frontend (ufrontend uc2 branch; detail in the frontend section): packages/e2e/uc2-wasm-boot.spec.js (menu, DM-Deadbolt with post-processing, a play test with real input; compares the canvas with the twin's reference pictures, tolerances 10 and 3), commits 7b947ff, 9bfac7c, 635450e, 125679e.

How the parts fit. Same driver as R-19; the only web-specific code is context creation/presentation in the GL4ES subclass, one #ifdef for the depth-stencil attachment point, and link flags. Owner-visible defaults equal native except AA (FXAA instead of MSAA, R-18).

Why. PLAN.md M11 "Web product"; owner (2026-10-06): the web build renders at the canvas size and maps play.

Decisions and alternatives. WebGL 1 only (R-18, R-19). Context on the worker (template's approach). Depth-texture fallback deferred with a written reason (web-renderer.md "Fallbacks").

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-20.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Tested in one browser/GPU combination only (headless Chromium, ANGLE on Vulkan, NVIDIA): every extension present, so no fallback was active in a real browser; the fallbacks are tested by masks on the twin and desktop.
  • No frame cap or rAF pacing: frame rate is whatever the worker achieves; power use and tearing behaviour in real browsers not examined.
  • Per frame in the browser (DM-Deadbolt, post-processing on, run 12 on a quiet machine): 26-30 ms, ~98 draws, 34,399 primitives, 93 material state applications (the engine hands a new pool object per draw, so the driver re-applies state almost every draw; content-based skipping not done), 3 back-buffer snapshots + 1 depth-as-colour per frame (Epic's blur reads, writes, reads the back buffer: not waste on GL).
  • Pointer lock was not granted in the headless page (frontend's business).

Status. Measured: browser spec 3 passed (runs 20, 23, 24, 25), 0 GL errors in every test; menu vs twin 6.36 (tolerance 10), DM-Deadbolt vs twin 1.12 (tolerance 3); browser PSS peak 1.4-1.7 GB, wasm memory 256 MiB. LOG.md 2026-10-08: browser boot 4/4 after M7b/O-10. Not verified: the DM-Azure pool cube-map fix (R-12) on the twin or in the browser; any phone or Safari/Firefox.

Off switch. None for the web renderer itself; masks (-glnos3tc etc.) are test switches.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-20.

What the player/owner sees. Nothing unless a switch or console command is used. They exist because there is no Xbox to compare with: most renderer evidence in this report comes from them.

  • In the shipping driver (all in Source/UC2GLDrv):

Outside the repo: <WorkUC2>/bin/oracle-shot.sh (Epic's avaris.exe under wine: the PC reference pictures).

Why / Decisions. No Xbox oracle (xemu exists but is slow, "last resort"); numbers over pictures where possible; every test runs inside a memory and time cap after the 2026-10-04 incident.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • About 2,500 lines of test/diagnostic code ship inside the driver (self-test, combiner test, pixel trace, censuses, shadow check) and dozens of switches; harmless when unused, but maintenance weight and attack surface for console commands.
  • Several picture checks are timing-sensitive and fail on a loaded machine (menu-movie shots, liandri noise, blur phase, the controls tests with AA); LOG.md records them as known FAILs rather than fixed tests.
  • run-uc2-shot.sh/fx-owner.sh reference pictures now run with the pre-D-2 UI and AutoFOV off and without AA: the pictures do not show what the player sees by default.

Status. These are the instruments; their results are in each feature's Status.

Off switch. Not needed (off unless invoked).

"Compiled" = value when the key is absent (UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:201-248). Rows are in Engine/Config/README.md (lines 12-24, 72).

#Open issues in this area

  1. No Xbox reference for most of the look. Glow and bloom strength, dynamic gamma on dark maps, distortion size (height/480 scaling), the Xbox shadow filter, the projector change on six maps, the berserk overlay on pawn attachments: all "seen only" or numerically checked against Epic's code, never against an Xbox picture. xemu exists (<WorkUC2>/xbox-emulation/run-uc2-iso.sh) but is slow; a few targeted xemu shots would settle the biggest ones (glow strength, a shadow, CTF-Recoil's grid).
  2. WebGL 1 without WEBGL_depth_texture: fallback not implemented (depth of field off, distortion drawn over nearer geometry); a device without fragment highp is not handled. m11-twin.sh reports both as known FAILs.
  3. Web vs desktop differences left open: no texture LOD bias on GLES (far textures softer than desktop's -0.5 bias); DM-Azure's fogged sky zone hazier on desktop than through GL4ES (cause not found); a 0.38/255 residue on DM-Deadbolt; the DM-Azure pool fix (R-12) not checked on the twin or in a browser.
  4. O-6 (DM-Azure waterfall in first person) not reproduced; needs the owner's exact spot, window size and view history (BLOCKERS.md). The "waterfall over the character" is Epic's half-resolution depth mask; not changed.
  5. POSTPROCESS 0 while running leaves distortion materials on a wrong fallback (DM-Azure's cobra waterfall becomes an opaque violet stream); starting with -nopostprocess was not checked.
  6. DOFShippedBlurAlpha: ini True, compiled default 0. An ini without the key gets the non-shipped alpha.
  7. Dynamic gamma is forced on at every session start (the device sets UClient::DynamicGamma = 1); a user can only turn it off per session (DYNAMICGAMMA 0).
  8. Performance not characterised on a GPU per effect. Software GL: post-processing 3.3-3.8x frame time. Known waste: material state re-applied on ~95% of draws (the engine hands a new pool object per draw), program path binds program and uploads uniforms per draw, 3-4 full-screen snapshots per frame, MSAA resolves, GL4ES' CPU copies of buffers and BGRA conversion of movie frames on the web.
  9. Never run: AMD or Intel GPUs, real Windows (wine only), phones, Safari/Firefox, split-screen, cinematics with focus depth of field, the sniper overlay; 12 of the 25 post-process shaders never drawn by content (additive and subtractive distortion, sniper colour remap, player-cam noise variants, the motion-blur mask, ...; five of them unreachable by design).
  10. Timing-sensitive checks recorded as known FAILs rather than fixed: Liandri overlay noise (needs fixed random numbers or a mask), menu-movie screenshots, blur phase between bindings.
  11. External dependency: GL4ES from the 10-p/gl4es fork, branch fix/cubemap-bind-active-unit, not pinned to a commit.
  12. Stale text to clean up: BLOCKERS.md "Open technical questions" still says the projector shaders and PS_Terrain3Layer are not drawn (done by M7b); the UnRenDev.h comment lists USubActionDepthOfField.cpp among the gate users (that file has no gate); the comment on UseXboxProjectors's compiled default says projectors on skeletal meshes "were drawn with nothing" (the notebook's pixel trace shows PC draws them). (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 20, open issue 12.)
  13. Smaller deviations kept on purpose: occlusion queries always "visible" (no occlusion culling); NV2A 8-bit combiner precision not modelled (no banding); DistortionCullDistance read only from the UC2GLDrv section; the emissive streak falloff is per frame (frame-rate dependent, as Epic's); no frame cap or requestAnimationFrame pacing on the web. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, R-11 and R-20.)

Our own engine-side test file Engine/Src/UC2NetTest.cpp (NETPLACE, used by the renderer checks) is not Epic's code.

#30. Input, UI, scripts, profiles and saves

Code as of main 4813dd9 (snapshot /home/ion/WorkUC2/engine-uc2-report), baseline 7633696 (Epic's tree as imported). Frontend: ufrontend branch uc2 at 5654da2 against 4affbe7. path:line are snapshot line numbers. Notebooks behind this section: docs/porting/analysis/ client-boot.md (M4), controls.md (M10), scripts.md (M14), ui-scale.md (D-2), profiles.md (D-3/D-4/D-5), mouse.md (D-6), console-fov.md (D-7/D-8), saveconfig.md, rebind.md (D-9), web-ini-saves.md (C-2).

#Summary: the five things to know

  1. Input is ours from SDL up, Epic's from UEngine::InputEvent on. Source/SDL2Drv (new, C++ only, no .u) replaces WinDrv/XboxDrv: keyboard by scancode, relative mouse, text through SDL_TEXTINPUT, and game pads through SDL's GameController API reproducing the Xbox driver (four ports, hot-plug, 30/255 threshold, repeat, rumble). Everything after that (UInput bindings, UCPlayerInput, the GUI) is Epic's code with targeted fixes. Pads are off on the web (UseJoystick=False) and no physical pad has ever been tried: every pad result is from SDL's virtual controller.
  2. Three script packages are rebuilt from source and ship in the asset tree instead of Epic's: GUI.u (D-4 player name), UCGameplay.u (D-6 mouse look, saveconfig's persistent mouse commands) and UCMenu.u (D-9 KEYBOARD / MOUSE page). Nine .uc files differ from Epic's; seven of them are this area's. Everything else in this section is native C++ or ini data and works with the shipped packages. The mouse look is the one feature that breaks if a package is missing: the D-6 DefUser.ini binds the mouse to aTurn/aLookUp, which the shipped UCPlayerInput overwrites, so with the shipped UCGameplay.u the mouse does not turn the view at all (the engine logs a warning saying so). The table below lists every feature against every layer. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.5 and 30.18.)
  3. The mouse is a PC mouse now, the pad is the Xbox pad. Mouse: linear, 0.022 deg x MouseLookSensitivity (2.5) per count, no clamp/curve/acceleration/friction/adhesion, own invert, rigid third-person camera while the mouse owns the look. Pad: Epic's Xbox curve, acceleration, dead zone 0.3, friction, adhesion, auto-centre (within 0.5% of the shipped scripts). Measured by script, never felt by a person; the rigid camera turned out to be the cause of the owner's "character jitter" report (O-11), fixed after this snapshot (section 50).
  4. The UI is drawn in a centred 4:3 box (UIScaleMode=Fit43, default): one native change in UCanvas::Update() hands every script and the GUI a 640x480 design space; the world (and, with D-8 AutoFOV, a Hor+ field of view) fills the window. No script change.
  5. Settings now persist the PC way: the game starts in the last profile (User.ini [Profiles] LastProfile, engine code), the profile owns the name, keyboard bindings and mouse settings live in User.ini and are written key by key (SAVECONFIGKEYS, behind [Engine.Engine] ScriptSaveConfig, which turns Epic's compiled-out script SaveConfig() back on), on the web the inis and a flat System/Profiles/<name>.PRO are the player's files.

#Which layer carries what (read this before reviewing the .u switch)

"Shipped .u" = what the player gets with our engine and inis but Epic's shipped script packages (the state before asset tree v2). Every feature not listed with a script part works with the shipped packages.

Feature Native (C++) Script (.uc, package) Ini / data Frontend With the shipped .u
Keyboard, mouse buttons, text (M4) SDL2Drv none [SDL2Drv.SDL2Client] none full
Pads (M10) SDL2Drv SDL2Pads.cpp, UnIn.cpp stick fix none [SDL2Drv.PadMap], Pad* keys, Joy* bindings none (pads off on web) full, but the left stick does not walk on PC (measured 0 units; M10 hid it with SpeedBase=2400000, D-6 removed that)
Menus by keyboard/mouse (M10) UnGUICore.cpp none [GUI.EmulatedJoypad], bHideMouseCursor none full
Mouse look separated (D-6) UC2InputMigrate.cpp (migration, warning), SDL2Drv UCPlayerInput, UCPlayerController, UCTrainingController in UCGameplay.u DefUser.ini mouse on aTurn/aLookUp, new [UCGameplay.UCPlayerInput] keys boot spec test no mouse look at all (warning logged); pad as Epic's
Keys as a stick, lock-on by mouse, pad-only adhesion/auto-centre (D-6) none UCGameplay.u bKeysAsStick, MouseLockBreakAngle none absent (Epic's behaviour)
UI box (D-2) UnCanvas.cpp, GUI, controller, interaction master none UIScaleMode, UIMenuBackground, UISharpText play test maps clicks into the box full

Profile names the player (D-4) UnGame.cpp LoadMap / MatchViewportsToActors GUIController.SetProfilePlayerName in GUI.u [DefaultPlayer] Name playerName.ownedBy with a profile: correct; without one the character's name ("Anubis") replaces the launcher's

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.5, 30.7, 30.8.

Package couplings that are not obvious:

  • UCGameplay.u and DefUser.ini must change together (D-6). A kept User.ini from an earlier build is rewritten at start-up by UC2InputMigrate.cpp only where a value is exactly an old default. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.7.)
  • UCMenu.u (D-9) refers to UCPlayerInput.MouseLookSensitivity/bInvertMouseLook (UC2Settings_Keys.uc:546-580), which exist only in the rebuilt UCGameplay.u. The rebuilt UCMenu.u with the shipped UCGameplay.u was never tried; expect unresolved imports at load. Treat the three rebuilt packages as one set.
  • UCGameplay.u is a server package (ServerPackages=UCGameplay): every peer of a network game must run the same file; GUI and UCMenu are client-only.

#Features

#30.1 SDL2Drv: keyboard, mouse and typed text (M4)

What the player/owner sees. Keys, mouse buttons, the wheel and typed characters reach the game on Linux, Windows and in the browser through one SDL2 driver; Alt+Enter toggles fullscreen; the mouse is captured (hidden, relative) in a match and free in a windowed menu.

Where.

  • New code (no Epic file changed for this feature): Source/SDL2Drv/ (Inc/SDL2Drv.h, Src/SDL2Client.cpp, Src/SDL2Viewport.cpp, Src/SDL2Drv.cpp), classes SDL2Drv.SDL2Client/SDL2Viewport, C++ only like WinDrv. Commits 49189ba SDL2Drv: SDL2 client and viewport (keyboard, mouse, text; pad hook for M10), 1700520 (OpenGL window/context for GL devices).
    • SDL2Viewport.cpp:41- InitKeyMap: SDL scancode (physical key) -> the Windows virtual-key code the PC build got (EInputKey); both Shift/Ctrl/Alt -> IK_Shift/IK_Ctrl/IK_Alt (UC2's bindings and GUI test those); SDL_SCANCODE_GRAVE -> IK_Tilde (:62, the console key of D-7).
    • SDL2Viewport.cpp:791-933 HandleEvent (the WndProc): key down/up -> InputEvent(IST_Press/IST_Release) with auto-repeat as further presses (:829-861); Backspace/Tab/Escape also as characters, never Enter, as WM_CHAR does (:843-845); SDL_TEXTINPUT UTF-8 decoded, only code points 32..255 sent as Engine->Key (:862-872); SDL_MOUSEMOTION -> IK_MouseX = +xrel*MouseXMultiplier, IK_MouseY = -yrel*MouseYMultiplier as DirectInput did, absolute position kept only while not captured (:873-888); buttons 1..5 (:889-915); wheel -> IK_MouseW 120 per notch plus IK_MouseWheelUp/Down press+release (:916-930).
    • SDL2Viewport.cpp:938-969 focus change: re-capture, ResetInput of GUI and UInput, gamma. :975-1000 SetMouseCapture = SDL_SetRelativeMouseMode + SDL_SetWindowGrab. :1005-1018 UpdateMousePosition (the GUI's absolute cursor in a window, SDL_GetMouseState). :1025-1066 UpdateInput: pumps events, then releases every key the engine holds that SDL says is up (Epic's GetKeyState resync).
    • SDL2Client.cpp:103-140 Init: SDL video, pads, the test channel, SDL_StartTextInput(); :70-93 compiled defaults (the WinDrv section's values, so Epic's own Default.ini still gives a usable window).
  • Ini: Default.ini:147- [SDL2Drv.SDL2Client] (a copy of [WinDrv.WindowsClient]; CaptureMouse=True :158); ViewportManager=SDL2Drv.SDL2Client in [Engine.Engine]. MouseXMultiplier/MouseYMultiplier are not in the ini (compiled 1.0). Web inis identical for these keys.
  • Web: the same driver through Emscripten's SDL2 port. Pointer lock is the frontend's existing shared packages/frontend/src/shell/rawPointerLock.ts (in the base 4affbe7, not ours): raw deltas where the browser allows unadjustedMovement (Windows, macOS), the desktop's accelerated deltas on Linux browsers.
  • Tools: every scenario of tools/dev/m10-controls.sh drives this path with real X events (XTEST, from tools/dev/m10-inject.py).

How the parts fit. Native only: SDL event -> USDL2Viewport::HandleEvent -> UEngine::InputEvent/Key -> UInteractionMaster (console first, then the GUI controller, then the viewport's interactions) -> UInput bindings -> script. No script or ini part is needed; without [SDL2Drv.SDL2Client] the compiled defaults apply.

Why. UC2's PC tree has only WinDrv (Windows) and an SDL 1.2 Linux driver that predates UC2's UClient interface (client-boot.md step 4); engine-ut2004's SDL2 driver has UT2004's signatures, so it was a source of SDL calls and the scancode table, not a drop-in.

Decisions and alternatives. Keep WinDrv's contract exactly (one code for both Shift keys, WM_CHAR semantics, relative mouse at all times, absolute cursor read on demand) so that Epic's GUI and bindings see what they saw on Windows; rejected: engine-ut2004's left/right modifier codes (UC2's bindings use IK_Shift).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Typing is Latin-1 only (TCHAR is ANSI by rule); characters above U+00FF are dropped silently.
  • Ctrl+letter does not produce the control characters WM_CHAR gives on Windows (client-boot.md); no IME.
  • No SDL mouse hint is set (grep SDL_SetHint finds none): whether the desktop's pointer acceleration reaches native mouse look is SDL's backend choice; not measured natively. In a Linux browser it does apply (frontend note above), so the browser's mouse feel depends on the desktop's mouse settings.
  • Fullscreen tested only under Xvfb.

Status. Measured: M4 gate (XTEST keys reached the GUI, LOG.md 2026-10-04); m10-controls.sh keyboard 16 checks and mouse 18 checks PASS on every later gate (latest logs/d9/final-m10.txt, coordinator's logs/coord/a12); browser uc2-wasm-boot.spec.js drives real keyboard and mouse (4/4 in the D-9 and E-2 gates). A real Windows desktop (not wine) and a real browser on the owner's desktop: not verified by us.

Off switch. None (it is the client). MouseXMultiplier/MouseYMultiplier in [SDL2Drv.SDL2Client] scale raw mouse counts.

#30.2 Game pads through SDL's GameController API (M10)

What the player/owner sees. An XInput/PlayStation-style pad drives menus and matches as the Xbox pad did: hot-plug (unplugging pauses with the "controller removed" box), analog triggers with the Xbox threshold, held buttons repeating, rumble from weapons and hits. Natively only; the web build has pads switched off.

Where.

  • New code: Source/SDL2Drv/Src/SDL2Pads.cpp (602 lines; commit e254487 SDL2Drv: game pads through SDL's GameController API, after XboxDrv; test channel). The specification is Epic's Xbox driver (header :1-20 cites XboxViewport.cpp/XboxClient.cpp line by line):
    • :43 DefaultPadMap and :90-126 LoadPadMap: SDL control -> Xbox control (Joy1..16, JoyX/Y/U/V, optional Invert); compiled default = the ini table, used for any control the section does not name.
    • :138-165 InitPads (UseJoystick, SDL_INIT_GAMECONTROLLER, -inputlog), :230-311 PadAdded/PadRemoved (first free of four ports; IK_JoySlider1/2 press+release to the pad's viewport or the interaction master; on removal the held buttons are released first: a deliberate deviation from the Xbox, which left them down until a reset).
