    • :312-413 ReadPad, SendButtonEdges: 16 buttons scaled to 0..255, pressed above PadButtonThreshold (30 = XINPUT_GAMEPAD_MAX_CROSSTALK), repeat as IST_Hold after 0.2 s then every 0.1 s.
    • :414-456 UpdateBoundPad (called from SDL2Viewport.cpp:1037-1038 only from the player tick, where DeltaSeconds>0): Engine->ActiveControllerId = pad, buttons through InputEvent, sticks through MasterProcessJoyInput (GUI) then Input->DirectAxis. :457-481 unbound pads -> interaction master only.
    • :482-516 rumble: WaveformManager->GetRumbleState -> SDL_GameControllerRumble(low=left, high=right, 1000 ms), sent on change, refreshed while non-zero; :588-600 zeroed on travel (SetClientTravel).
    • :530-560 console SETDEADZONE (Epic's, stores JoyDeadZone, which nothing reads) and PADS.
    • :570-586 GetAnalogButtonAmount: the bound pad's analog value, or 255 while a keyboard/mouse key bound to the same command is held. Epic's UPlayer returned 255 always on PC, so UCPlayerInput's timed input sequences saw triggers/A/X held permanently (Epic's PC defect, fixed here in our driver).
  • Epic's code changed: Source/Engine/Src/UnIn.cpp:316-325 (UInput::Exec, the AXIS ... SPEEDBASE= branch; commit de94c55 Engine: stick axes mean on PC what they mean on the Xbox). Bug fix, non-Xbox only. UInput::ReadInput scales every axis by 20/DeltaSeconds on PC (1 on the Xbox); right for mouse/key deltas, wrong for a stick, which is a position: the stick was 20/dt times its Xbox strength (1,200x at 60 fps) and frame-rate dependent. Now an IST_Hold stick contribution is pre-divided by 20/dt. No keyboard or mouse binding uses SpeedBase, so their numbers are unchanged (controls.md step 8 shows the arithmetic).
  • Ini: Default.ini:157 UseJoystick=True (WinDrv default False), :188-197 PadButtonThreshold=30, PadStickDeadZone=0, PadRepeatDelay=0.2, PadRepeatRate=0.1, PadRumble=True, PadRumbleScale=1, JoyDeadZone=0.35, LogInput=False; :202-221 new section [SDL2Drv.PadMap] (a=Joy1 ... rightshoulder=Joy5 (Black), leftshoulder=Joy6 (White), lefty=JoyY Invert, righty=JoyV Invert). Black = right shoulder, White = left is a convention (the script names Black "Next"; xemu maps them the same way), not an Xbox fact. Bindings: DefUser.ini:178-202 Joy1..Joy16, JoyX/Y/U/V, JoySlider1/2 = KeyBinds.ini Layout_0 (30.6). Web: UseJoystick=False (Engine/Config/web/Default.ini, put there by tools/dev/sync-web-ini.py:22, "game pads through the browser's Gamepad API are untested").
  • Tools: tools/dev/m10-controls.sh pad (33 checks) and both (14 checks) with SDL's virtual controller.

How the parts fit. The native side turns SDL controller state into the exact key events the Xbox driver produced; the ini decides which SDL control is which Xbox button and how sticks are bound; the profile (script, unchanged) then applies its controller layout by rewriting the Joy* bindings. No script change. Without [SDL2Drv.PadMap] the compiled table applies. Without the UnIn.cpp fix the right stick saturates at any deflection and turns at a frame-rate dependent speed.

Why. The owner wants the pad as on the Xbox (PLAN.md D-6 "the pad keeps its Xbox response curve"); WinDrv's DirectInput joystick (off by default) has none of the Xbox behaviour.

Decisions and alternatives. Reproduce XboxDrv rather than port WinDrv's joystick; keep the dead zone in the bindings (DeadZone=0.3) as the Xbox did (PadStickDeadZone=0); fix the stick scaling in UInput (one branch, non-Xbox) instead of retuning every value as UT2004 did ("amb" fix). M10 first compensated the left stick with JoyX/JoyY SpeedBase=2400000; D-6 replaced that by a script fix (30.5) because a profile's "swap sticks" rewrites SpeedBase=100.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • No physical pad has ever been connected (BLOCKERS.md "M10, pads"): SDL's own mapping of real devices, real rumble motors, [SDL2Drv.PadMap] overrides, PadStickDeadZone>0, PadRumble=False, rumble zeroed on travel, key repeat assertions and GetAnalogButtonAmount were never exercised (controls.md "Implemented, never exercised").
  • No split screen: Start on a second, unbound pad does not join (SDL2Pads.cpp:16-20; E-1, owner decision).
  • Pads off on the web.
  • The pad's slow turns lose up to one rotation unit per frame (Epic's integer rotation): just past the dead zone nothing turns below ~0.335 deflection at 60 fps, ~0.352 at 144 fps, where the Xbox at 30 fps turned from ~0.32 (mouse.md "Not done"). Fixing it changes Epic's pad arithmetic: owner decision, not done.

Status. Measured with the virtual pad: m10-controls.sh pad 33 PASS, both 14 PASS (every gate since M10; latest D-9 logs/d9/final-m10.txt, coordinator logs/coord/a12); trigger 29/255 no, 33/255 yes; rumble request reaches the virtual device; pad curves vs the shipped scripts within 0.47% (d6-mouse.sh pad). Real pad: not verified (planned from CI builds, E-5).

Off switch. [SDL2Drv.SDL2Client] UseJoystick=False; PadRumble=False.

#30.3 The input test channel (-inputtest=<file>) and the probes

What the player/owner sees. Nothing in a normal game. It is how every input, UI and settings claim in this section was measured.

Where.

  • New code: Source/SDL2Drv/Src/SDL2InputTest.h (command list :1-30), SDL2InputTest.cpp (558 lines); started in SDL2Client.cpp:122-123 only with -inputtest= and pads initialised (so not on the web, where UseJoystick=False). Commits e254487 (M10: pad attach/detach/button/axis, probe, exec, log; a virtual controller via SDL_JoystickAttachVirtualEx at :249), 43dc4fd (works on the Windows CRT, under wine), 792407e (D-6: mouse <dx> <dy> pushes a real SDL_MOUSEMOTION :361, watch, place, aim, probe2/probe3 :489-524), 84c3082 (D-9: keys, probe4 :515).
  • Log name SDL2Input, quiet unless LogInput=True, -inputlog or the channel (SDL2Pads.cpp:133-148); made RF_Native (SDL2Client.cpp:110-113) because the name purge otherwise handed its index to another name.
  • Source/SDLLaunch -maxfps=<n> (1efa52e, D-6): a frame cap for frame-rate-dependent measurements.
  • Tools: tools/dev/m10-controls.sh (one capped Xvfb run per scenario; M10_RES, M10_UIMAP, M10_XVFB, M10_INI, M10_KEEP, M10_SLOW, M10_KEYHOLD were added by later tasks), tools/dev/m10-inject.py (XTEST keys/mouse, channel lines, log waits, probe parsing), tools/dev/d6-mouse.sh + d6-checks.py, tools/dev/d9-rebind.sh, tools/dev/d2-ui-scale.sh, tools/dev/d78.sh, tools/dev/d3-profiles.sh, d4-names.sh, d5-text.sh, sc-saveconfig.sh.

How the parts fit. Native test hook only; the scenarios combine real X input (XTEST) with channel commands and read the log. Nothing in script.

Why. No physical pad, no xdotool at the time, and no person to play: the evidence had to be log lines.

Decisions and alternatives. Drive the real path from SDL on (a virtual SDL controller, a pushed SDL motion event, real X key events) rather than calling engine functions directly; rejected: a kernel-level virtual pad through /dev/uinput (it would appear on the owner's whole desktop session while a test runs, BLOCKERS.md).

Disadvantages, risks. The tests are timing-sensitive under software GL on a loaded machine: UC2's menu animations count frames, so fixed waits failed at load 24-50 and had to be replaced by waits on log lines (7eaaa7d, 628038a, mouse.md steps 8-10; console-fov.md step 7 needed M10_SLOW/M10_KEYHOLD). A click shorter than one frame can be lost (controls.md coordinator note: inferred from pass rates, not traced); a test file name containing -server starts a dedicated server (ParseParam matches it, profiles.md step 6).

Status. In use by every gate since M10.

Off switch. It is off unless -inputtest= is given.

#30.4 The menus by keyboard and mouse (M10; the cursor mapping from D-2)

What the player/owner sees. Arrows or WASD move through the menus, Enter/Space accept, Escape/right click go back, Tab is Start, F1 is Back, PageUp/PageDown are the triggers, the wheel scrolls lists; hovering over an item selects it, a left click acts on the item (or list row) under the cursor; in fullscreen the game draws its own cursor. On a page with a text field the letter keys type instead (30.12).

Where.

  • Epic's code changed, Source/GUI/Src/UnGUICore.cpp (commit 69b31bc GUI: menu key table as ini data ([GUI.EmulatedJoypad]); mouse click/hover; typing into on-screen keyboards):
    • :860-1036 new: [GUI.EmulatedJoypad] parser (LoadEmulatedPad :931-980, read once, on the first menu key). Behaviour change: Epic's hard-wired PC table (letters A B X Y K W, [ ], ; ', Enter = Start, Escape = Back) becomes data; Epic's table is kept verbatim as the fallback when the section is missing (:944-958), so an Epic or community ini behaves as before.
    • :1046- GetXCode and :1218-1260 the emulation in NativeKeyEvent read the table; a second code applies while the active page has a writable text field (TextEntryTarget :1002-1005).
    • :2543-2551 hover: the item under the cursor takes the focus when the mouse really moved (Epic's hover code is #if 0); :2556-2604 MouseClickPress/Release (called from :1467, :1482): a press on a list selects the row under the cursor (its own InternalOnClick), a release over the same item that has no OnClick but an XControllerEvent handler sends XC_A. DevUI log lines Mouse hover selects ..., Mouse click is A on ....
    • D-2 additions (14d9738, 66445f6): cursor and hit-testing in the UI's design pixels (:675, :706, :1288-1296 software-cursor deltas divided by the UI scale, :1767-1776 the cursor may leave the 4:3 box, :1814, :1844).
  • Epic's code changed, Source/Core/Inc/UnCoreNative.h:27 (commit 75e4258 Core: static native lookup ignores case). Bug fix in shared Core, found by this feature: with Delete=... in the system ini, uc2.bin and ucc-bin stopped at start-up (Can't find 'intUObjectexecDelete'): the ini is read before Core.u, its keys become FNames, an FName keeps the first spelling, and the static-link native table compared case-sensitively. Now appStricmp; all 788 native names are distinct without case (checked).
  • Ini: Default.ini:531-566 [GUI.EmulatedJoypad] (table below; the comment block above it documents the syntax), :519 bHideMouseCursor=False (Epic: True; fullscreen has no system cursor and the GUI draws its own only when this is False), :517 bEmulatedJoypad=True and :521 MenuMouseSens=1.0 (Epic's). Web identical.
Keys Pad code (/ on a text page) Why
arrows; W A S D D-pad; D-pad / Type WASD like the game's movement keys, but they type on text pages
Enter; Space A / Start; A / Type Start = "done" on a keyboard page
Escape, right mouse; Backspace B; B / Type back (cancels text entry too)
X, Y; Q, E X, Y / Type; White, Black / Type the letters the glyphs show; Q/E as the shoulders
PageUp/PageDown; [ ] triggers; triggers / Type [ ] are Epic's
Tab; F1 Start; Back 15 menu files act on XC_Start apart from A; XC_Back = scoreboard in game
Home, End; Delete stick clicks / Type; Raw / Type XC_RightThumb is used in 7 menu files
NumPad 8 2 4 6; wheel right stick; D-pad up/down Epic's; wheel scrolls by moving the selection

How the parts fit. UC2's pages (script, unchanged) act on pad codes (XC_A, XC_Start ...), not on keys or OnClick. The native side turns keys into those codes from the ini table and turns clicks into XC_A where Epic's OnClick path would do nothing. Without the ini section: Epic's letter table (W and A navigate even while typing; no WASD). Without bEmulatedJoypad: raw keys reach pages that ignore them.

Why. Epic's PC table collided with WASD and with typing; many UC2 items have no OnClick, so a click did nothing, and a click on a list accepted the previously selected row (controls.md step 3).

Decisions and alternatives. Data instead of code (an owner can remap menu keys); a click is A only where Epic's click does nothing; hover selects only on real motion, so a resting cursor never steals the focus from keys or pad. Not done (needed script, listed for M14 and still open): button glyphs that show the bound key instead of the Xbox A/B (GUIController.XBoxButtonChars, Footer.uc), hover on list rows (GUIVertList.uc).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Hints still say "A SELECT" etc.; the player has to know Enter = A. The Tab=Start row was dead until D-7 (the console sat on Tab). The UnCoreNative.h change touches every native lookup of the static build (bind time only).

Status. Measured: m10-controls.sh mouse 18 checks (hover then Enter opens the hovered page; click opens the item under the cursor, not the focused one; right click answers B; a click on a list row selects and accepts it; wheel moves a list both ways), at 640x480 and, mapped into the D-2 box, at 1920x1080 (d2-ui-scale.sh); D-2 browser spec: hover and click hit at 1920x1080. Epic's fallback table (no section): not exercised.

Off switch. Delete [GUI.EmulatedJoypad] (Epic's table); bHideMouseCursor=True (Epic's cursor rule).

#30.5 Mouse look separated from the pad (D-6): "the mouse support"

What the player/owner sees. With the mouse: one count turns 0.055 degrees at the default field of view (0.022 x MouseLookSensitivity 2.5), whatever the speed (200 counts in one frame turn ten times what 20 do), the same up/down as sideways (a circle stays a circle), the view stops the moment the mouse stops, the third-person camera no longer slides after a turn, no aim assist pulls the mouse, walking no longer re-centres the pitch, and the mouse has its own invert. With the pad: the Xbox's response (squared curve, acceleration to 2.5x after 0.5 s, dead zone 0.3, friction, adhesion, auto-centre). Both can be used in the same session; the device that moved last "owns" the look.

The owner's complaints, and what was measured as their cause (mouse.md steps 0, 3, 6; evidence runs before = native with M10's ini, epic = Epic's PC DefUser.ini, which the web build carried until c66d275, 2026-10-07, i.e. what the owner played in the browser on 2026-10-06):

Owner's words Cause Before (measured) After (measured)
"mapped to the gamepad joystick ... if you exceed the gamepad movement speed, you get capped" Epic's PC binding MouseX=... Axis aMouseX Speed=6.0 feeds the stick's variable; UCPlayerInput clamps it to aMouseX_Limit=100 per tick, then a turn rate web: 20 counts in one tick turn 441 units, 200 counts 440 10x the counts = 10x the turn
"extremely sensitive ... a tiny bit of movement will make the camera jerk" same path + Speed 6; natively M10's linear tuning at 52.4 units/count; and the profile's pad sliders (0.8) applied to the mouse 1.23 deg/count (web), 0.29 deg/count (native) 0.055 deg/count
"the camera itself slides to the direction of the movement" the third-person camera is lazy: AUCPlayerController::execCalcThirdPersonView (UCGameplay.cpp:289, MoveVectorTo :565) moves toward its place behind the pawn at CameraInterpSpeed; a mouse turn swings that place in one frame 23.8 units of slide after a circle, 63.9 after a flick (native) 0.0 units
"feels hexagonal if I try to do a circle" per-axis clamp + squared fall-off of the stick path, applied separately to X and Y circle 12,293 x 11,212 units, 16.6% out of round (web) 0.29%
"keyboard bindings being backwards" no example given; audit in 30.8 found nothing inverted O-14 still open

Where.

  • Native (C++):
    • Source/SDL2Drv/Src/SDL2Viewport.cpp:873-888: relative mouse counts as IK_MouseX/IK_MouseY (since M4; D-6 did not change it). Source/Engine/Src/UnIn.cpp (Epic's, not changed by D-6): a count adds 0.01 x Speed to the axis the binding names; ReadInput multiplies by 20/dt.
    • Source/Engine/Src/UC2InputMigrate.cpp (new) called at UnGame.cpp:188: kept User.ini values moved (30.7) and the start-up warning when the mouse is bound to aTurn/aLookUp but the loaded UCGameplay.u has no MouseLookSensitivity (:111-117, commit 6857174).
    • Test channel mouse/watch/place/aim and probe2/probe3 (30.3, 792407e); SDLLaunch -maxfps (1efa52e). (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.5.)
  • Script, all in the rebuilt UCGameplay.u (commits 49d4705 UCGameplay: D-6 mouse look separated from the pad, 0475725 camera at its place at once, exact FOV scale, d75990b, e3e927d console commands persist):
    • UCGameplay/Classes/UCPlayerInput.uc:84-96 new properties (MouseLookSensitivity, bInvertMouseLook, MouseLockBreakAngle, bKeysAsStick globalconfig; bMouseOwnsLook, MouseYaw/MousePitch, carries, push).
    • :333-345 in Epic's PlayerInput: what the mouse and the look keys put in aTurn/aLookUp this tick is saved BEFORE Epic's stick code overwrites those two variables; the look owner is updated (stick moved -> pad; mouse or look key moved -> mouse; the mouse wins a tick both moved in; starts as the mouse on PC, the pad on Xbox).
    • :396-397 after Epic's stick code and the pad's invert: AddMouseLook(...); :483-516 AddMouseLook: rotation units = counts x MouseLookSensitivity x 4.0049778 (= 0.022 deg in units) x DesiredFOV/90, own invert, the fraction of a unit carried to the next tick, added to aTurn/aLookUp as (units +- 0.5)/(32 dt) so that UpdateRotation's int(32 dt aTurn) gives exactly that many units. With the mouse still nothing is added: the stick keeps Epic's arithmetic to the bit.
    • :728-730, :772-774 aim adhesion returns 0 while the mouse owns the look (its state keeps updating). Friction needs no change: it acts on aMouseX/aMouseY, which is now the stick alone.
    • :553-569 MouseBreaksLock; :577-590 console MouseLookSens <x>, MouseLookInvert <bool> (30.15); :1335-1338 defaults.
    • UCGameplay/Classes/UCPlayerController.uc:2809-2813, :2836-2839 the lock's pull is held off while the MOUSE moves against it (Epic did this for the stick only); :2877-2882 the mouse breaks a lock by moving MouseLockBreakAngle (25) degrees past the clamp; :2929-2933 reset when not locked; :2950-2953 auto-centre pitch only while the pad owns the look; :3036-3067 MouseOwnsLook, MouseAgainstLock, MouseLookCamera (while the mouse owns the look the camera is put at DesiredCameraLocation, where Epic's lazy camera is heading, walls included); :3227 called after CalcThirdPersonView in CalcBehindView.
    • UCGameplay/Classes/UCTrainingController.uc:262-322 (D-6 hunks at :272, :303-322): the tutorial's "look up" question answered with the mouse sets the mouse's invert; its toggle toggles the invert of the device that owns the look.
  • Ini (Engine/Config/DefUser.ini, commits b22c067, 88c61c7; the web DefUser.ini is identical, regenerated by tools/dev/sync-web-ini.py):
Key Epic's PC value Now Meaning
[Engine.Input] MouseX / MouseY (:101-102) Count bXAxis | Axis aMouseX Speed=6.0 Count bXAxis | Axis aTurn Speed=1.0 (Y: aLookUp) the mouse feeds the mouse channel
Left/Right/PageUp/PageDown (:64,66,107,62) StrafeLeft/StrafeRight/empty/LookDown Axis aTurn Speed=-16/+16, Axis aLookUp Speed=+16/-16 look keys on the linear channel (~88 deg/s)
JoyX/JoyY/JoyU/JoyV (:197-200) none on PC SpeedBase=100.0 DeadZone=0.3 (retail Xbox) the right stick stays on aMouseX/aMouseY
[UCGameplay.UCPlayerInput] MouseLookSensitivity, bInvertMouseLook, MouseLockBreakAngle, bKeysAsStick (:310-316) none 2.5, False, 25.0, True new mouse keys
bNonlinearAccel, aMouseX_Limit/aTurn_Limit, bAccelMultipliers, friction ranges (:321-344) true, 100/1024, true, 0.75/0.65 the retail disc's: same, friction 0.95/0.9 the pad's curve; friction now acts on the stick only
[UCGameplay.UCPlayerController] bAutoCenterPitch (:285), LockSearchTimer (:253) true, 0.1 true, 0.3 (retail) auto-centre is the pad's now

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.5.

  • Frontend: ufrontend 153d8dc test "mouse look (D-6)" in packages/e2e/uc2-wasm-boot.spec.js and the targets/uc2/README.md "Mouse look" section; manifests for the rebuilt package 8510f59, 9e1f22b.
  • Tools: tools/dev/d6-mouse.sh (look ini migrate pad lock before epic), tools/dev/d6-checks.py (circle least-squares fit, camera there-and-back metric), m10-inject.py.

How the parts fit.

  • The native side only delivers counts: SDL -> IK_MouseX/Y -> the UInput binding adds 0.01 per count to the variable the ini names. It is the same code as before D-6.
  • The ini decides the route: the mouse into aTurn/aLookUp (the mouse channel), the right stick into aMouseX/aMouseY (the stick channel, Epic's route, also what a profile's controller layout writes).
  • The script does the separation: Epic's UCPlayerInput.PlayerInput computes aTurn/aLookUp FROM the stick channel through clamp, friction, acceleration and fall-off, overwriting whatever was in them. D-6's code saves the mouse's value first, lets Epic's code run unchanged for the stick, then adds the mouse linearly, and turns the pad-only helpers off while the mouse owns the look.
  • Without the .uc change (shipped UCGameplay.u, our engine and ini): the mouse writes aTurn, Epic's code overwrites it every tick: the mouse does not turn the view at all. The engine logs Input: the mouse is bound to aTurn/aLookUp (D-6) but this UCGameplay.u has no D-6 mouse look .... The pad still works (Epic's path), but the left stick does not walk on PC (Epic scales it by 24000 only on the Xbox; 30.8).
  • Without the ini change (rebuilt package, a User.ini that still says Axis aMouseX): the mouse goes back through the stick's curve (capped, accelerated, hexagonal, with friction and adhesion): what the owner complained about. UC2InputMigrate.cpp rewrites exactly-old values at start-up (30.7). (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.5.)
  • Web: the same package and inis; the browser's pointer lock delivers movementX/Y to SDL; on a Linux desktop the browser applies the desktop's pointer acceleration (frontend note, 30.1).

Why. Owner, PLAN.md review of 2026-10-06: the mouse is "mapped to the gamepad joystick ... no relative movement ... extremely sensitive ... the camera itself slides", "feels hexagonal", "separating the inputs of both really rigorously". Measured causes in the table above.

Decisions and alternatives.

  • The mouse channel is aTurn/aLookUp, which Epic's code overwrites (so they were dead as inputs): no new input variable and no native layout change. M10 had made the stick path linear in the ini so the mouse would be proportional, which took the Xbox curve away from the pad; rejected in D-6.
  • Scale: the Quake/Source convention 0.022 deg/count x 2.5 (about 21 cm per 360 degrees at 800 dpi), times DesiredFOV/90 (zoom slows the mouse, as on the pad).
  • "Who owns the look" by last motion, not a setting. The rigid camera under the mouse: a first attempt (shifting OldCameraLoc) left a slide wherever a wall shortens the camera arm; replaced by putting the camera at its target. Not done: keeping some movement smoothing for the mouse (a lag on the pivot only).
  • Mouse invert outside the profile: the profile is a binary file with one invert flag, which its menu calls the thumbstick's.
  • The pad's values back to the retail disc's (friction 0.95/0.9 instead of the PC leak's 0.75/0.65, which were tuned for a mouse that went through the stick path).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • It is a gameplay-script change in a server package: every peer must run the same UCGameplay.u (shipped and rebuilt peers together: not supported, not tested).
  • The camera under the mouse has no smoothing of the pawn's movement (stairs, dodges). O-11 (owner, 2026-10-08): "the camera moves smoothly, it is the character that moves jittery": this rigid camera drew each frame from the view computed before the pawn moved; fixed after this snapshot by computing the view again (section 50). The walk bob still reaches the screen in full under the mouse (about +-8 px at 1080p).
  • The feel was never judged by a person: 0.055 deg/count is a convention, measured exact, not tuned.
  • The profile still applies its pad sliders to MouseSensitivity_X/Y (even the transient "no profile" profile: 0.8/0.8), so the ini's 1.0/0.9 never act; the pad's speed is the profile's (Epic's behaviour).
  • Lock-on checks are flaky (bot positions; 2 of 69 failed once in the D-9 regression, also on main without D-9).
  • Epic's UCPlayerInput.UpdateSensitivityX assigns the instance twice and never the default (BLOCKERS.md): left as is, no effect on the mouse since D-6.

Status. Measured (natively, null renderer, 60 fps; pad at 30 fps): d6-mouse.sh 67 PASS at the D-6 gate, 69/0 after the saveconfig checks (coordinator logs/coord/a9..a12): 10.012 units per count at 100, 500 and 2000 counts and in one tick (exact within the carried fraction), pitch 10.012, circle radius deviation 0.29%, camera 0.0 units of motion after a circle or a flick, the two inverts independent, adhesion 881 units under the pad and 0 under the mouse, auto-centre only under the pad, pad curves within 0.47% of the shipped scripts. The evidence runs reproduce every symptom in the table. Browser (uc2-wasm-boot.spec.js, 4/4 in the D-6, D-9 and E-2 gates): 10 / 10.0125 / 10.01 / 10.01 units per pixel and no drift; pointer lock was refused by headless Chromium, so the browser test ran without it. Felt by a person: not verified.

Off switch. No single switch. MouseLookSensitivity, bInvertMouseLook, MouseLockBreakAngle in User.ini [UCGameplay.UCPlayerInput]; binding MouseX/MouseY back to aMouseX/aMouseY sends the mouse through the pad's curve again (Epic's behaviour, with our script). -noinimigrate keeps an old User.ini untouched.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.5.

#30.6 PC control defaults: DefUser.ini and KeyBinds.ini (M10, revised by D-6)

What the player/owner sees. A PC layout around WASD that reaches every pad command: left mouse/Alt fire, right mouse alt-fire and shield, middle mouse or L lock-on, E melee, Q back to ranged, C 1st/3rd person, V voice and taunts, Space jump/dodge, Ctrl jump/dodge without combo, Backspace plain jump, Tab/F1 scores, T chat; and a pad works before any profile has applied its layout.

Where. Ini only (no code). Engine/Config/DefUser.ini [Engine.Input] (commits e3c06aa config: PC keyboard/mouse/pad bindings in DefUser.ini, e6a0457, 7bbd7ea README rows; D-6's b22c067 for the mouse, look keys, sticks, wheel). Every row is in Engine/Config/README.md:35-46. Changes against Epic's PC file (the leak's DefUser.ini = the community's User.ini):

Key (line) Epic's PC binding Now Reason
RightMouse (:51) AltFire AltFire | Shield 1 | onrelease Shield 0 the pad's left trigger: PC players had no block/reflect
MiddleMouse (:52) Zoom Lock 1 | onrelease Lock 0 lock-on (pad right-stick click); Zoom is a UT2004 leftover
Tab (:53) Type ShowScores pad Back; T stays chat
Ctrl (:56) Jump SmartJump 1 the pad's left-stick click
E (:117), Q (:129) empty, AllAmmo (a cheat) melee (pad B), MeleeFinishAttack | NextWeapon (pad Y)
C (:174), V (:134) none, CallForHelp BlackButton | ToggleThirdCam, WhiteButton | VoiceMenuNext pad Black / White
P O U I M, NumPad0-6, Home/End PlayersOnly, ShowDebug, editor and camera hacks, ACTOR ALIGN emptied / CenterView debug keys next to the movement keys
Joy1..Joy16, JoySlider1/2 (:178-202) none on PC KeyBinds.ini Layout_0 a pad works before a profile applies its layout; Epic's DefUserXbox.ini had Joy11/Joy12 swapped against the XInput bit order, fixed here
look keys, mouse, sticks, wheel see 30.5 and 30.8

Engine/Config/KeyBinds.ini (the profile's pad layouts) and DefUserXbox.ini/DefaultXbox.ini are not changed (git diff --stat 7633696 4813dd9 -- Engine/Config/): the pad layouts are Epic's, and the Xbox inis are what the script build compiles with (30.17).

How the parts fit. Pure data. The profile (script, unchanged) applies its controller layout by writing only the Joy* keys (ControllerConfiguration.Apply), so the Joy* lines here only matter before a profile applies and the keyboard keys are never touched by a profile. A User.ini is derived from DefUser.ini once and then kept: these defaults reach a player only with a fresh User.ini, through the migration (30.7) for exact old values, or through D-9's DEFAULTS button (30.16).

Why. Epic's PC file was a development file: no melee key, no shield on PC, cheats and debug commands next to WASD, no pad lines (controls.md step 4).

Decisions and alternatives. Each pad command on a key near WASD; arrows turn (UT2004's default) where Epic's strafed. Owner choices still open on the D-9 page (LOG.md 2026-10-08): the C/V order (C = Black = "next" sits left of V = White = "previous", the reverse of the shoulders), Space dodging while moving (30.8), swap vs clear on a conflict.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. A deviation from Epic's PC defaults throughout. Dead commands Epic bound are still bound (Shift Walking, G Grab, J Taunt Taunt1, R ForceReload, F2, F3, =, -, \, [, ], and the Shield 1/0 part, which Epic's Xbox binding has too): harmless, listed in mouse.md step 4.

Status. Measured: m10-controls.sh keyboard 16 checks (W forward 600 units, Right arrow turns, E melee, Q ranged, Alt fires, Escape pause and back). Exercised later: Space and J jump (d9-rebind.sh match). Not exercised by any test: scoreboard keys, lock-on keys in a match by key (the D-6 lock test uses the console), taunt/voice, the view key, the combo key X.

Off switch. Edit User.ini, or the D-9 page.

#30.7 Old User.ini values moved at start-up (UC2InputMigrate, D-6)

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.7 (removed).

What the player/owner sees. A player whose User.ini came from an earlier build (natively, or the copy the browser keeps) gets D-6's mouse and pad without deleting anything; the log says what moved.

Where.

  • New code: Source/Engine/Src/UC2InputMigrate.cpp (commits 02289c7, 6857174, 82f25cc): :30-53 the list (Epic's and M10's MouseX/MouseY, M10's look keys and sticks, the dead wheel commands, Epic's PC friction ranges), :56-64 M10's linear pad section (recognised by its 1,000,000/10,240,000 limits, moved as a whole), :76-90 apply one key only when its value is EXACTLY the old one (case-insensitive, trailing blanks ignored), log Input: User.ini [...] ... is a value from before D-6; now ..., and re-read the config of a class that is already loaded (the static build loads UCGameplay before the game engine starts; found by the test); :96-100 -noinimigrate; :111-117 the warning when the shipped UCGameplay.u is combined with D-6 bindings.
  • Epic's code changed: Source/Engine/Src/UnGame.cpp:183-188 one call at the start of the client part of UGameEngine::Init, before any viewport reads its bindings.
  • Tools: tools/dev/d6-mouse.sh migrate (and before/epic use -noinimigrate to reproduce the old state).

How the parts fit. Native only, on the config cache; the file is written at exit (web: at flushconfig). Works with any package set.

Why. User.ini is derived once and kept for years natively and for ever on the web (C-2); the owner's browser may still hold Epic's or M10's bindings, which would send the mouse into the stick's curve with the rebuilt script.

Decisions and alternatives. Exact-match only, so anything a player chose stays. Rejected: resetting User.ini or overwriting whole sections.

Disadvantages, risks. A hand-maintained list; a value that differs in anything but case/trailing blanks is not moved. D-9's rebinding page writes only today's commands, none of which is in the list, so the migration can never undo a rebinding (rebind.md step 0).

Status. Measured: d6-mouse.sh migrate 8 PASS (18 lines moved from M10's User.ini, 6 from Epic's; afterwards 500 counts = 5,006 units and the pad's limits 100 with the curve on).

Off switch. -noinimigrate.

#30.8 Keys as a stick, the stick on PC, and the "backwards bindings" audit (D-6)

What the player/owner sees. On a keyboard, Space while moving dodges and wall-dodges (as A does on the pad), Lock + A/D switches target, the on-screen "push the stick" prompts can be answered; the wheel changes weapon; the pad's left stick walks on PC with Epic's own binding.

Where.

  • Script (UCGameplay.u, commit 49d4705): UCGameplay/Classes/UCPlayerInput.uc:403-405, :528-541 KeysAsStick: when bKeysAsStick and a movement key is held, a full stick (0.707 per axis on a diagonal) is added to Ctrl_Move_Fwd_Pct/Ctrl_Move_Stf_Pct, the stick direction that SmartJump's dodges, the lock-target search and the timed input sequences read. :406-412: on PC aForward/aStrafe (the stick's 0..1) are multiplied by 24000 in PlayerInput, because UCPlayerController.PlayerMove does it only when Level.IsXBox() (UCPlayerController.uc:3901-3908, :4079-4086, Epic's comment "aForward range on PC is +/- 24000.0").
  • Ini: DefUser.ini:316 bKeysAsStick=True; :214-215 wheel NextWeapon both ways (Epic: NextSkill/PrevSkill, which exist in no script package; UC2's PrevWeapon is an empty function); :197-198 JoyX/JoyY SpeedBase=100.0 DeadZone=0.3 (M10 had SpeedBase=2400000 to work around the missing scale). (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.8.)
  • Tools: d6-mouse.sh lock (Lock + D switches target), d6-mouse.sh pad (0.28 deflection walks/turns nothing) and m10-controls.sh pad (the left stick walks at 0.35 and at full deflection).

How the parts fit. Script does both; the ini switch and the wheel binding are data. Without the .uc: the keyboard never dodges or switches target, and the left stick does not move the player on PC (measured -0.0 units with the shipped scripts and SpeedBase=100). M10's ini work-around made the stick walk but was undone by any profile that swaps the sticks (ControllerConfiguration.ApplyJoysticks writes SpeedBase=100) and saturated Ctrl_Move_*_Pct at any deflection.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.8.

Why. The owner's "keyboard bindings being backwards" (no example given). The audit (mouse.md step 4) looked up every [Engine.Input] command as an alias, an exec function or an engine command and checked every direction against measurements: nothing is inverted or swapped. What was wrong: the dead wheel commands, the movement keys not counting as a stick (the community's reddit fix bound WASD to analog axes for this), the look keys on the stick's curve. The reddit guide's other advice ("turn OFF Centerview") is the auto-centre that D-6 made pad-only.

Decisions and alternatives. Keys count as a full stick (not proportional). Side effect accepted as UC2's SmartJump design: Space while moving dodges; Backspace stays a plain jump (owner choice open: bind Space to JUMP ONLY on the D-9 page).

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.8.

Disadvantages, risks. Changes gameplay on PC compared with Epic's PC build (which could not dodge by keyboard at all); the owner's example is still needed (PLAN.md "Needed from the owner", O-14) to know whether "backwards" meant something else, e.g. the C/V order.

Status. Measured: lock target switch by Lock + D; the left stick walks at 0.35 and full deflection, not at 0.28; the keyboard dodge itself is not asserted by a test (seen through the binding audit and the code).

Off switch. bKeysAsStick=False.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.8.

#30.9 The UI in a centred 4:3 box (D-2)

What the player/owner sees. At any window size the menus, the HUD and the reticle are drawn in the largest centred 4:3 area (scaled to the height, or to the width on a window narrower than 4:3), at the same layout as the Xbox's 640x480 screen; the 3D world (and the menu level behind the menus) fills the window; a menu page's dark background layer covers the whole window; text drawn larger takes its glyphs from bigger sizes of the same font, so it stays crisp. At 640x480 nothing changes.

Where.

  • Epic's code changed (commits 14d9738 Engine: the UI's 640x480 design space in a centred 4:3 box, 66445f6 menus clipped to the UI box, f9e055f/519b5de sharper text, dd78796 the console's line clipped, 37fa6d4):
    • Source/Engine/Src/UnCanvas.cpp:985-987 UCanvas::Update() calls ApplyUIBox; :1007-1083 ApplyUIBox: with Fit43, ClipX/ClipY/SizeX/SizeY = 640x480 (a split viewport: its share) and CanvasToScreen = scale s = min(W/640, H/480) plus the box's corner; the identity case (640x480, or Native640) runs Epic's code path unchanged. Behaviour change of the viewport canvas only (camera effects, post-processing, shadows and scripted textures build their own FCanvasUtil and are untouched).
    • UnCanvas.cpp:56-72 a tile that covers the whole design space without a transform is a full-screen layer: in game it goes to the whole viewport; in a menu it follows UIMenuBackground (logged once per material, UIScale: full-screen tile ...). :96 what the GUI's pages draw is clipped to the box (as the Xbox's screen clipped it). :190-410 (FontHeight, ParseFontPt, GlyphInkCentre, SharpFontFor) and :426-560 (their use where strings are drawn): sharper text, the 640x480 font's layout with each whole glyph taken from a taller size of the same family in the font's package (sizes ranked by real glyph height, ink centred on the small glyph's ink); :1330 DrawActorTile, :1588 DrawPortal, :2238 SetCanvasMatrix mapped through the box; :1085-1120 GetUIScale, TargetToDesign, SetUIContext.
    • Source/Engine/Inc/UnCanvas.h:85-95 (non-virtual methods, no new data: the script layout of Canvas is untouched), Source/Engine/Inc/UnRenderUtil.h:256-290 (FCanvasUtil gains the box and a game/menu context), Source/Engine/Src/UnRenderUtil.cpp:793.
    • Source/Engine/Inc/UnCamera.h:499-510, :546-548 UClient gains UIScaleMode, UIMenuBackground, UISharpText (C++ only); Source/Engine/Src/UnCamMgr.cpp:98-107 their config properties, :143-161 console UISCALE [Native640|Fit43] [Box|Fill] [Sharp|Soft] (session only).
    • Source/Engine/Src/UnController.cpp:3307, :3340 WorldToScreen returns and ScreenToWorld takes design pixels (the lock-on reticle, the reflect icon and the Sonar blips land on their actors).
    • Source/GUI/Src/UnGUICore.cpp:1594 the GUI's ResX/ResY = canvas size; :1662-1723 pages drawn in menu context, cursor and designer frames back in game context; cursor conversions (30.4).
    • Source/Engine/Src/UnInteractionMaster.cpp:288-292 the global interactions (console line, GUI) clipped to the box (at 4K the console line ran 72 px into the side band).
    • Source/Engine/Src/UnMovie.cpp:325 (our M9 file) the full-screen movie frame stays in window pixels.
    • Measurement aid: Source/SDL2Drv/Src/SDL2Client.cpp:207-213 TOGGLERENDERLEVEL (the Xbox client's command), Source/Engine/Src/UnRender.cpp:2352 clears to black with the level off.
  • Ini: Default.ini:179-186 [SDL2Drv.SDL2Client] UIScaleMode=Fit43, UIMenuBackground=Fill, UISharpText=True (also the compiled defaults, SDL2Client.cpp:91-93); README row Engine/Config/README.md:27. Web identical.
  • Tools: tools/dev/d2-ui-scale.sh + tools/dev/d2-measure.py (runs and PIL checks at 8 sizes), tools/dev/d2/*.scen, the browser spec outside the frontend tools/dev/d2/web/uc2-d2-uiscale.spec.js + playwright.d2.config.js; m10-controls.sh M10_RES, M10_UIMAP (clicks mapped into the box).
  • Frontend: ufrontend e76227c the boot spec's play test maps 640x480 positions into the box.

How the parts fit. Native only. Scripts and the GUI keep drawing in 640x480 design pixels (the HUD's pos_* structs, the crosshair at 320,240, absolute menu layouts, relative ones times ResX/ResY); the canvas maps that into the box. No script was changed, so it works with the shipped packages. With Native640 the canvas is the window's pixels again, i.e. exactly the old behaviour (menus 1:1 top-left, relative parts stretched, HUD in the corner).

Why. Owner (addendum, 2026-10-06): "it is a disaster beyond 480p, where the menu is very distorted, but worse, the in game ui stays in the corner, including the reticle! We need to make the UI to scale to the 4:3 of fullscreen and be centered." Cause: UC2's HUD is 640x480 design pixels (UCHUD.uc:2 "designed for 640x480 only") with ScreenWidth = Canvas.SizeX; the GUI multiplies relative sizes by the window and draws absolute ones 1:1.

Decisions and alternatives. One native place (UCanvas::Update) instead of per-element script fixes (the HUD is a tree of hundreds of CanvasElements); identity at 640x480 by construction (the regression anchor). Menu background Fill by default (with Box the box's edge shows as a brightness step over the moving menu level). The HUD is not clipped (actor markers may lie outside the box). Fonts: UC2 has one font per GUIFont for all resolutions, so picking by resolution was not an option; glyph substitution keeps every measured layout.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • A large edit of Epic's shared canvas code (UnCanvas.cpp +507 lines) that every 2D draw goes through.
  • The full-screen rule is a heuristic: any tile exactly covering 640x480 at 0,0 is treated as a full-screen layer.
  • The crosshair art sits about 1.5 design pixels left of its anchor (Epic's art): -1 px at 640x480, -2.5 px at 2560x1440, -4 px at 4K from the screen centre. Not ours to fix without changing the art or the script.
  • Sharp text: small kerning differences against the soft text (the 'f' of "Profile"); the Xbox button glyphs have no bigger size and stay magnified.
  • Split screen (E-1) has code for its share of the box but was never run.

Status. Measured: d2-ui-scale.sh final pass 81 PASS, 0 FAIL (ui-scale.md step 6): UI box exact at 8 sizes (including 5:4, 16:10 and portrait), 640x480 pixel-identical to main outside the self-animating parts, Native640 99.68% identical to main at 1920x1080, HUD scaled within 0.5 px, lock-on reticle at the same window pixels boxed and unboxed, the M10 mouse scenario 18/18 at 640x480 and 1920x1080; browser spec 1 passed at 1920x1080 (box 240,0 1440x1080, hover and click hit). Seen only: the pictures (logs/d2/run/contact-sheet.png).

Off switch. UIScaleMode=Native640 (Epic's), UIMenuBackground=Box, UISharpText=False; console UISCALE.

#30.10 Profiles and saves: start in the last profile, deletion, the web's files (D-3, C-2)

What the player/owner sees. The game starts in the profile used last (its settings, character and name applied before any menu), profiles survive a restart natively and a closed browser tab, and deleting a profile on the Profiles page works on Linux.

Where.

  • New code: Source/Engine/Src/UC2Profiles.cpp (commits e3529cc Engine: the first player starts in the profile they last played with, c2e7b83 PROFILESTATE diagnostics): :63-87 UC2StartWithLastProfile (reads User.ini [Profiles] LastProfile; if the profile exists it is loaded into the first viewport's transient profile with the same two calls as APlayerController::execLoadProfile; the PlayerController takes it over at its first SetGamepadID); :89-116 UC2RememberProfile (once per tick: the first viewport's profile name, "" for "no profile", against the remembered one; on a change SetString + Flush of User.ini and a log line); :164- console PROFILESTATE.
  • Epic's code changed: Source/Engine/Src/UnGame.cpp:245-249 (call right after Client->NewViewport in UGameEngine::Init), :3529 (per tick, first thing in UGameEngine::Tick), :457 (exec chain).
  • Epic's code changed, bug fix in shared Core: Source/Core/Inc/FFileManagerGeneric.h:240-243 DeleteFiles cut the pattern at the last \ only, so with / every file was deleted as <dir>/*/<file> and deleting a profile never worked on Linux (commit 10b802f).
  • Web layout, Epic's code changed under a macro: Source/Engine/Src/ULoadSaveManager.cpp:18-23 and the five functions at :109, :134, :148, :186, :217: under __EMSCRIPTEN__ (or -DUC2_FLAT_PROFILE_FILES=1) a profile is one flat file Profiles/<name>.PRO (same bytes) instead of Epic's Profiles/<name>/AVARIS_PROFILE.PRO (commit 22e9dbf, C-2). Native keeps Epic's layout.
  • Related engine pieces from C-1/C-2 that saving depends on (reported in detail with the web work): FLUSHCONFIG (Source/Engine/Src/UnEngine.cpp:308-316, 61c9f48), which the frontend sends before it stores the inis; the RemoveNames fix (f14ba00: the config cache was dropped without being written at a garbage purge); M13's FFileManagerUnix::Exists fix (0c9d5d2: a saved profile was reported missing, so every load failed). Source/UCC/Src/UCC.cpp:335- ucc-bin profiletest save|load|list|exists|delete.
  • Ini: User.ini [Profiles] LastProfile (written by the engine; not in DefUser.ini; a section no class reads). The dead [Engine.PlayerProfile] defaultProfileDirectoryPath (Default.ini:510) is Epic's and read nowhere.
  • Frontend: targets/uc2/target.json userFiles: ["System/Profiles/*.PRO"] (d6554d4), manifestExclude System/Profiles/**, *.PRO, System/Shot*.bmp, System/AutoPatch.txt; Avaris.ini/User.ini persisted as engine files (the existing manifestExclude derivation + flushconfig); README section "Profiles, the player name and text entry" (1a8f570); opt-in packages/e2e/uc2-wasm-saves.spec.js (646970f .. c69301f).
  • Tools: tools/dev/d3-profiles.sh, tools/dev/c1-saves.sh (file inventory + restart), tools/dev/c1-web-saves.sh (the wasm ucc-bin.js under Node with the frontend's own userFiles compiler).

How the parts fit. Engine only, no script change: comparing state once per tick covers every script path (select, create, copy, rename, delete) without hooks, so it works with the shipped packages and on the web. The profile file itself is written by Epic's code (ULoadSaveManager). On the web the frontend's daemon stores the flat profile file (a userFiles glob may have a wildcard only in the file name) and the inis after flushconfig.

Why. Owner: saving does not work. Found (web-ini-saves.md step 3, profiles.md step 0): profiles DID save natively after M13's fix, but Epic's game starts every run on a transient "No Profile", every setting lives in the profile, a transient profile is never saved, and nothing remembers the last profile (the Xbox neither): a player who made a profile found "No Profile" and default settings at the next start. Deleting failed on Linux; the web could not persist a per-profile directory.

Decisions and alternatives. Remember in User.ini (per player, persisted by the web host) rather than in a new file; first viewport only (a split-screen guest keeps Epic's behaviour); poll instead of script hooks. For the web, flat files in the engine; alternative left to the coordinator: teach the frontend a directory wildcard and revert 22e9dbf.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. New behaviour compared with the Xbox and Epic's PC build (they always start without a profile). UC2's UI has no "no profile" choice once a profile exists (only deleting the active one gets back to none). Native and web store the same bytes under different paths. A known gap (BLOCKERS.md): a launch that names no class (the web Play form, a console open) uses [DefaultPlayer] Class, which after switching profile without launching is the previous profile's character.

Status. Measured: d3-profiles.sh ALL PASS (27 lines: starts in "Saver" with no menu action, invert and character applied in a directly started map, rename/delete followed, starts without a profile after deletion); c1-saves.sh 11 PASS; c1-web-saves.sh 16 PASS under Node; browser uc2-wasm-saves.spec.js 1 passed (D-3 runs 9, 10, 11 with the wasm's sha256 proved; coordinator gates a7, a12, a13). Browser runs 4-7 were void (another branch's engine was served from the shared serve-web/, profiles.md step 10).

Off switch. None for the remembering (an empty LastProfile starts without a profile; deleting the active profile empties it). The web layout: the UC2_FLAT_PROFILE_FILES compile-time macro.

#30.11 Player name vs profile name (D-4)

What the player/owner sees. Without a profile the player is called by the launcher's name (the web name field, -ini:, ?Name=), also after choosing a character; with a profile, by the profile's name everywhere, also in a map started directly by the launcher; the launcher's own name is never overwritten by the profile's; in the browser the name field is disabled with "Your game profile "X" names you in the game ..." while a profile is active.

Where.

  • Script, the rebuilt GUI.u (commits 4069362 GUI: without a profile the player keeps the launcher's name; fd81f64 the launcher's name is the first player's only): GUI/Classes/GUIController.uc:2757-2781 SetProfilePlayerName, transient-profile branch: the name is PC.Level.GetDefaultURL("Name") (= User.ini [DefaultPlayer] Name) for the first player; only if that is empty, or for a split-screen guest, the character's name as Epic did. GUIController.uc:186, :190: bUnlockCharacters, bShowAllMuts are config again, as in the shipped GUI.u (the source had lost the flag, so the community bUnlockCharacters=True tweak would have stopped working with a rebuilt GUI.u; M14 finding).
  • Epic's code changed (commit 6dda321 Engine: a saved profile's name is not the player's own name): Source/Engine/Src/UnGame.cpp:2961-2964 UGameEngine::LoadMap writes the URL's Name back to [DefaultPlayer] only while no saved profile names the player (UC2ProfileNamesThePlayer, UC2Profiles.cpp:130); UnGame.cpp:1162-1167 MatchViewportsToActors spawns the first viewport's player with a copy of the URL whose Name is the active profile's (UC2ProfileNameInURL, UC2Profiles.cpp:149).
  • Frontend (generic, data-driven, every target): network.playerName.ownedBy (0b269e9): packages/glue/src/config/ini-identity.js readPersistedNameOwner, packages/frontend/src/stores/net.ts resolveNameOwner/nameOwnerHint, NetworkPanel.vue (field :disabled, hint), ProxyNetworkPanel.vue, tests config-name-owner.test.ts, ini-identity.test.cjs; the uc2 target names User.ini [Profiles] LastProfile with its hint (targets/uc2/target.json:70-74, 5785ebd, 341635a).
  • Tools: tools/dev/d4-names.sh (n1..n4); the saves spec with UC2_E2E_REBUILT_GUI=1.

How the parts fit. The .uc decides the name without a profile; the engine keeps [DefaultPlayer] Name as the player's own and makes the profile's name reach a directly started map (the GUI binds its name delegate only when a menu is first shown); the frontend reads the key D-3's engine code writes and disables its field.

  • Without the rebuilt GUI.u: with a profile everything is right (engine part); without one, the character's name ("Anubis", "Sapphire") replaces the launcher's as soon as the character page is used, goes into the match URL and is then written to [DefaultPlayer] Name (measured: d4-names.sh on the shipped packages, 4 FAIL exactly there).
  • Without the engine part: the profile's name leaks into [DefaultPlayer] Name, which the web launcher reads back as the player's own name next time.
  • Without the frontend part: the field stays editable while its value has no effect.

Why. Owner's rule (PLAN.md D-4): "the frontend's name wins when there is no profile; a profile's name wins when one exists; if changing a profile's name from outside is impractical, the frontend disables its name field". The facts are in web-ini-saves.md step 4.

Decisions and alternatives. Disable the field rather than rename a profile from the launcher (a profile's name is its file/directory name). A split-screen guest keeps Epic's character name.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. A changed GUI script function (the only one in GUI.u; 684 -> 778 bytes; one of Epic's load-time patches targets it, which the patcher now skips for the rebuilt file). A generic frontend change that touches every target (inactive unless a target declares ownedBy).

Status. Measured: d4-names.sh ALL PASS on the rebuilt tree (n1 11, n2 7, n3 3, n4 4 scenario checks + file checks; coordinator a7); browser with the rebuilt GUI.u: "without a profile the player has the launcher's name: Player-dd0b49"; the disabled field and hint in the saves spec. Frontend unit tests: glue 4 new, frontend 5 new.

Off switch. None in the engine. Remove ownedBy from the target to keep the field editable.

#30.12 Text entry with a real keyboard (M10, D-5)

What the player/owner sees. Every on-screen keyboard page (new/rename/copy profile, the host's session name, the mid-game server name) takes the real keyboard: letters type exactly what is pressed (W, A, S, D, X, Y no longer navigate), Backspace deletes and clears a preselected name, Enter finishes, Escape cancels; the X that opens the session-name page is not typed into it. Chat (T) and the console are Epic's Console and work as before.

Where.

  • Epic's code changed, Source/GUI/Src/UnGUICore.cpp (commits 69b31bc, 86e0cf7 typed characters reach an on-screen keyboard's field through its OnKeyType, 18b839d D-5 fixes):
    • :1002-1005 TextEntryTarget: the active page's visible, writable GUIEditBox (on a keyboard page it never has the focus; its key buttons do).
    • :1219-1258 with a text field up, a key whose table code is Type (or any key that makes a character) is not a pad code: editing keys go to the field's NativeKeyEvent, nothing else on the page sees them. :1248-1253 D-5: a real Backspace on a fully selected text is given as Delete (clears it); the on-screen Backspace button (pad) keeps deleting one letter.
    • :2384-2399 characters go to the field's OnKeyType delegate (the handler UC2KeyboardPanel calls for its own buttons; UGUIComponent::NativeKeyType refuses because the field has bAcceptsInput false).
    • :1018-1023, :1226-1231, :2389-2394 D-5 GPadKeyHeld: a character key whose press acted as a pad button while no text field was up has its character dropped if it arrives at a keyboard page (SDL delivers the character after the press, which had already opened the page synchronously).
  • Ini: the /Type and /Start codes of [GUI.EmulatedJoypad] (Enter=A/Start, Escape=B, letters /Type).
  • Tools: m10-controls.sh text (7 checks), tools/dev/d5-text.sh (k1 new profile, k2 rename, k3 copy, k4 session name, k5 chat), browser uc2-wasm-saves.spec.js.

How the parts fit. Native and ini only; UC2KeyboardPanel/UC2Keyboard (script) unchanged; works with the shipped packages. Without the routing: Epic's behaviour, where W/A/X/Y navigate or accept while "typing".

Why. Owner (PLAN.md D-5): "the on-screen keyboard pages accept a real keyboard, Enter accepts, Escape cancels". D-5's runs found the two defects fixed above (profiles.md step 6).

Decisions and alternatives. On a text page Enter = Start (= "done" in UC2's keyboard pages), Escape = B (cancel). Backspace on a selection behaves like any PC text field.

Disadvantages, risks. Latin-1 only (30.1); profile names are cut at Epic's 15 characters; GPadKeyHeld is a file-static heuristic (cleared at the key's release or any other press).

Status. Measured: d5-text.sh ALL PASS (k1 13, k2 14, k3 4, k4 14, k5 10); m10-controls.sh text 7 checks (WaSdKxYb typed exactly, no navigation while typing); browser saves spec types, deletes, renames and cancels. The mid-game server-name page (UC2MidGame_Config) uses the same UC2Keyboard but was not run.

Off switch. None (removing the /Type codes from the table brings Epic's navigation back).

#30.13 The console on the key left of 1 (D-7)

What the player/owner sees. The key left of 1 (`/~ on a US layout; whatever that physical key is on another layout) opens Epic's one-line console in a match and over the menus; Enter runs the line, Escape closes it without closing the menu page beneath; Up/Down recall lines. Tab is the scoreboard in a match and Start in the menus again. Replies are written to the log only.

Where.

  • Ini: Default.ini:501-502 [Engine.Console] ConsoleKey=192 (IK_Tilde; Epic's 9 = Tab) (commit 9e96e95; README :28; web regenerated). SDL2Viewport.cpp:62 maps SDL_SCANCODE_GRAVE to IK_Tilde.
  • Epic's code changed (commit 6f9a9fa): Source/Engine/Src/UnGame.cpp:277-289 a ConsoleKey of 9 (a kept Avaris.ini, the web's stored one) is moved to IK_Tilde at start-up for the run, with a log line.
  • Epic's code changed (commits 868f41d, 0f77515): Source/Engine/Src/UnInteractionMaster.cpp:85-97, :114-116, :154-169 (GConsoleOwnsKey): the release (and the control character) of a key whose press the console took stops at the console. Bug fix: Epic's Console.uc consumes presses only, so the release of Escape went on to the menu page, which closes on Escape's release.
  • Tools: tools/dev/d78/console-game.scen (12 checks), console-menu.scen (8), tools/dev/d78.sh; browser tools/dev/d78/web/uc2-d78.spec.js (outside the frontend).

How the parts fit. Ini + native; Engine/Classes/Console.uc unchanged. Works with any package set.

Why. Owner: "Is the console locked or hidden? Can we unlock it?" It was never locked: Epic's shipped ini put it on Tab, which PC players do not try, and the console sees every key first, so it also hid M10's Tab=ShowScores and Tab=Start.

Decisions and alternatives. A physical key (scancode), not a character. Not done: an on-screen output area (Epic's Console.Message is empty, Console.uc:55-58); it would need a Console.uc change or a native drawing path.

Disadvantages, risks. Replies only in the log (System/Avaris.log, the terminal with -log, the browser console). ConsoleKey=9 can no longer be chosen: the start-up code moves 9 to 192 on every run (the runtime value is not written back). The console key pressed during a D-9 key capture opens the console (left so, asserted).

Status. Measured: console-game 12/12, console-menu 8/8 (console-fov.md step 2); browser 1 passed (Backquote opens it over the main menu, AUTOFOV and get run, Escape leaves the menu as it was).

Off switch. [Engine.Console] ConsoleKey=<another key code> (not 9).

#30.14 AutoFOV: Hor+ field of view on wide windows (D-8)

What the player/owner sees. On a 16:9 or 21:9 window the view shows more to the sides instead of a vertically zoomed 4:3 view; the 4:3 picture is the exact centre of the wide one (world, first person, zoom, letterbox); the first-person weapon keeps its size and place; the lock-on reticle stays on its target. 4:3 is unchanged.

Where.

  • Epic's code changed (commit 6f9a9fa Engine: AutoFOV (Hor+) for every view of the player; port of engine-ut2004's template, ufront steps 2.16/2.29):
    • Source/Engine/Inc/UnRender.h:1140-1199, Source/Engine/Src/UnRender.cpp:1754-1916: [ufront.AutoFOV] read once; ufrontAutoFovSize (:1801, split viewports use the whole window's aspect), ufrontEffectiveFov (:1830), ufrontBaseFov (:1858, the inverse), ufrontWeaponFov (:1869), console AUTOFOV (:1898, session only), log lines in the template's format; :2297-2309 the letterbox's ScaleFOVY made exact in tan space when widened.
    • Source/Engine/Src/UnGame.cpp:3096-3144 FScopedAutoFov, armed at :3176 in UGameEngine::Draw after PlayerCalcView: Viewport->Actor->FovAngle is the effective FOV only while the frame is drawn, so script (and the mouse's DesiredFOV/90) sees the real FOV; a FOV written during the frame (the teleport effect) is mapped back to base units. :455 exec chain.
    • Source/Engine/Src/UnCanvas.cpp:1394-1409 the canvas-drawn actor (first-person weapon) pass; :1445 execDrawActor's omitted FOV is -1 so it can be resolved exactly.
    • Source/Engine/Src/UnController.cpp:3250-3260 AutoFovViewFov, used by WorldToScreen/ScreenToWorld (:3286, :3337).
  • Ini: Default.ini:504-507 [ufront.AutoFOV] AutoFOV=2 (Adaptive), ReferenceAspect=1.333333, WeaponScale=1.0 (also compiled defaults); README :29; web identical.
  • Tools: tools/dev/d78.sh, tools/dev/d78-measure.py, tools/dev/d78/fov.scen, sniper.scen.

How the parts fit. Native only, no script; the template's section name lets the frontend's -ini:System:[ufront.AutoFOV]:AutoFOV=0 reach it.

Why. Owner: "Lets implement the AutoFOV work that has worked so well in all of the other engines." UE2's FovAngle is horizontal, so at 16:9 the vertical FOV shrank from 73.7 to 58.7 degrees (Vert-); the Xbox's widescreen mode did the same.

Decisions and alternatives. The template's scoped override; UC2 additions listed in console-fov.md step 0 (split-viewport aspect, the in-frame FOV write-back, the tan-space letterbox, WorldToScreen outside the frame, the exact omitted-FOV case, a session console command for A/B).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Deviates from the Xbox (Vert-) by design. The teleport write-back path was never exercised (no teleport at spawn in DM). The AUTOFOV console command departs from the template's "one home per setting" rule. The frontend's shared AutoFOV e2e (autofov-tests.js) is not registered for uc2.

Status. Measured: d78-measure.py 16 PASS (logged effective FOV = 2 atan(tan(base/2) x aspect/(4/3)) within 0.001; the 4:3 picture vs the centre of 16:9/21:9: third person 99.55%/98.56%, first person and zoom 100%; weapon area and centroid 0.0; lock-on reticle within 1 px; 640x480 and 1024x768 identical to main in the static part); browser 1 passed (menu level base=65 effective=80.69 at 1920x1080).

Off switch. [ufront.AutoFOV] AutoFOV=0; console AUTOFOV 0 for the session.

#30.15 Script SaveConfig() on PC, writing only what changed (saveconfig: ScriptSaveConfig, SAVECONFIGKEYS)

What the player/owner sees. What script saves now stays after a restart (the weapon hand from SetWeaponHand / ShowGun, the mouse sensitivity and invert from the console or the D-9 page, rebound keys); nothing else in the inis changes, and a match's URL options never leak into Gametypes.ini.

Where.

  • Epic's code changed, a gate lifted behind a setting: Source/Core/Src/UnCorSc.cpp:4909-4991. Epic compiled the bodies of execSaveConfig/execStaticSaveConfig out on PC and Xbox (#if 0 // rjp - calling SaveConfig() has mucho side-effects; the Xbox reason: certification forbids unprompted disk writes, settings persist through the profile there). Now UC2ScriptSaveConfigEnabled() (:4925, [Engine.Engine] ScriptSaveConfig) decides; _XBOX keeps the no-op; a live GameInfo instance is not saved (logged once). Commits e3e927d Core: script SaveConfig()/StaticSaveConfig() write the ini on PC; SAVECONFIGKEYS; a base-class save leaves out what a subclass sets, 7a5dac9 a script StaticSaveConfig writes only what changed, f72face, 6d0798a.
  • Epic's code changed, Source/Core/Src/UnObj.cpp: :1446-1563 the filter state and helpers, :1565-1667 UObject::SaveConfig with the filters, active only for the script path and SAVECONFIGKEYS (GSaveConfigOnly; UC2NoteDefaultOverrides :1469; UC2SetByAnotherClass :1507; UC2ConfigValueChanged :1529; used at :1583-1640); :2735 UC2SaveConfigFromScript; :2752-2796 (UC2SaveConfigKeys :2762) and :2813 the console command SAVECONFIGKEYS <Class> <Prop>... (exactly the named config properties, the same file/section/ExportText/flush code; replies one line per key, or off). Source/Core/Src/UnClass.cpp:1499, :1879 record a class's deliberate defaults right after its default object loads its config. Source/Core/Src/UnMisc.cpp:2158-2160 reads the switch once in appInit (after the -ini: overrides). New private header Source/Core/Src/UC2ScriptSaveConfig.h.
  • Test hook: Source/UCC/Src/UCC.cpp:411- ucc-bin saveconfigtest static|instance|gameinfo|keys|get (runs the native as the VM would; also in the wasm ucc-bin.js).
  • Script (UCGameplay.u): UCPlayerInput.uc:571-590 MouseLookSens/MouseLookInvert set the class default and send SAVECONFIGKEYS UCGameplay.UCPlayerInput <key> (not StaticSaveConfig(), which would also write the profile's pad invert and sensitivity that the profile applies to that class's defaults).
  • Ini: Default.ini:42 [Engine.Engine] ScriptSaveConfig=True (web the same); README :47.
  • Tools: tools/dev/sc-saveconfig.sh (u: native ucc, w: wasm ucc under Node, g: game set/kill/restart/off, m: menu round trip and a full match with the natives on and off); d6-mouse.sh look checks the two console commands land in User.ini.

How the parts fit. The native side makes Epic's natives work and safe; the five script call sites stay Epic's (saveconfig.md step 1: PlayerController.SetHand (wanted), PlayerInput.UpdateSensitivity/Accel/Smoothing (harmless in UC2), UdpGamespyQuery (server, unreachable as shipped), DeathMatch.AdjustSkill (a live gametype: now refused), charconfig (developer)). The only script change is the two mouse commands. With the shipped UCGameplay.u: the engine part works (e.g. the weapon hand persists) and there are no mouse commands to save.

Why. Owner: "this is a PC game now". What "mucho side-effects" really meant, found by experiment: a whole-class save of a base class writes its globalconfig defaults over every subclass's at the next start (SetWeaponHand saved [Engine.PlayerController] DesiredFOV=85, and UCPlayerController's FOV became 85 instead of 90 after a restart: FOV, mouse scale and AutoFOV base all change); and a class dump bakes ~36 untouched script defaults into the ini, so a later rebuilt package's default would never reach that player.

Decisions and alternatives. A class save writes a property only when it differs from a baseline (the ini's value, else the default recorded at load, else the superclass default), and never a globalconfig property another class deliberately sets; an instance save (a server object's state) writes the whole instance as before; SAVECONFIGKEYS writes exactly the named keys. The rule between profile and ini: the profile owns everything it applies (pad invert and sensitivities, auto-centre, handicap, weapons, character, name; applied after the ini at every player start, so an ini value never wins over a loaded profile); the ini owns what the profile has no field for (the mouse, the weapon hand, the FOV).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Changes the heart of UObject::SaveConfig (native C++ callers take the old path; only the script path and SAVECONFIGKEYS are filtered). Arrays and maps are not written by a class save. Cost measured: 995 class records, 1.5 ms in total at start, about 50 KB. Known gap (BLOCKERS.md): the character of a launch that names no class comes from [DefaultPlayer] Class, not the active profile.

Status. Measured: sc-saveconfig.sh 91 PASS, 0 FAIL (notebook gate; coordinator a9..a12 91/0): exactly one key written for SetWeaponHand, FOV still 90 after a restart, ScriptSaveConfig=False writes nothing and logs off, the profile wins where both hold a value.

Off switch. [Engine.Engine] ScriptSaveConfig=False (Epic's no-op; SAVECONFIGKEYS answers off; the mouse commands and the D-9 page then last for the session only).

#30.16 The KEYBOARD / MOUSE page: rebinding in game, mouse slider (D-9)

What the player/owner sees. OPTIONS (from the main menu and from the pause menu) has KEYBOARD / MOUSE between CONTROLLER and GAMEPLAY, on PC only: a mouse sensitivity slider (0.1-10 with the value and the degrees per count shown), INVERT MOUSE LOOK, and a list of 33 actions with two key columns. Enter, pad A or a click on a cell then any key or mouse button binds it; Escape cancels, Backspace/Delete clear; a key another action had is swapped (the bottom line says what moved); White = DEFAULTS (our DefUser.ini), Black = CANCEL, B/Escape = save and back. Pad buttons are never listed or changed (they belong to the profile's controller layout).

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.16.

Where.

  • Script, the rebuilt UCMenu.u (commits 07d0d06 UCMenu: KEYBOARD / MOUSE settings page, first version, 3219806, c401614, c9982ee):
    • UCMenu/Classes/UC2Settings_Keys.uc (new, 1,246 lines): actions with their exact DefUser.ini commands :1159-1191 (MENU on Escape and CONSOLE shown but fixed); :372- LoadBindings reads the live input object (GetRawInputObject().GetPropertyText) or, for DEFAULTS, GetConfigValue("DefUser","Engine.Input",<Key>) (:394); :428-460 SaveBindings: SET Input <Key> <cmd> for each changed key, then SAVECONFIGKEYS Engine.Input <keys> in batches of 16; :505- LoadMouse, :546-580 SaveMouse (SAVECONFIGKEYS UCGameplay.UCPlayerInput ...); :586- SliderClick (Epic's GUISlider has no mouse handling); :661-691 KeyCaptured (Escape cancels, Backspace/Delete clear, pad buttons and the console key refused); :693- AssignKey (swap rules).
    • UCMenu/Classes/KeyBindingPanel.uc (new, 323 lines): the list (a Settings_ListPanel like WeaponPriorityPanel), rows drawn by the page through OnDrawItem.
    • UCMenu/Classes/UC2Settings_Main.uc:18, :65-66, :106-108, :130, :183-193: the button, added only when !Level.IsXBox().
  • Epic's code changed: Source/GUI/Src/UnGUICore.cpp:1025-1029, :1158-1193 (commit 4567556 GUI: a raw key capture takes only keys pressed while it runs). Bug fix for UC2's menus, which act on presses: Epic's OnNeedRawKeyPress path delivered the RELEASE of the Enter that started the capture as the new key, and SDL's auto-repeat presses too.
  • Epic's code changed, build tooling: Source/Core/Src/UnObj.cpp:4485-4490 (commit e2b3a8c). Epic's bug: a conformed package save sorted as many imports as were added, but starting at 0, reordering existing imports; first met when D-9's package added imports. Runs only in ucc make/strip; the game never saves packages.
  • Engine pieces used: SET Input (Epic's StaticExec/GlobalSetProperty), SAVECONFIGKEYS (30.15).
  • Test channel keys, probe4 (84c3082). Tools: tools/dev/d9-rebind.sh (menu, match, restart, nodefaults, pad, actions, look); tools/dev/mkassets-uc2.sh lists UCMenu (69ae15d).
  • Frontend: opt-in packages/e2e/uc2-wasm-keys.spec.js (5a1f7b8), manifest for the rebuilt package (5c87870), README "Keys and mouse settings in game".

How the parts fit. The page is script (UCMenu.u); the capture fix and the key-by-key save are native; the mouse fields are the rebuilt UCGameplay.u's properties. Without the rebuilt UCMenu.u: no button; keys can still be changed in User.ini or with SET Input + SAVECONFIGKEYS Engine.Input <Key> on the console. Without the capture fix: the starting Enter would bind itself. Without SAVECONFIGKEYS (or with ScriptSaveConfig=False): changes last for the session.

Why. Owner (addendum): "We need to have an extra IN GAME option to rebind controls. This is the most absolute last of them all." Plus the mouse slider D-6 left undone.

Decisions and alternatives. Edit a copy, write only the changed keys; two columns (more keys shown as "+n"); conflicts swap rather than clear; a key bound to a command no row lists (Epic's SwitchWeapon N, Taunt Taunt1) is simply taken and the hint says what it was; defaults read from DefUser.ini at run time; a DefUser.ini without [Engine.Input] keeps every key ("DEFAULT KEYS NOT FOUND").

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.16.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. A new UI of about 1,570 lines of UnrealScript in Epic's style that we maintain. The cell order is recomputed after a restart (bindings unchanged). The engine writes a key as j (FName spelling; cosmetic). The console key during a capture opens the console. Owner choices still open: C/V order, Space dodge, swap vs clear (LOG.md).

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.16.

Status. Measured: d9-rebind.sh 132 PASS, 0 FAIL (logs/d9/final/summary.txt; coordinator 119/0 in a12): slider by mouse and keyboard, capture by click and Enter, swaps, clearing, defaults, cancel, pad capture refused, J jumps after the rebind and a restart, mouse sensitivity 5.0 doubles the turn, invert flips; browser keys spec 1 passed (19/19 steps: rebinding and sensitivity survive a closed tab).

Off switch. None (the button is absent on the Xbox only).

#30.17 The script compiler on Linux: ucc-bin make and the proof against the shipped .u (M14)

What the player/owner sees. Nothing directly. The owner gets a way to change UnrealScript: ucc-bin make on the native 32-bit Linux build rebuilds all 17 script packages from Source/<Pkg>/Classes the way Epic's build machine did, and a tool proves the result equal to the shipped packages plus Epic's 30 load-time patches. Every .u change in this section was built with it.

Where.

  • New code: Source/Editor/CMakeLists.txt (Epic's Editor package built static, GPackage=GEditorPackage, UC2_STATIC_CLASS_ACCESSORS=1); Source/Editor/Src/UC2EditorRegistrants.cpp (registers Editor's 82 classes and 2 native tables in ucc-bin; verifyclasses checks the Editor layouts too); Source/Editor/Src/UC2EpicClassOrder.h (generated by tools/dev/epic-class-order.py). Commits 93686c0 Editor: builds natively and ucc-bin make compiles all 17 script packages, 586600b, c3c7397, 434ac53, d2d1648.
  • Epic's Editor code changed (portability and reproduction; each from a compile error or a byte difference, scripts.md steps 1-2b): Editor/Inc/Perlin.h (register INT), Editor/Inc/Editor.h:282 (GCC_PACK(4) on AutosaveTimeMinutes: GCC packed it into a bitfield word, every later member was 4 bytes off and make crashed at exit), :613 (forward declaration), Editor/Inc/EditorClasses.h (first members tagged), Editor/Src/UnVisi.cpp (pointer-to-member syntax), UAnalyzeMeshesCommandlet.cpp (#undef MAXFLOAT), TTFontImport.cpp:8- (GDI TrueType import only under MSVC), UnParams.cpp:22- (parsers Engine also defines, compiled once), UnEdSrv.cpp:447, :527, :1705 (#exec file names split at \ too), UMakeCommandlet.cpp:34, :97-98, :116, :207-212 (.uc files in NTFS order; native defaults refreshed as a DLL load did; -CONFORMNOCONFIG), UnScrCom.cpp:7298-7408 (classes compiled in Epic's DLL registration order: the scripts only compile in that order, e.g. UCWeapon.uc uses UCPawn.EWeaponAnimType; -NOEPICORDER turns it off; UC2RefreshNativeDefaultsLikeDllLoad :7377), UStripSourceCommandlet.cpp (NTFS order, -CONFORM), UnEdExp.cpp (the C++ header exporter's GCC_PACK rules).
  • Epic's Core/Engine code changed for it:
    • Source/Core/Inc/UnObjBas.h:475-478 static IMPLEMENT_CLASS accessors (Editor only).
    • Source/Core/Src/UnClass.cpp:370, :399 bug fix of our static link: native UStructs were constructed on the heap with garbage ScriptTextCRC/StructFlags (Epic's DLLs used zeroed static storage), so dependency records carried garbage CRCs.
    • Source/Core/Src/UnObj.cpp:3629 real Linux bug, also in the game: a package name taken from a file name now splits at \ too (#exec obj load file=..\Textures\T_HUD.utx named the package "").
    • Source/Core/Src/UnAnsi.cpp:510-532 appAtoi = MSVC's algorithm (wraps modulo 2^32; glibc saturates), found by const INPUT_All = 4294967295. Engine-wide: an out-of-range ini integer now wraps as on Windows/Xbox.
    • Source/Core/Src/UnObj.cpp:4485-4490 the conform import sort (D-9, 30.16).
    • Source/Engine/Src/UnScriptPatcher.cpp:291-308 a rebuilt package (not the first-generation file a patch was made for) is left unpatched, logged ScriptPatcher: <Pkg> is not the package the patch was made for ...; applied patches log ScriptPatcher: <Pkg> patched (...) (:346).
  • Source/UCC links Editor when the target exists; root CMakeLists.txt:430-435: Editor is built for 32-bit Linux only (its hand-written UEditorEngine mirrors script's int Pad[8] with four pointers; MinGW not tried).
  • UC2's backtick preprocessor (Editor/Src/FTextFilter.cpp, `include, `log, `logd, Core/Classes/Avaris.huc) is unchanged: Epic's build defined neither debug (only -debug) nor FINAL_RELEASE (only the Xbox launch configs), and ours does the same, so `log compiles to a log() call and `logd to nothing, byte-identical to the shipped bytecode. The 0x02 glyph bytes in 37 .uc strings pass through unchanged.
  • Tools: tools/dev/script-build.sh (191 lines; stages assemble make strip compare; trees run/m14* or run/d<N>*; --headers exports C++ headers into the tree only, never Source/), tools/dev/script-diff.py (1,163 lines; parses every export with the v151 serialisers and compares field by field and token by token; per-function file and patch table), tools/dev/upkg151.py, tools/dev/m14-scripts-check.sh (the game on rebuilt packages: verify, menu, a match, controls, audio, effects, map census, movies, net, leftovers), tools/dev/mkrun-uc2.sh UC2_SCRIPTS= (a run tree with rebuilt packages). BUILD.md "Scripts (M14)".

How the build works (script-build.sh, reproducing Source/BuildProcess/sync_avaris.pl): the tree hard-links the asset tree's content, copies System/ without the 17 EditPackages, puts the shipped .u in GUIRes/ (conform target, taken from epic-sources/System since 8161ead, because the asset tree now holds rebuilt files), symlinks <Pkg>/Classes to Source/, and compiles with Epic's Xbox inis (DefaultXbox.ini/DefUserXbox.ini) as ucc-bin make -nobind -CONFORMNOCONFIG -noh; then ucc-bin editor.stripsource -CONFORM with the PC inis and the retail disc's Characters.ini/Gametypes.ini/Maplists.ini (from reference/iso-extract), keeping UCMenu.u unstripped as Epic shipped it (it still carries Epic's retail UCMenu source text); then script-diff.py.

Why. PLAN.md D-1 ("build ucc/Editor, recompile the scripts, prove the bytecode against the shipped .u, retire ScriptPatches") so that the addendum's script changes (D-4, D-6, D-9, saveconfig) are possible without guessing. engine-ut2004 had no working ucc make on Unix to copy.

Decisions and alternatives. Reproduce Epic's exact pipeline (conform, Xbox inis for make, PC inis for strip) so that an export-by-export comparison is possible, rather than "it compiles". -CONFORMNOCONFIG: conforming without loading the ini into defaults (otherwise GameEngine.MainMenuClass would default to StartMenu, not UC2MainMenu).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Scripts can be built only on the native 32-bit Linux build (not Windows, not 64-bit, not the web).
  • The strip stage wants reference/iso-extract (falls back with a note: a few config defaults then differ) and #exec resources come from epic-sources.
  • Two builds differ in the InternalTime stamp of 78 #exec-imported textures (by design); everything else is deterministic.
  • 85 compiler warnings (Epic's scripts; e.g. locals used before assignment in GUIController.uc:3365, :3387), recorded, not touched.
  • The C++ headers are compared, never regenerated (our PTRINT 64-bit placeholders would be lost).
  • A generated class-order table to keep in step with the sources if classes are added (D-9's two new classes compiled without touching it).

Status. Measured (M14 gate, scripts.md): Success - 0 error(s), all 17 packages; compare: 0 unexplained differences (Core, UCAnnouncements, UnrealEd, UWeb byte-identical; 9,621 of 10,456 functions byte-identical, 813 differ only in line positions in the 17 classes changed after retail); 29 of the 30 patches are already in the source; m14-scripts-check.sh 11 PASS on all 17 rebuilt packages (census 58/58 as shipped, net 16/0). Since then each D-task's build: Success - 0 error(s), 85 warning(s), and the compare shows problems only in the changed packages (coordinator a12: UCGameplay 18, UCMenu 5, all intended).

Off switch. Not applicable (tooling). The game runs on whatever .u the tree holds.

#30.18 The .uc files that changed, the rebuilt packages, the asset-tree overlay, and ScriptPatches

The nine .uc files that differ from Epic's (git diff --stat 7633696 4813dd9 -- 'Source/*/Classes/*'):
