Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.18.

What each rebuilt package differs in (script-diff.py against the shipped packages): GUI.u: one function (GUIController.SetProfilePlayerName) plus the two config flags; UCGameplay.u: 18 "problems", all in the changed code (PlayerInput, Get*AdhesionAdjust, the new functions and properties, UpdateRotation, CalcBehindView, the training states) and 6 findings (M14's 5 + the new defaults); UCMenu.u: 5 problems (the appended exports of the two new classes, the btn_Advanced -> btn_Keys property link, InitOptions, CloseComplete, Free). Every other package compares as the shipped one; at D-9 a fresh build gave GUI.u and UCGameplay.u byte-identical to the asset tree's copies.

How the asset tree carries them. tools/dev/mkassets-uc2.sh:63-84 (asset tree v2; commits e087510, 8161ead, 69ae15d): UC2_REBUILT_PACKAGES (default GUI UCGameplay UCMenu) are copied from assets/uc2-scripts/System (the coordinator copies them there from a script-build tree) over System/, the shipped files are put aside in assets/uc2-shipped/System (never deleted), System/SCRIPTS.txt lists each rebuilt package with the first 16 hex digits of its sha256, and MANIFEST.sha256 is regenerated. A package missing from the rebuilt folder keeps the shipped file. Run trees take an overlay with mkrun-uc2.sh UC2_SCRIPTS=<dir>. Frontend manifests follow the tree (4419272 GUI.u, 8510f59 and 9e1f22b UCGameplay.u, 5c87870 UCMenu.u); Core.u is unchanged, so the frontend's asset fingerprint (sha256 of Core.u) still holds.

Network. A rebuilt package keeps the shipped GUID and adds a generation (a conformed save). UCGameplay is a server package: native dedicated servers and all clients (native and web) must switch together; a peer with the shipped file and one with the rebuilt file were never tested together and are not supported. GUI and UCMenu are client-only. Evidence: net 16/0 in M14 (all 17 packages rebuilt) and the coordinator's native net dedicated2/listen/travel 24/0 on main after the UCGameplay.u switch (LOG.md D-6).

ScriptPatches status. Source/Engine/Inc/ScriptPatches.h (Epic's 30 load-time patches: 21 bytecode patches, 9 appended objects) is unchanged and still applied at load to the shipped packages it targets; the patcher skips the three rebuilt packages (their source already contains the patches). "Retire ScriptPatches" (D-1's wording) is therefore done only for GUI, UCGameplay and UCMenu; Engine, Editor, UnrealGame, UCInventory and UCMutators still run patched shipped files. Two patch facts change behaviour with a rebuilt package: Epic's CTFFlag.home patch is defective (wrong label, stale label-table offset), so on the shipped UCGameplay.u a flag returning home never runs its Begin: code (Sleep(0.1); CheckTouching()), while the rebuilt UCGameplay.u, now in the asset tree, runs it: an enemy standing on the base gets the returning flag after 0.1 s instead of on the next touch (a CTF gameplay difference from the shipped game, by inference also from the Xbox title update; not tested in a game); ConnectionFailureMenu can probe its Timer (UCMenu, client-side). The UCMutators patches were never exercised in a game (no test loads UCMutators).

Regarding the owner's earlier instruction to leave .u changes to the end. PLAN.md's Phase D allowed script changes from D-1 on, and the three packages were switched in during D-4, D-6 and D-9 (LOG.md 2026-10-07/08). The table at the top of this section lists what reverting to the shipped packages would cost; the mouse look is the only feature that then stops working outright, and it would need DefUser.ini and UC2InputMigrate.cpp reverted with it.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30.18.

#Open issues in this area

  1. O-11, third-person character jitter: fixed after this snapshot (main c59c02d, section 50). The cause was indeed D-6's rigid mouse camera drawing the frame from the view computed before the pawn moved; the fix is in UCGameplay.u only.
  2. O-14, "keyboard bindings being backwards": no example from the owner yet; the audit found nothing inverted (30.8). Candidates if it was meant: the C/V order, arrows turning instead of strafing. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30, open issue 2.)
  3. No physical pad has been tried (all pad evidence is SDL's virtual controller); pads are off on the web (UseJoystick=False, browser Gamepad API untested; frontend gamepad support is E-3); real-pad sessions are planned from CI builds (E-5).
  4. The mouse's feel was never judged by a person. Native OS pointer acceleration under SDL's relative mode was not measured; Linux browsers apply the desktop's acceleration under pointer lock; headless tests ran without pointer lock.
  5. The pad's slow turns at high frame rates (Epic's integer rotation per tick): just past the dead zone nothing turns below about 0.335 deflection at 60 fps. Carrying the fraction would fix it but changes Epic's pad arithmetic: owner decision. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30, open issue 5.)
  6. Owner choices left on the D-9 page: C/V order, Space dodging while moving (vs JUMP ONLY), swap vs clear on a key conflict. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 30, open issue 6.)
  7. Not done, needs script: button glyphs that show the bound key (the hints still show Xbox A/B/X/Y), hover on list rows, an on-screen console output area, camera movement smoothing for the mouse.
  8. Split screen on PC (E-1): Start on a second pad does not join; the UI box has code for split viewports, never run.
  9. The character of a launch that names no class comes from [DefaultPlayer] Class, not the active profile (BLOCKERS.md).
  10. Rebuilt-package consequences: CTF's returning flag behaves as the source says, not as the shipped+patched game (not tested in a game); shipped and rebuilt UCGameplay.u peers must not be mixed; the rebuilt UCMenu.u was never tried with the shipped UCGameplay.u; ScriptPatches.h still patches five shipped packages; the UCMutators patches were never exercised.
  11. The script compiler exists only in the native 32-bit Linux build.
  12. Tests: the menu scenarios are timing-sensitive under software GL on a loaded machine (frame-counted menu animations), the lock-on checks are flaky (bot positions), a click shorter than a frame can be lost (inferred).
  13. Smaller: the teleport effect's FOV write-back (D-8) never exercised; the frontend's shared AutoFOV e2e not registered for uc2; the crosshair art sits up to 4 px off centre at 4K (Epic's art); ConsoleKey=9 cannot be chosen any more; native and web keep profiles under different paths (alternative: a directory wildcard in the frontend, then revert 22e9dbf); Windows input verified only under wine.

#40. Audio, movies, networking and the frontend

Code as of main 4813dd9 (snapshot /home/ion/WorkUC2/engine-uc2-report), baseline 7633696 (Epic's tree as imported). Frontend: ufrontend branch uc2 at 5654da2 against 4affbe7 (its base on origin/main; the branch has since moved on with an unrelated jitter spec, 156d736, which is not covered). path:line are snapshot line numbers. Notebooks behind this section (docs/porting/analysis/): audio.md (M8), video.md (M9), web-audio.md (O-1, O-2, O-4), network.md (net-join, net-loss), webrtc.md (E-2), design.md (audio and video design notes), plus LOG.md and PLAN.md ("Owner review of 2026-10-06"). Where a notebook and the code disagree the code is used and the difference is said (there are four, listed at the end of the summary).

Names to know: XACT is the Xbox audio tool and runtime Epic's game used (sound banks .xsb, wave banks .xwb, the project file Sounds/Avaris.xap); cooked means converted offline into files of ours; O-1/O-2/O-4, M8/M9/M11, E-2 are the owner-review tasks and milestones of PLAN.md.

#Summary: the five things to know

  1. Sound is Epic's Xbox sound data, re-encoded offline and played by a new device that copies XACT's rules. tools/cook-audio.py reads the XACT project text (Avaris.xap, 248,057 lines) and the source WAVs and writes Ogg Vorbis packs plus cue tables (182 MB instead of the Xbox banks' 376 MB). Source/UC2ALAudio (new, C++ only, OpenAL Soft) implements UAudioSubsystem the way XboxAudio/Src/XACTAudioSubsystem.cpp did: cue names, 2D/3D choice, distance cull, categories, variation playlists, loops, ambient sounds, music, pause. No .uc file changed for any of it; Epic's engine changed by one virtual function (OpenMovieAudio). It is measured, never compared with a real Xbox: 39 numeric checks pass (gain, pan, pitch, loops, playlists, reverb decay), but the absolute mix level is only right to "a dB or two", HRTF is not reproduced, and 19 source WAVs do not exist in the tree (the Xbox banks hold them; here 15 cues play nothing).
  2. Zone reverb is on, which the Xbox did not do. Epic's NotifyZoneChange is inside #if 0; the Xbox ran one fixed Default reverb in every map. We switch an OpenAL EFX reverb preset per zone (26 I3DL2 presets from the XDK). The owner decided (2026-10-06) to keep it on. In DM-Azure (the owner hears reverb there on the Xbox) all 18 zones are Default, so our device does there what the Xbox did; in the 34 of 58 maps with other presets it differs from the Xbox by that decision. The reverb's decay times are measured; its level against the Xbox is not known and nobody has listened to it side by side.
  3. Movies are cooked from XMV (WMV2) to Ogg Theora + Vorbis and played by a small, thread-free player. tools/cook-movies.py (not .sh) turns 110 XMV files into 186; Source/UC2Movie + Engine/Src/UnMovie.cpp (new) play the start-up logos, the ladder cinematics (?StartCinematic= / ?EndCinematic=) and every MovieMaterial texture, with the picture following the sound clock. Verified frame-for-frame against ffmpeg. Not done: the animated loading movie (a static loading screen instead), attract mode, the Xbox legal/rating screens; the non-English sound tracks rest on Epic's code, with no reference recording.
  4. Network play is Epic's code with its latent bugs fixed, not a new transport. IpDrv/TcpNetDriver.cpp has zero changed lines. A client could not join at all (an assertion Epic's Xbox build compiled out) and, once it could, a lost first handshake packet or any loss after ~16,000 packets was never resent (a silent hang; stale scores minutes into a lossy match). Seven fixes sit in shared engine files (UnScriptPatcher, UnCoreNet, UnConn, UnPenLev, UnChan, UCC, UnUnix). Natively: 89 PASS, 0 FAIL in the coordinator's full run at the net-loss merge, 32 PASS again after E-2 (dedicated and listen servers, 1-2 clients, travel, disconnect, 5 % loss + 100 ms lag, a 10-minute soak, a wine server). Never tried on a real network, with team games, package download or more than 3 clients.
  5. The browser has the same sound device and a new network driver. Web audio is the same UC2ALAudio on the 10-p/openal-soft-web fork's Web Audio backend (O-2; plays in Chromium, measured by an analyser, not by ears). Web multiplayer is Source/WebRTCDrv (E-2), ported from engine-ut2004: one host and one guest play a match over the frontend's WebRTC data channels, from the launcher's host form and invite link (2 browser tests pass). Not done: in-game server discovery (UC2's System Link browser is UDP broadcast), two guests, travel over WebRTC. The frontend uc2 target (targets/uc2, 42 commits) is mostly data; three of its changes touch code shared with UT2004/UT99/Unreal 1 (package-version 145-151 readers in the glue, the disabled name field network.playerName.ownedBy, and a CI gate that now forbids the literal uc2 in generic source).

Where notebook and code disagree: (a) the lead task and PLAN.md say tools/cook-movies.sh; it is tools/cook-movies.py. (b) audio.md says category volumes "below 0.1 = -100 dB silence"; the code (and Epic's CalcVolume) clamps 0..0.1 to -10 dB and only a volume of ~0 is silent (40.2). (c) WebRTCDrv.h and webrtc.md say the data channel is unordered / no retransmits; the glue's channel the browser tests measured is ordered=true, maxRetx=null (reliable). (d) targets/uc2/README.md still describes the first-light state in places (593 files / 1,094.7 MB, "audio and a net driver needed next"); the manifest has 1,224 files / 1,991 MB.

#Which layer carries what

Frontend target (40.16-40.20) none none n/a targets/uc2, glue v151 readers, e2e specs n/a

#Features

#40.1 The audio cook: Avaris.xap + WAVs -> Ogg packs and cue tables

What the player/owner sees. Nothing directly. It produces the files the game plays; without them the game is silent (and says so once in the log and on the console).

Where.

  • New code: tools/cook-audio.py (1,135 lines, commit ec082f4). Strict parser for the .xap text grammar, a schema of every block and key (anything unknown goes to the report: 0 on Avaris.xap), an .xsb/.xwb cross-check reader, and the encoder driver (ffmpeg libvorbis, -q:a 4 effects and speech, -q:a 5 music). Options: --epic, --out (default <WorkUC2>/assets/uc2-cooked), --jobs, --banks, --no-encode, --quality, --music-quality, --relink-localised (see 40.8).
  • Outputs (formats owned by us, specified in audio.md "Cooked format"): Sounds/<WaveBankFile>.uc2wb (182, text index per wave bank, localised banks _deu/_fra/_ita/_spa), .ogp (161, one Ogg Vorbis stream per wave, concatenated per wave bank), Sounds/<SoundBank>.uc2cues (78, text: sounds, tracks, events, variations, cues), Music/<Track>.ogg (23), Sounds/cook-report.txt, .cache/ keyed by source SHA-1.
  • Asset tree: tools/dev/mkassets-uc2.sh hard-links Sounds/ Music/ Movies/ Textures/Movies/ from assets/uc2-cooked into assets/uc2 ("asset tree v1", 8cbea58); tools/dev/mkrun-uc2.sh does the same for run trees and copies libopenal.so.1 beside uc2.bin.
  • Frontend: targets/uc2/file-manifest.json lists the cooked files (Sounds 421 files / 124 MB, Music 23 / 65 MB); manifestExclude drops the cook's by-products (Movies/.cook/**, */cook-report.txt, ...).
  • Script/ini: none.

How the parts fit. The cook is offline and run by us; the shipped asset tree already contains its output. The runtime never reads the Xbox .xsb/.xwb; reading them at run time is milestone M16 ("end of project").

Why. The engine's audio interface (Engine/Inc/UnAudio.h) is shaped around named XACT cues and had only an Xbox implementation; the PC build ran Engine.NullAudioDevice. Owner decision (PLAN, 2026-10-04): "Cook from sources (xap + WAV -> OGG + cue table). Runtime Xbox bank loading = end-of-project."

Decisions and alternatives.

  • Units decoded from the data, not guessed: volumes in hundredths of a dB, pitch in cents, a sound group's volume is baked into each sound (confirmed on all 2,428 sounds: stored volume = -512 - (Volume + group Volume)/16).
  • One .ogp per wave bank (the engine's loading unit: the Xbox registered a bank at first use). Rejected: 6,777 per-wave files; one 122 MB archive (all fetched up front on the web).
  • Quality q4 for effects (SDR 20.2 dB against the Xbox ADPCM's 16.8-27.8 dB on weapons; 12 % of PCM size against the Xbox's 28 %), q5 for music. Measured on 24 samples; SDR flatters ADPCM, so this is "at least as good", not "identical".
  • Loop points live in the index (loop=start,length), not in the Ogg (Vorbis has none).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • 19 wave entries have no source WAV (the shipped .xwb has them): A_Combo 4, A_Gameplay 3, A_Weapon 1, A_WeaponRace 1, A_Announcer_spa 3 (Congratulations, FinishHer, FinishIt), 7 level ambients. 15 cues have no playable wave (e.g. Combo_BladeRush_Travel, Gib_Explosion_LI, seven level ambient loops) and play nothing. Decoding them from the .xwb was considered and not done (the brief said not to invent data; open in BLOCKERS.md: "decode those 19 from the .xwb ... or leave them out"). (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 40.1.)
  • A_Vehicle.xsb/.xwb (31 vehicle cues) ship but are not in the project, so they are not cooked. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 40.1.)
  • One source differs from the shipped bank: A_ChemicalDawn entry 7 (Machine_Generator03): the tree's WAV is 55,808 frames, the Xbox's 193,472; the cook uses ours with its loop region clamped. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 40.1.)
  • Vorbis q4 is lossy; the encode starts from the project's source WAVs, not from the Xbox's ADPCM, so there is one lossy generation, not two.
  • The script finds bin/capped by a fixed relative path (<WorkUC2>/bin/capped) and ffmpeg on PATH: it is a workspace tool, not a portable one.

Status. Measured (logs/m8/cook-2.out): 182 wave banks, 7,181 entries, 78 sound banks, 2,428 sounds, 2,438 cues, 6,777 distinct sources, 0 encode failures, 0 unknown schema keys; 78/78 sound banks agree with the shipped .xsb (names, counts), 181/182 wave banks agree with the .xwb entry by entry (the one difference is the Machine generator above). Every cooked wave has the Xbox wave's length to within one 64-frame ADPCM block (a 2.9 ms worst case) except that one. Inferences without documentation, listed so the owner can veto them: playlist type numbering (consistent with the data: the 43 "no immediate repeat" events are footsteps, swings, pain), priority direction (lower value = more important), "Recurring = fade".

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 40.1.

Off switch. None (offline tool). A tree without Sounds/ simply has no sound.

#40.2 The audio device: XACT behaviour on OpenAL Soft

What the player/owner sees. Menu music, the announcer, weapons, footsteps, pickups, ambient loops and taunts in the player's language, in stereo with panning and distance falloff, at the Music/FX/Voice volumes of the options menu. With no usable sound device the game still starts, silent.

Where.

  • New code, Source/UC2ALAudio/ (CMake Source/UC2ALAudio/CMakeLists.txt; commits 0b216d7 the device, 3ab01aa decoded waves cut to the index length, 6aa5fc2 timestamped cue log + -NOEFX, c37b1c3 music headroom):
    • Inc/UC2ALAudio.h - class UUC2ALAudioSubsystem : UAudioSubsystem with the config properties of the switches table, the commandlets UC2AudioCheck, UC2AudioSelfTest, UC2ZoneSurvey.
    • Src/UC2ALAudioSubsystem.cpp (2,320 lines) - the XACT rules. Anchors: banks :37-45 (23 persistent sound banks, XACT :215-237), cue-name resolution ResolveCue :405 (Bank.Group.Cue -> Bank\Cue), GetWaveBank :265 (<Bank>_<lang> then <Bank>), voice pool AllocVoice :788, variation picker ChooseVariation :830, per-voice gain/pitch/position UpdateVoice :951, StartTrack :1002, PlaySoundCue :1322 (2D/3D choice :1368-1391, distance cull :1392-1404), GetSoundCueDuration :1461, GetSoundCueRadius :1481, categories :1500-1509, ambient cues UpdateAmbientCues :1719, Update :1792, NotifyLevelChange :1865, NotifyPauseChange :1946, console commands Exec :1982 (UC2AUDIO STATS|ZONE <n>|PLAY <cue>, XACT MUSIC SONG=, XACT SOUNDTEST).
    • Src/UC2AudioData.cpp - readers for the cooked text files and Vorbis decoding from memory (vorbisfile callbacks).
    • Src/UC2ALDevice.cpp - OpenAL binding (dlopen of libopenal.so.1/OpenAL32.dll natively, linked symbols on the web), the I3DL2 preset table :186-214, EFX loader UC2ALLoadReverb :227.
  • Epic's code changed: Engine/Inc/UnAudio.h:115 one virtual OpenMovieAudio (default NULL; used by movies, 40.10); Engine/Src/UnScript.cpp:5131 ALevelInfo::NotifyPauseChange lost a stray FORCEINLINE (a function defined in a .cpp and called from elsewhere does not link with GCC; a build fix). Epic's call sites (UnEngine.cpp:73 InitAudio, UnLevAct.cpp zone notification, UnGame.cpp update/level change) are unchanged.
  • Registration: Source/SDLLaunch/Src/SDLLaunch.cpp:521-523 and Source/UCC/Src/UCC.cpp:556-557 (AUTO_INITIALIZE_REGISTRANTS_UC2ALAUDIO), Source/SDLLaunch/CMakeLists.txt:30; root CMakeLists.txt:226-350 builds the vendored Ogg/Vorbis (Thirdparty/Vorbis, libvorbis 1.0 from engine-ut2004) and fetches OpenAL Soft.
  • ini/data: see the switches table; script (.uc): none.
  • Tests: 40.7.

How the parts fit. Epic's script calls PlaySoundCue(actor, 'A_Weapon.RL_Fire', ...), PlayMusic, SetCategoryVolume; the engine routes them to UAudioSubsystem; our class looks the cue up in the cooked tables, chooses a variation, takes a voice (an OpenAL source), and each frame (Update) refreshes gain, pitch and position of every live voice. Without the cooked data the class loads and refuses every cue; without OpenAL it loads and plays nothing; the engine falls back to the null device only if Init() returns 0 (it never does: "sound is off" keeps the object).

The rules in one place (what the numbers are).

  • 2D vs 3D: no actor = fire-and-forget 2D. An actor that is a viewer's player controller, or is owned by its view target, plays 2D (no panning); anything else is 3D for the closest viewport (XACT :2176-2203).
  • Distance cull (non-ambient 3D cues without a "Volume" parameter): skipped when radius < dist * (1 + cues/15), cues = live cue instances; radius = 2*MinDist + MinDist*(2/Rolloff). A sound with no 3D block gets DirectSound's defaults (min 1, rolloff 1) so its radius is 4 and it is culled beyond 4 units on another actor (an inference; see risks).
  • Volume of a voice, in hundredths of a dB (mB): sound volume (+ its group's) + variation volume + Set Volume events - 600 (6 dB per-voice headroom, unless "Headroom Boost") + category + RPC curve + distance, clamped to [-10000, 0] (DirectSound cannot amplify), then AL_GAIN = 10^(mB/2000). OpenAL's own distance model is off (AL_NONE) and doppler is off, as on the Xbox (XACT :500); positions only steer panning.
  • Category (Music/FX/Voice; VoiceChat is stored only): 1000 * log10(clamp(v, 0.1, 1)) mB, i.e. v = 1 is 0 dB, 0.4 is -4.0 dB, 0.1 is -10.0 dB, and everything between ~0 and 0.1 is also -10 dB; only a value below KINDA_SMALL_NUMBER is silent (-100 dB). This is Epic's CalcVolume (XACT :864-873), kept; it makes the options sliders gentle (the notebook's "below 0.1 = silence" is wrong). Initial values come from the default profile object (Music 0.40, FX 0.80, Voice 0.80) until the profile or the options page sets them.
  • Distance: DirectSound's rolloff Min / (Min + Rolloff*(d - Min)), d clamped to [Min, Max], computed in software.
  • Pitch: cents, AL_PITCH = 2^(cents/1200).
  • Voices and stealing: 64 sources (config). When all are busy the least important sound (highest priority value; at equal priority the oldest) is stopped for a more important one; a new sound that is less important than every voice is dropped ("no voice" in the cue log). Direction (lower value = more important) is inferred from the data (announcer 40-50, weapons 100, footsteps 150, taunts 225, ambience 240-254).
  • Variations: six playlist types implemented; the data uses Random (2,515 events), Random-no-repeat (43) and Ordered (1). Loops: infinite on one wave = AL_LOOPING (partial loop region through AL_SOFT_loop_points); finite or "new variation per loop" = one buffer queued per pass.
  • Ambient: static AmbientSound actors are managed once a second: the 5 nearest within their cue radius play (XACT :2010-2120); an ambient cue replaces the actor's previous one.
  • Pause: all live cues pause and their schedules shift; music keeps playing (XACT :338-351). Level change: everything stops, music too, non-persistent banks unload.
  • 3D voices are mono: stereo waves are decoded a second time as a downmix, per wave, on first 3D use.
  • Not played (counted only): low-pass events (4 in the project), LFO (1), pitch LFO (1), envelope generator (1), mix bins (5), loop start (1), parametric EQ (15 sounds), LFE send. Cones have no effect in the data (outside volume 0).

Why. The brief: PC had no audio driver; design.md "Audio design" lists the behaviours to reproduce. PLAN.md listed engine-ut2004's ALAudio as reusable for device set-up; the cue logic had to be new because the interface is XACT-shaped, and the web build settings follow engine-ut2004's (O-2).

Decisions and alternatives.

  • XACT behaviour reproduced, not simplified to "play a WAV": the data (2,428 sounds, 2,559 tracks, RPC curves, variation weights) only makes sense through XACT's model.
  • Software distance attenuation with OpenAL's own distance model switched off (AL_NONE): the DirectSound formula is summed with the other terms and clamped at 0 dB as DirectSound does (checked to 0.00 dB at six distances).
  • Dynamic loading of OpenAL natively (dlopen), so a machine with no OpenAL runs the game silent and nothing of OpenAL runs before the engine's allocator exists.
  • Music as a streamed Ogg through a dedicated source, 4 x 32,768-frame buffers refilled once per frame on the game thread (8,192-frame buffers left audible gaps during long first-load frames under software GL: logs/m8/deadbolt-2).
  • One audio device object serves the movie player as well (the movie's sound must share the device, the volume and the capture path), hence OpenMovieAudio in Epic's interface.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Never compared with a real Xbox. OpenAL Soft's centred-source gain is -1.10 dB (3D) / -1.52 dB (2D) against XACT's unknown routing, so the whole mix may be a constant off by that order; the owner has been sent captures to listen to (LOG 2026-10-04); no reply is recorded.
  • No HRTF (the Xbox used "full HRTF" on 3D voices): plain stereo/surround panning; OpenAL Soft's own configuration (alsoft.conf) decides what a real device does. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 40.2.)
  • Inferred semantics (documentation does not say): priority direction, "Recurring" = fade, default weight of an unset variation (counted as 10,000 when others are set), cue radius of sounds with no 3D block (4), "Parameter controlled" playlist (takes the first; nothing in UC2 sets it). The non-3D radius means other players' combo activations and similar non-3D cues on remote actors are silent here; if the Xbox reported another radius they were audible there.
  • Voice stealing, split-screen listener placement (MakeRelativeToViewport equivalent), pause/resume and the Shuffle/Ordered from random/Parameter-controlled playlists are implemented but have never run in a recorded test (64 voices were never exhausted; no split-screen run; the data uses none of those playlists).
  • Memory: a wave pack (96 KB - 4.3 MB) is read whole on first use and stays resident with its decoded buffers while its bank is loaded; the 23 persistent banks never unload. Worst case is the cooked effects total (122 MB packed).
  • MaxVoices 64 against the Xbox's 252: a very busy fight can steal voices the Xbox would not have.
  • Each wave is decoded on the game thread at first play (a possible hitch at first use of a large wave; not measured).

Status. Measured: UC2ALAudio.UC2AudioCheck (all 7,141 cooked waves decode to exactly their indexed length, 78 cue tables / 2,438 cues, 0 failures; 2,417 cues resolve fully, 6 partly, 15 to nothing); UC2AudioSelfTest 39 checks, 0 failed (details in 40.7). Seen/heard by a person: nothing recorded. Not verified: absolute level against the Xbox, HRTF colour, stealing, split screen, pause.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 40.2.

Off switch. AudioDevice=Engine.NullAudioDevice in Avaris.ini, or -nosound for one run (Epic's switch). The device object then stays but plays nothing; without libopenal it also stays and plays nothing.

#40.3 Music

What the player/owner sees. The menu music (NuBattle) fades in over 3 seconds when the main menu opens; a level's music starts with the level; one song at a time.

Where. UC2ALAudioSubsystem.cpp: PlayMusic :1556, StopMusic :1625, StopAllMusic :1651, UpdateMusic :1656 (refill and fade each frame); buffers UC2_MUSIC_BUFFERS=4 x UC2_MUSIC_BUFFER_FRAMES=32768 (:29). Data: Music/<Track>.ogg (23 files: the 21 tracks named by the M_* wave banks plus AnubisFightsSelket and Theme), cue in A_Music.uc2cues. Epic's script calls (ClientSetMusic) are unchanged.

How the parts fit. The Xbox played the song as a streamed wave bank M_<Song>.xwb plus the cue <Song> of A_Music; we read the cue for the volume and the loop flag and stream the matching .ogg with libvorbis. Fade times are honoured (MusicFades=True) although the Xbox ignored them ("change the actual song cue").

Why. The M8 brief asked for fades (audio.md: "deliberate difference (brief): fades").

Decisions and alternatives. Music gets the same 6 dB per-voice headroom as every cue (found in a capture: it was 6 dB too loud before c37b1c3; the Xbox plays music as a cue of A_Music).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Fades are a deliberate deviation (MusicFades=False restores the Xbox). Decoding 32,768 frames of Vorbis on the game thread costs 5.8-6.8 ms on average and up to 16 ms in the browser (logged once per run: UC2Audio: music refill cost), one such hitch every ~0.74 s at 44.1 kHz; native cost was not recorded.

Status. Measured: tools/dev/audio-music-check.py finds the menu track in the capture by envelope correlation (0.84-0.87) and measures its mix gain: -19.02 dB against the expected -19.00 dB (= -900 mB sound

  • 600 mB headroom + category 0.40); a control track correlates 0.115. Web: music started 1.34 s (quiet machine) to 3.1-3.6 s (busy) after the device opened; 0-1 underruns of 1-3 ms in 12 s.

Off switch. MusicFades=False for the fades; no music: delete the Music/ folder or lower the Music slider.

#40.4 Zone reverb (EFX, the Xbox's I3DL2 presets) and the DM-Azure finding (O-4)

What the player/owner sees. Walking from a corridor into a hangar or underwater changes the room tone of 3D sounds (footsteps, gunfire of others, ambient loops): echo in a hangar, muffled underwater. In DM-Azure there is one fixed (Default) reverb, the one every map has as its base.

Where.

  • New code: UC2ALDevice.cpp:186-214 the 26 DSI3DL2_ENVIRONMENT_PRESET_* of the XDK's DSound.h in EZoneAudioEffect order (Engine/Classes/ZoneInfo.uc:56-84, unchanged), :227 UC2ALLoadReverb (mB -> gain, percent -> 0..1, clamped to EFX ranges; EAX reverb when the device has it, else the standard reverb); UC2ALAudioSubsystem.cpp:630-653 creates one auxiliary effect slot at start-up, :1917-1943 NotifyZoneChange (loads the preset of the zone; "unchanged" and "no EFX" are logged), :1090-1098 the per-voice send: 10^(I3DL2/2000) through a low-pass filter used as a gain, 3D voices only, none on 2D.
  • Epic's code: none changed. Epic's UnLevAct.cpp already calls NotifyZoneChange for the local player's pawn.
  • Tools: UC2ALAudio.UC2ZoneSurvey (UC2ZoneSurvey.cpp, ucc-bin UC2ALAudio.UC2ZoneSurvey [MAP=<name>]) lists every map's zones and presets; UC2AUDIO ZONE <n> forces a preset; -NOEFX / UseEFX=False is the dry comparison.
  • ini: ZoneReverb, UseEFX (switches table). Script: none.

How the parts fit. The map data (each ZoneInfo.ZoneAudioEffect, an enum) and each sound's "I3DL2 Level" (in the XACT project: 1,694 sounds at 0 mB = full send, 733 at -300 mB, 1 at -333 mB) are Epic's; the engine tells the device about zone changes of the local pawn; the device swaps the one reverb effect's parameters.

Why. The brief asked for zone reverb. The shipped Xbox code has the zone switch inside #if 0 (XACTAudioSubsystem.cpp NotifyZoneChange), so the Xbox game never changed reverb per zone. O-4 (owner, 2026-10-06): "the owner hears reverb/echo on the Xbox in DM-Azure". The survey found: 58 maps, 34 with a non-Default zone, DM-Azure 18 zones, all Default. The Xbox loads xbox.bin (byte-identical to the XDK's standard effects image, I3DL2 reverb with its Default initial state) and never sets a listener, so every 3D sound went through one Default reverb at its own send level in every map. Our device with ZoneReverb=True does the same in Azure.

Decisions and alternatives. Owner, 2026-10-06: "Zone reverb: keep it on." Alternative kept as the switch's other setting: ZoneReverb=False = the Xbox's one fixed Default reverb everywhere.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • In the 34 maps with non-Default zones the reverb differs from the Xbox by decision (hangars echo, underwater muffles; the Xbox sounded the same everywhere).
  • Whether our reverb's level equals the Xbox DSP's (the Xbox ran an I3DL2 DSP program, reverb24k; we run EFX/EAX reverb with the parameters carried over 1:1) is not known and cannot be measured here without an Xbox capture. The decay times are matched (below), the loudness is not calibrated.
  • 2D sounds (your own weapon, menus) are dry by rule; 3D sounds with no 3D block send at full level (XACT white paper).
  • Needs EFX; on a device without it the sound is dry and the log says so (zone ... no EFX, sound stays dry).

Status. Measured: the self-test fits the decay tail (RT60) of one cue per preset: Hangar 10.07 s against 10.05 s (+0 %), StoneRoom 2.48 s vs 2.31 s (+8 %), Default 1.68 s vs 1.49 s (+13 %), NoReverb no tail, 2D no tail (also inside the wasm32 twin under Node). In-game, log-correlated only: in DM-Praxis the player walks Default -> Underwater -> Default; the log shows zone ZoneInfo2: reverb preset Underwater and the capture is +2.0 dB louder with EFX in that window (gameplay not identical between the two runs); the DM-Azure pair: +0.4 / +3.0 / -0.0 dB by window (the window before the spawn, the only comparable one, differs by 0.4 dB). Nobody has listened to it against the Xbox (the owner hears Azure's reverb on the Xbox; no side-by-side exists). Not verified: level against the Xbox.

Off switch. ZoneReverb=False (fixed Default reverb) or UseEFX=False / -NOEFX (dry).

#40.5 Native output: backend order, device choice and "sound is off" messages (O-1)

What the player/owner sees. The game plays through PipeWire, else PulseAudio, else ALSA, else OSS, and the log says which; if there is no sound data, no OpenAL or no output device, the log and the console say why, once, and the game runs silent instead of failing quietly.

Where. UC2ALAudioSubsystem.cpp:458-461 (compiled default Backends=pipewire,pulse,alsa,oss, Linux only), :533-576 (the order becomes ALSOFT_DRIVERS unless the environment already has one; OpenAL Soft's log is captured while the device opens and reported), :577-590 (on failure the devices of that backend are listed), :514-531 and :1800-1814 (the notices: Notice string, logged at Init and sent once to the console with eventClientMessage from Update, together with the movie notice). UC2ALDevice.cpp:99-177 the log capture (alsoft_set_log_callback, found through alcGetProcAddress: it is not a dynamic export). Commit 0c0c39e. Native OpenAL Soft is built shared and loaded with dlopen (UC2ALDevice.cpp:18-61; Windows: OpenAL32.dll / soft_oal.dll beside the exe, then the system's). tools/dev/mkrun-uc2.sh copies libopenal.so.1 beside uc2.bin and prints a note when a cooked folder is missing; bin/capped sets ALSOFT_DRIVERS=null so tests never play through the desktop (CLAUDE.md).

How the parts fit. Epic's engine asks for AudioDevice= and -nosound as before; the order of backends is ours. PipeWire is only present when libpipewire-0.3-dev was installed at CMake configure time.

Why. The owner (2026-10-06) reported "no sound, no movies ('they skip or play green, there are errors in the console')". The open question in PLAN.md ("how the native build was launched when it had no sound") was never answered in the notebooks; PLAN.md itself notes that the web build had neither at that time ("expected, O-2"). Owner request: "try PipeWire, PulseAudio, ALSA in order and log which".

Decisions and alternatives. OpenAL Soft's own order made explicit (so it is documented and logged), not a second implementation. Backends= empty leaves the choice to the library and its alsoft.conf.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • On this machine the 32-bit game's PipeWire backend fails at run time (Failed to create PipeWire event context (errno: 2): the i386 PipeWire client modules are not installed) and the game falls back to PulseAudio, which the PipeWire server speaks, so sound works. BLOCKERS.md: sudo apt install libpipewire-0.3-modules:i386; a player's 32-bit machine needs the same. The 64-bit preset has the amd64 modules.
  • Backends initialise once per process, so the log capture sees only the first device open.
  • OpenAL Soft is fetched from https://github.com/10-p/openal-soft-web.git branch ufront/web (a branch name, GIT_SHALLOW; root CMakeLists.txt:238-247), not a pinned commit: a rebuild next month can differ, and a build needs network access to GitHub at configure time. Same for the web build (:302-310).
  • Windows output is untested: its backends (WASAPI, DirectSound, WinMM) were never exercised; every Windows run was under wine with the null or wave backend. The real-Windows session is planned (E-5).

Status. Measured by runs with the null backend, a PulseAudio null sink and a missing-data tree (literal log lines in web-audio.md step 1): order pipewire,pulse,alsa,oss: pipewire failed, pulse initialised; playing through pulse; no cooked data -> Game sound is off (effects, speech, music): no cooked sound data in ../Sounds ...; no output device -> OpenAL could not open an output device: sound is off and the menu still comes up; -nosound -> no audio line. Not verified: whether the owner's original "no sound" was fixed (cause not identified); the notice appearing on the menu's console (log only was looked at).

Off switch. OutputDevice= / Backends= to choose; AudioDevice=Engine.NullAudioDevice or -nosound for none.

#40.6 Web audio: openal-soft's Web Audio backend (O-2)

What the player/owner sees. In the browser the menu has music and page sounds after the Start click, matches have the same sound as the native game, and the launcher's Advanced panel can set the audio period and buffer count.

Where.

  • Engine, UC2ALAudio is no longer native-only: root CMakeLists.txt:300-349 builds the same fork static under Emscripten (ALSOFT_BACKEND_WEB, SSE4.1 required through Emscripten's SIMD headers, EAX off, wave writer off, exception catching on because OpenAL Soft throws and catches internally) and Source/UC2ALAudio/CMakeLists.txt links OpenAL on the web; UC2ALDevice.cpp:23-30 fills the entry-point table with the linked symbols. Source/SDLLaunch/cmake/Web.cmake:79-87 -sPTHREAD_POOL_SIZE=4 (the backend's mixer thread and the context's event thread on top of main() and Core's file-streaming thread); Source/UCC/CMakeLists.txt:24-28 exports _malloc/_free from ucc-bin.js (the fork's config reader needs them). Commit 19ca6e1.
  • Web inis: Engine/Config/web/Default.ini:23 (AudioDevice=), :138-146 the section with an empty Backends= (one backend, web), generated by tools/dev/sync-web-ini.py (which treats these lines as web-owned: WEB_KEYS, WEB_SECTIONS, commit ef34e6c), README rows in Engine/Config/README.md:69-74.
  • Log lines added for browser checks: UC2ALAudio: N of 23 persistent cue tables read in X ms, UC2Audio: [t] read wave pack ..., [t] first sound: <cue>, [t] music <song>: playing ... file read in X ms.
  • Frontend: targets/uc2/target.json audioBuffer {periodMs: 10, periods: 3} (enables the launcher's two audio fields; openal-soft reads Module.alsoftConfig), capability webAudio recommended (not required), packages/e2e/uc2-wasm-audio.spec.js (40.19), README "Sound and movies".
  • Script: none.

How the parts fit. The mixer runs on a pthread of its own and renders into a ring in the shared wasm memory; an AudioWorklet reads it. The engine itself runs in a worker (PROXY_TO_PTHREAD), so no AudioContext call is made from the engine thread (the backend proxies them to the browser main thread). The context starts on the user's first gesture: the frontend's Start click counts as sticky activation (the context was running when the device opened in every run) and the backend adds pointerdown/keydown/touchend listeners as a fallback. The cooked files come through the same FetchFS manifest as the packages, each fetched whole on first touch.

Why. O-2: "openal-soft's Web Audio backend from the fork the sibling port uses, the cooked Ogg audio ... through the frontend's file system, precache rules." Before this the web build used Engine.NullAudioDevice.

Decisions and alternatives. Same device, one code path (rejected: a separate web mixer in JS). Audio buffer defaults are engine-ut2004's "measured-safe" 10 ms x 3, not tuned for UC2.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Needs cross-origin isolation, AudioWorklet and threads, all already required by the target; without Web Audio open() fails and the game is silent.
  • No end-to-end latency measurement (input to ear). The ring is ~1,536 frames at 48 kHz (about 32 ms) plus the browser's output latency, which differs per machine. Underruns: 0 in the quiet run, 1 (1-3 ms) in busy runs, tied to a 45-100 ms mixer gap or a 67 ms long task on the page.
  • Start-up cost on a busy machine: the 23 persistent cue tables take 0.5 s (quiet) to 1.3-1.6 s, first sound 3.1-3.6 s after the device opened; cue tables, menu music and page sounds are not in precache.json (README: "not added yet"), so they are fetched one by one on demand. Cinematics (e.g. 01_AscPre1.ogv 29.7 MB) are fetched whole at first touch: a stall at the ladder's travel.
  • The music refill hitch (40.3) and the fork pinned to a branch (40.5).
  • Pool size 4 adds two idle workers to the whole artifact.
  • Chrome-only evidence: only Chromium was used.

Status. Measured (uc2-wasm-audio.spec.js, 3 tests, passed in pass A and B of web-audio.md step 6 and in every coordinator run since: "audio 3/3"): the device opens on Web Audio with reverb EFX (EAX reverb); the AudioContext is running with no page input after Start; the AnalyserNode on the worklet node shows the output is not silent (audible 0.98-1.0); logos play 20.0 s of sound in 20.2-20.4 s wall time; a key press at ~3 s ends them and the music follows. wasm32 code under Node: UC2AudioCheck 0 failures, UC2AudioSelfTest 39/0 (reverb included). Seen/heard by a person: not recorded (the spec mutes Chromium and routes to a null sink, PULSE_SINK, by design). Not verified: other browsers, mobile, latency, long-session stability.

Off switch. Web inis AudioDevice=Engine.NullAudioDevice, or the launcher's engine option -nosound.

#40.7 Audio tests and the regression suite

What the owner sees. One command line of results per item; nothing to listen to.

Where.

  • ucc-bin UC2ALAudio.UC2AudioCheck (UC2ALAudio.cpp:36): every cue of every cue table resolves, every wave decodes to the exact length in its index, all languages, music streams open. No device needed.
  • ucc-bin UC2ALAudio.UC2AudioSelfTest (UC2AudioSelfTest.cpp, 552 lines; commit ad44320, reverb 1739a84): the device runs in OpenAL Soft's loopback mode (LoopbackTest, set only by the commandlet) with simulated time; the cue goes through the device's own path (UC2TestPlay/UC2TestRender, UC2ALAudioSubsystem.cpp:2059-2136) and the output is compared with values computed independently from the documented rules.
  • tools/dev/audio-regression.sh <tree>: Check + SelfTest + four captures (audio-menu, audio-deadbolt, audio-praxis-efx, audio-praxis-dry) through tools/dev/run-uc2-audio.sh (Xvfb, -audiowave= wave writer, seeded input loop, optional pre/post console commands), audio-capture-report.sh (RMS per band, silent seconds, spectrogram), audio-music-check.py (identifies the menu track and its level in the capture). One SUMMARY line each in logs/<tree>/audio-summary.txt.
  • Debug: UC2AUDIO STATS prints per-cue requested/played/culled/failed/no-voice counts at shutdown.

How the parts fit. The checks use the same device code as the game; the capture runs use the real game, so a bug in loading, voices or reverb shows up in either.

Why. No Xbox oracle: the owner asked for verification "without ears".

Decisions and alternatives. Expected values are computed in the test from the XACT rules, not copied from the device. Rejected: comparing with ffmpeg's own mix.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • The suite proves arithmetic and wiring, not that it sounds like the Xbox.
  • The pitch measurement carries a +4.3 cent method bias, measured on a 0-cent control and subtracted (raw errors are printed too).
  • Captures are not repeatable to the cue (bots and frame timing vary; the same Praxis scenario played 547, 346 and 260 cues in three runs); the checks decide on silence, level, identification and log lines, not on counts.
  • The float wave writer does not clip: peaks of +0.3 and +3.1 dBFS were recorded; a 16-bit device would clip or limit.
  • The wave-writer path and the self-test only run natively (and under wine/Node); the hardware path is exercised only by a PulseAudio null sink.

Status. Measured (the standing numbers, repeated unchanged by the coordinator at every merge, last 2026-10-08): UC2AudioCheck 7,141/7,141 waves, 21/21 music streams, 2,438 cues, 0 failures; UC2AudioSelfTest 39 checks, 0 failed (gain: 0.00-0.16 dB from the formula over 18 cases at 6 distances, 4 category levels and the RPC curve; pan +/-16 dB for a source 2,000 units to the side; pitch -0.4..+0.0 cents on three cues; finite and infinite loops; playlists 4, 1, 0; radius/duration; reverb decay); menu music identified at -19.02 dB (expected -19.00), correlation 0.84-0.87; the four captures 0 silent seconds, 0 failed cues, 0 Critical. Same results under wine (OpenAL32.dll) and in the wasm32 build under Node.

Off switch. Test-only.

#40.8 Localised taunt order and the missing source WAVs: kept as the Xbox had them

What the player/owner sees. In German, French, Italian and Spanish the Sapphire taunts 40-53 (and one tutorial line in Spanish) are the wrong lines for their names, exactly as on the Xbox; 15 cues are silent.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 40.8.

Where. Data: A_TauntSapphire_{deu,fra,ita,spa} entries 40-53 are shifted by one against the English bank (99 entries in all, found by comparing lengths with the shipped .xwb); tools/cook-audio.py --relink-localised (line 753, 872-885) re-orders by name; the default does not. Nothing in the engine.

Why. Epic's data defect. Owner decision, 2026-10-06 (PLAN, BLOCKERS.md): "Localised taunt indices: keep the Xbox behaviour (no relink)."

Decisions and alternatives. Keep the Xbox order (faithful), rather than repair it. The repair exists as a switch of the cook and costs nothing to enable later.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Players of those four languages hear mismatched taunts. The 19 missing source WAVs (40.1) remain open in BLOCKERS.md; decoding them from the .xwb was not decided.

Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 40.8.

Status. Measured: lengths of localised entries match the shipped .xwb's order (entry 40 of A_TauntSapphire_deu is 21,564 ADPCM bytes = the 76,626-byte Sorry source, not the Spammer of the base bank). Not verified by ear.

Off switch. Re-cook with --relink-localised.

#40.9 The movie cook: XMV (WMV2) -> Ogg Theora + Vorbis, with a sound file per language

What the player/owner sees. Nothing directly; it produces the movies the game plays. The ladder's 19 cinematics, the credits, the start-up logos, the attract clips and the 87 menu/HUD texture clips all exist as .ogv files.

Where.

  • New code: tools/cook-movies.py (493 lines, commits c9cc2fd, c4e7fd3). It runs ffmpeg and ffprobe inside bin/capped 2G, at most --jobs 4 at a time. Options: --epic, --out, --only, --video-quality 7, --keyint 150, --audio-quality 4, --pcm-audio-quality 5, --no-measure, --force. A re-cook skips files whose source SHA-1 and settings are unchanged (Movies/.cook/<Name>.json).
  • Outputs (format version 1, ours; video.md "Cooked format"): Movies/<Name>.ogv (23: Theora + the international track, XMV audio stream 0, as Vorbis), Movies/<Name>_<lang>.oga (76 = 19 cinematics x deu/fra/ita/spa, written only when the track differs from stream 0), Textures/Movies/<Name>.ogv (87: video only), Movies/movie-manifest.json, Movies/cook-report.txt.
  • Helpers: tools/dev/movie-lang-evidence.py, tools/dev/xcorr.c (language-track evidence), tools/dev/movie-selftest.sh (40.10 tests).
  • Asset tree and frontend: as for sound (mkassets-uc2.sh hard-links Movies/ and Textures/Movies/; the web manifest lists 99 + 87 = 186 files, 654 MB).
  • Script/ini: none.

How the parts fit. The cook is offline; the runtime player (40.10) reads the .ogv and, for a non-English game language, the matching .oga. A web client therefore fetches one .ogv and at most one .oga per movie, never five languages.

Why. The Xbox plays XMV (WMV2 video, Xbox ADPCM or PCM sound) with the XDK's decoder; nothing on PC reads it. Owner decision (PLAN, 2026-10-04): "Video: Offline transcode (Theora video + Vorbis audio, libtheoradec). Runtime ffmpeg XMV decode = end-of-project" (M16). The owner asked for a small portable decoder; libtheora's decoder is 10.5k lines of plain C that builds for native, MinGW and wasm without assembly.

Decisions and alternatives.

  • Theora q:v 7, GOP 150 (5 s): 85 % of the WMV2 video size at PSNR-Y 36-45 dB. Quality grid in video.md: q8 costs +25 % size for +1.6 dB; q6 saves 25 % and loses 2 dB (the 256x256 clips fall to SSIM 0.969).
  • The WMV2 encoder dropped frames (held pictures; gaps up to 1,035 ms); the cook makes a constant 30 (or 15) fps stream with -vf fps, which libtheora writes as zero-byte "dropped frame" packets. The player counts packets, not decoded pictures.
  • Audio is decoded to WAV first and then encoded: ffmpeg's xmv demuxer stamps PCM packets with the ADPCM time base (granule positions 3.55x too large).
  • TestMaps production sources (AVI/MOV) were checked and not used: not the same cut, video only, and 15-bit RGB stored (banding not obviously better than WMV2). PLAN.md M9 had said "use TestMaps AVI/MOV sources where they exist"; this deviates from it with evidence.
  • Rejected (PLAN): runtime XMV decoding with ffmpeg, kept as the end-of-project option (M16). The notebooks do not record an evaluation of other codecs.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Lossy-to-lossy: WMV2 -> Theora. Worst clip Crab_Display02 (128x64) PSNR-Y 35.5 dB; Portrait_Char_Corrosion SSIM 0.972; median PSNR-Y 40.1 dB, SSIM 0.983 against the decoded XMV.
  • Theora is an old codec; fine here because only our cooked files are decoded.
  • Language tracks 1-4 = deu, fra, ita, spa rest on Epic's code only (XMVHelper.cpp:160-189); no reference recording in those languages exists in the tree. Track 0 = English is established from content (waveform cross-correlation with the English voice stems: 10-60x the other tracks, 4 of 4 cinematics with stems).
  • The cook needs ffmpeg built with the xmv demuxer, libtheora and libvorbis, and bin/capped at a fixed relative path; 25 minutes wall time for everything.
  • 417 benign ffmpeg warnings (xmv demuxer EIO after the last packet) are kept in the report, classified, not hidden.
  • Sizes: 1,052 MB of XMV -> 654 MB of Ogg (62 %); the web manifest is 42 % cooked audio/video by size.

Status. Measured, logs/m9/cook-1.out: 110 XMV -> 186 files, frame/sample accounting exact for 110 of 110 (packets out = granule frames = slots = round(last pts x fps) + 1; sound samples = decoded XMV samples), 0 decode errors; A-V difference -0.034..-0.037 s on 21 movies (the cooked sound ends one frame early; the player pads it with silence). Quality: PSNR-Y min 35.50 / median 40.14 / max 45.13 dB; SSIM min 0.9722 / median 0.9831 (single-threaded ffmpeg references; ffmpeg's threaded Theora decoder is not deterministic).

Off switch. Offline tool.

#40.10 The movie player and full-screen playback: logos, cinematics, loading screen, menu textures

What the player/owner sees. The 20-second start-up logos (skippable with any key after half a second) before the menu; a ladder match's intro cinematic when it loads (?StartCinematic=), its outro when it ends (?EndCinematic=); a still "LOADING..." screen between maps; animated portraits and TV screens on menu pages and in maps (MovieMaterial).

Where.

  • New code, Source/UC2Movie/ (CMake Source/UC2Movie/CMakeLists.txt; commits 5b7ec74 player, 34d2e39 build + registration, 8001554 self-test): Src/UC2MoviePlayer.cpp (703 lines) FUC2MoviePlayer : FPortableMoviePlayer - one Ogg reader per stream on its own file handle (16 KB reads, only that stream's pages), libtheora decode, libvorbis decode, fixed-point BT.601 limited-range conversion to BGRA with chroma replicated per 2x2; Src/UC2MovieSelfTest.cpp (511 lines) the commandlet UC2Movie.UC2MovieSelfTest.
  • New code, Engine (our files in Epic's package): Engine/Inc/UnMovie.h (144 lines) the portable interface (FPortableMoviePlayer, FMovieAudioStream, GCreateMoviePlayer, the named capability WITH_PORTABLE_MOVIES, which is 1 on every non-Xbox build); Engine/Src/UnMovie.cpp (664 lines) what XboxMovieThread.cpp and XMVHelper.cpp did, written once: name resolution from the Xbox paths the content uses (appFindMovie :88, case-insensitive: the content says Portrait_Gametype_NS for Portrait_GameType_NS.xmv), the language rule (:79-123), full-screen loop appPlayMovieFullscreen :368, draw DrawMovieFrame :319 (aspect kept; the "hide subtitles" bands over rows 0-96 and 384-480 of a 480-line picture when GShowSubtitles is off; "LOADING..." text), input capture appMovieInputEvent :255, MovieMaterial update appUpdateMovieMaterial :523, and the hooks PlayIntroCinematic :621, PlayOutroCinematic :612, PlayLoadingMovie :634, SetEngineReady, IsPlayingMovie with the Xbox names. Core/Inc/UnMovieBase.h (new) holds FMovieThread/FMoviePlayer, moved unchanged out of UnVcWin32.h:792 so every platform has one definition (UnGnuG.h includes it; the GCC port had carried a copy).
  • Audio side: UC2ALAudioSubsystem.cpp:2138-2316 FUC2ALMovieStream: one relative 2D source with 12 x 4,096-frame buffers; the clock is buffers unqueued + AL_SAMPLE_OFFSET, made monotonic; an exclusive (full-screen) stream pauses all cues (NotifyPauseChange) and playing music and resumes them when deleted. Epic's interface gets OpenMovieAudio (UnAudio.h:115).
  • Epic's code changed (small, delimited hunks): Engine/Src/UnGame.cpp - #include "UnMovie.h" :24; the four #if lines that were _XBOX only now say (defined(_XBOX) || WITH_PORTABLE_MOVIES) (extern block :1821, bWasMenu :1856, the start-of-LoadMap block :1860-1885 that plays the outro and starts the loading movie, the end-of-LoadMap block :2998-3009 that plays the intro); the condition of GRenDev->ResetRenderStates() at :1865 gains && GLevel && GLevel->Engine->GRenDev (the Xbox always had a level and a device by then; a PC's first load and a dedicated server do not); UGameEngine::Exec handles the Xbox client's SUBTITLES command :460-466 (a gate lifted: it was XboxClient.cpp:169-180). Engine/Src/UnEngine.cpp:651-653 first statement of UEngine::InputEvent: if( appMovieInputEvent( iKey, State ) ) return 1;. UnAudio.h, UnVcWin32.h, UnGnuG.h as above; UnMaterial.cpp:1134-1319 INT -> PTRINT for MovieMaterial's pointer fields (64-bit, not movie logic). Not lifted: PaintProgress and Draw's IsPlayingMovie/SetEngineReady blocks (nothing runs in the background here).
  • Launcher: Source/SDLLaunch/Src/SDLLaunch.cpp:599-604 plays ..\Movies\UC2_Logos.xmv after Engine->Init() and LOADWINDOWPOSITION, before the main loop (commit 9df0ad4); registration :524-526, Source/UCC/Src/UCC.cpp:559-561.
  • Renderer (section 20 covers it): Source/UC2GLDrv/Src/UC2GLMovie.cpp (116 lines, commit dbbf486) is the PC body of SetMovieMaterial (BGRA upload with glTexSubImage2D only when the picture changed); Epic's 20-line PC stub in UC2GLMaterialState.cpp is replaced by a comment.
  • Vendored: Thirdparty/Theora (libtheora 1.2.0, decoder only, byte-identical copies with SHA-256 recorded in README.uc2, no assembly, no legacy API; our CMakeLists.txt defines ogg_uint16_t/ogg_uint64_t because the vendored libogg is 1.0), root CMakeLists.txt:352-354. Thirdparty/Vorbis (libvorbis 1.0, 2001).
  • ini: optional [UC2Movie] StartupMovies / Cinematics / LoadingMovie (default True when absent; only the web Default.ini:148-151 writes them out); switches -NOMOVIE, -NOSTARTUPMOVIES, -NULLMOVIES, -movieshot=.
  • Script (.uc): none. Epic's TournamentLadderRung.uc:244,559 and UC2RungDone.uc:339 already append ?StartCinematic= / ?EndCinematic=; T_UI_2 etc. already hold the MovieMaterials.
  • Tests: 40.10 Status below. Frontend: manifest (186 files), -nostartupmovies in the boot spec, the audio spec's logo tests (40.19).

How the parts fit. The content (Epic's) asks for a movie by its Xbox name, D:\Movies\04a_AscPre5.xmv or a MovieMaterial.MovieFileName; UnMovie.cpp maps it to the cooked file, picks the sound file by the game's language, creates a FUC2MoviePlayer through GCreateMoviePlayer (set when the package registers) and opens the device's movie stream. The picture follows the sound clock (frame n shows while played frames are in [n/fps, (n+1)/fps); the cooked sound is padded to the video's length so a loop is seamless); without sound or audio device it follows real time. Full-screen playback is a blocking loop in the calling thread (no thread, so the single-threaded web build can do the same); a MovieMaterial is advanced by the render device when it is drawn. Without UC2Movie registered GCreateMoviePlayer stays NULL and nothing plays, as before; without the cooked files each missing movie is logged once and the first failure says why on the console.

Why. UC2's movies were Xbox-only: CXMVPlayer and XboxMovieThread for the logos, cinematics and the loading movie, D3DDrv's SetMovieMaterial (#ifdef _XBOX) for textures; PC builds returned 0 and drew the default material. The brief: logos, cinematics, a menu preview animate.

Decisions and alternatives.

  • No thread: the Xbox thread existed to keep the loading movie going during a blocking load. Here blocking playback runs in the engine thread and the loading movie is not animated (below).
  • The loading movie is a static screen: the first picture of UI_LoadingMovie with "LOADING..." is drawn once before the load (PlayLoadingMovie :634). Drawing from inside the loader would re-enter the renderer while the level is replaced. The animated version "stays off behind its hook" (LoadingMovie ini key only switches the static one).
  • Logos play after Engine->Init(), not before as on the Xbox, because the window opens at the end of init. The Xbox's legal (5 s) and rating (4.9 s) screens (Startup/UC2Startup.xpr textures) are not shown.
  • bDecodeFromMemory (the Xbox's background whole-file load with a fallback texture) is ignored: the player streams from the file with bounded buffers.
  • Skip: any key, mouse button or pad button press from 0.5 s on (the Xbox armed its skip after the same half second, so the press that started a travel does not skip its cinematic); all other input is swallowed while a movie plays.
  • Movie sound is 0 dB in no category (the Xbox played XMV sound through DirectSound outside XACT).
  • Chroma replicated per 2x2 block rather than interpolated (what a 4:2:0 texture shows unfiltered, and cheaper); the Xbox decoder's choice is not documented.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • Not done / off behind hooks: the animated loading movie; attract mode (UC2_Attract.xmv, A51.xmv cooked and sitting in the manifest, 104 MB, but nothing calls them: the Xbox's trigger is in UCMenu script); the legal and rating screens.
  • Never exercised in a run: outro cinematics (?EndCinematic=, same code path as intro), a MovieMaterial with sound (the menu's "View movies" page: the T_UI_2 cinematics with bEnableAudio and bPlayLooping; on the web each would fetch its file whole, up to 66 MB for the credits), non-English sound tracks in game.
  • Cinematic and logo sound ignores the options sliders (no category, as on the Xbox).
  • A slow machine does not skip pictures: every Theora packet up to the clock's frame is decoded in order (they predict from each other), at most 90 per Advance and only the last is converted, so a slow decoder lets the picture lag behind the sound rather than stall the game; the sound never waits. Decode cost 1.2-2.2 ms per update on average, 36-118 ms at worst (the first update includes the whole-file fetch on the web).
  • Blocking loop: nothing else runs during a cinematic (as on the Xbox); appSleep of 2-10 ms per iteration.
  • On the web: the player is built in and active (UC2Movie is not native-only; the web Default.ini writes the three [UC2Movie] keys), so the 20-second logos play by default in the browser, skippable, unless the launcher's engine options say -nostartupmovies. The movie file is fetched whole before it plays (3.2 MB for the logos, 30 MB for a cinematic, 66-75 MB for the credits and A51), so a ladder travel stalls for the download. gl4es converts every BGRA picture to RGBA on the CPU on WebGL 1 (no BGRA format there); that cost per picture was not measured. The pictures were never seen or asserted in a browser (below).
  • A 640x480 BGRA picture (1.2 MB) plus libtheora's frames per open movie, and one OpenAL stream per movie sound.
  • Shared Epic code touched: see the closing list (UnGame.cpp, UnEngine.cpp, UnAudio.h).

Status. Measured (tools/dev/movie-selftest.sh, ucc-bin UC2Movie.UC2MovieSelfTest, commit 8001554): 99 checks, 0 failed (96 at M9 + 3 for 40.11): frame count = packet count; a new picture exactly where ffmpeg decodes one; every picture identical to ffmpeg's decode (Adler-32 of the Y'CbCr planes, e.g. 1,310 of 1,310 on 03_AscPre3); BGRA vs ffmpeg swscale (BT.601 limited, replicated chroma) maximum difference 1; loop wrap with and without sound; A/V sync error 0 frames over a whole movie under random 5-40 ms steps; sound within one 16-bit step of ffmpeg's decoder; language rule (int/deu/fra/ita/spa/jpn for 03_AscPre3 -> .ogv/_deu/_fra/_ita/_spa/.ogv; UC2_Logos in German falls back to the .ogv). In the game, tools/dev/m9-movies.py 19 PASS: logos start, advance, match their cooked frames at 65.8-79.7 dB PSNR, end after 20.000 s (1,097 pictures in 20.002 s), skip at 2.987 s; the menu portrait animates in its frame; an intro cinematic travels from the menu, matches its frames (66.6 / 67.3 dB; the subtitle bands black), its sound correlates 0.989-0.997 with the cooked track at 0.00 dB gain, and it is skippable; null renderer and server play none. Web: the logos run in real time with the Web Audio device (uc2-wasm-audio.spec.js, sound 20.000 s in 20.2-20.4 s); GL4ES with LIBGL_NOBGRA=1 (WebGL 1 has no BGRA) on the native ES 2 twin shows the same pictures (82.4 / 65.8 dB). Weak: the menu-movie-shots check (a portrait changes between two screenshots 2 s apart) is timing-sensitive and failed once under load (24.0 dB where < 20 was wanted); d6d7e33 made three timing-sensitive checks (movies among them) robust. Not verified: the movie picture in a real browser (no spec asserts it: the boot spec launches with -nostartupmovies; the audio spec runs the logos but checks sound and time); outro cinematics; MovieMaterial sound.

Off switch. -nostartupmovies, -NOMOVIE, [UC2Movie] StartupMovies=False / Cinematics=False / LoadingMovie=False. Removing UC2Movie from the registrants restores the old behaviour (no movies).

#40.11 A movie texture that cannot play no longer shows the engine's green default texture

What the player/owner sees. Portraits and screens with no movie data show their LoadingFallback picture or plain black instead of bright green (the owner's "videos play green").

Where. Engine/Src/UnMovie.cpp:469-521 (FBlackMoviePicture: a 4x4 opaque black picture, revision -2; NoPicture) and :551-565; commit 0c0c39e. The GL device's code is unchanged (it uploads whatever picture it gets). Test: 3 more checks in UC2MovieSelfTest.

How the parts fit. When a material has no picture yet (movie missing, nothing decoded, nothing converted) the update returns its LoadingFallback if it has one, else the black picture; one log line per material and reason. Before, SetMovieMaterial returned 0 and the device drew Material.DefaultMaterial, which in UC2 is Engine.DefaultTexture, bright green (the Xbox did the same for a movie it could not open, but every movie the content referenced on purpose existed there; the web build had none in its manifest, so every texture was green).

Why. O-1c, owner report. Seen: logs/m11audio-nomovies/m9-menu-movie-0.png showed the arena page's portrait as a solid green square; after the fix it shows the fallback (T_Portrait.Arena.UI_Arena_Null_Portrait).

Decisions and alternatives. Black over green: a missing movie is not an error the player needs to see. The theory that all-zero YUV converts to green was checked and does not apply (our BGRA buffer starts zeroed).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Hides missing-data problems visually (the log still names them). Names the content references but the game never shipped (all CH_* crosshair clips, RL_XB_*, ...) failed to open on the Xbox too; they are logged once and shown as fallback/black.

Status. Measured: the self-test cases; seen: the picture pair above.

Off switch. None (behaviour fix).

#40.12 Network join: a client can connect to a server and play (branch net-join)

What the player/owner sees. A client joins a dedicated or a listen server, loads the level, spawns, moves, shoots, scores, follows a server travel and leaves, on Linux against Linux and against the MinGW server under wine. Before this a joining client died with check(GObjBeginLoadCount) right after Welcomed by server, on every platform.

Where. Source/IpDrv/Src/TcpNetDriver.cpp and UnTcpNetDriver.h have zero changed lines; every fix is in the shared engine:

  • Source/Engine/Src/UnScriptPatcher.cpp:143-155 - BeginLoad()/EndLoad() around the AppendImport loop of FLinkerPatchData::Apply (commits 0926219 first attempt, 63de2e4 the fix). Gate/bug fixed.
  • Source/Core/Src/UnCoreNet.cpp:276-292 - UPackageMap::Compute counts only what the package file holds (its last generation) and not the names/exports the script patcher appended; :304-305 Epic's commented per-package line revived under DevNet so two ends can compare tables (commit 8a5b0e2). Bug fixed.
  • Source/Engine/Src/UnConn.cpp:164-179 - UNetConnection::Destroy destroys (does not delete) its channels while GIsGarbageCollecting; Source/Engine/Src/UnChan.cpp:179-190 - UChannel::RouteDestroy marks the channel RF_DebugDestroy after the connection destroyed it; Source/UCC/Src/UCC.cpp:721 - Commandlet->AddToRoot() so the collector inside the server's LoadMap does not free the commandlet whose Main() is running (commit 006e725). Bugs fixed (found with AddressSanitizer).
  • Source/Core/Src/UnUnix.cpp (bounded appStrncpy/appStrncat into GMachineCPU[32], 5 sites, commit a6c8edc): the port's own bug (UT2004's code over UC2's smaller buffer), not Epic's.
  • Diagnostics under DevNet (silent by default): UnChan.cpp:1008-1009 what a bunch contained when "Received invalid actor class" fires, :1440-1451 which persistent actor has no package-map index / which class is unmapped.
  • IpDrv build/portability changes (no behaviour): GCC_PACK(4) and PTRINT on the pointer-sized members of the generated IpDrvClasses.h (64-bit), _WIN32 instead of _MSC_VER for WinSock, WSAEHOSTUNREACH, a duplicate errno guard for Emscripten (UnSocket.cpp:267-271), SendStatLine qualification (GCC), a CMake file. Commits 482cfbb (builds), 754ce47 (MinGW), f60b44e (64-bit).
  • ini: none. Script: none. Frontend: none (native).

How the parts fit. Epic's join sequence (HELLO -> CHALLENGE -> LOGIN -> USES per package -> WELCOME -> level load -> JOIN, network.md "The join sequence") is unchanged. On a client every package of the server's USES list first exists as an empty UPackage; when LoadMap runs the script patcher it creates linkers for those placeholders and appends imports whose package chain is unverified, which called GetPackageLinker outside BeginLoad. Epic's shipped Xbox build defines FINAL_RELEASE, which compiles check() out, so it never saw this. The second fix matters because the package map assigns object and name indices by running counts: a server patches what it has loaded at its first LoadMap and the rest after Listen, a client patches every USES package before its Compute, so the counts differed (UCMutators 765 names on the server, 766 on the client) and with them every later base and the bit width of a name index. Both ends must run the fix; a fixed client against an unfixed server can desync.

Why. BLOCKERS.md: a client joining ucc-bin server DM-Deadbolt got Welcomed by server: LEVEL=DM-Deadbolt and then the assertion (Linux -> Linux and Linux -> wine). All Epic's code paths, latent in the shipped build.

Decisions and alternatives.

  • First attempt (hold BeginLoad across the whole Apply) was wrong: it queued the classes PreloadExports creates until after the reader swap, and they were serialised from the patch bytes (Bad export index, garbage). Lesson recorded: the position of each EndLoad in that function decides which reader an object is read from. The narrow scope (the AppendImport loop only; it creates linkers, never objects) is the fix.
  • Package-map counts: clamp to the file's last generation. Patched-in objects (function locals) and names (all present in other packages' name tables) are then not addressable over the network, which nothing replicated needs.
  • Received invalid actor class once per join was left as is: the server opens a channel for the run-time-spawned DefaultPhysicsVolume (bNoDelete, no linker, DM-Deadbolt has none in its file), the client marks the channel broken and keeps its own volume. UT2004 does the same. A candidate fix (skip actors with no package-map index in ULevel::ServerTickClients) is noted, not applied.
  • ASan found and did not fix: 1-byte over-reads in Epic's interpreter (ProcessInternal peeking EX_DebugInfo past a function's last byte, UnCorSc.cpp:5422/:5481; execLess_IntInt / P_FINISH reading past the end of a class's script when a replication condition is evaluated, UnScript.cpp:3582). Benign heap slack, recorded for the script milestone.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • The package-map clamp changes what client and server must agree on: an old unfixed peer can desync (a Windows server built before this branch). With M14's rebuilt script packages the patcher skips a package that is not the first-generation file the patch was made for (UnScriptPatcher.cpp:291-308, commit d2d1648), so the clamp matters for the shipped packages that are still patched.
  • UCGameplay.u is a server package: every peer must run the same file (the rebuilt one is in the asset tree; see 30).
  • UC2 returns a pawn to LastServerMoveLocation at every ServerMove and in the pawn tick (UnController.cpp:413, UnLevTic.cpp:401-412, Epic's "moelfke" code). A server-side teleport of a live player pawn is undone unless the code also resets that field (PlayerController.Restart does; respawns are fine). Not tested with teleporters online.
  • Never exercised: team games/CTF online, package download (ChannelDownload), HTTP redirect download (IpDrv.HTTPDownload does not exist in UC2's IpDrv.u: a harmless server warning), split-screen child connections, voice, LAN beacon / server browser, admin login, match end by frag or time limit (travel was tested with servertravel), more than 3 clients, any real network.
  • Observed, then handled elsewhere: after a travel the client logged in with its character's name ("Anubis"), not the launcher's (profile/name work, section 30).

Status. Measured, tools/dev/net-test.sh, final fresh-tree run at b78b396 and the coordinator's on merged main: dedicated1 8 items, dedicated2 9, listen 8, travel 6, disconnect 6 (including a frozen client dropped after the 30 s ConnectionTimeout and a killed one in 0-3 s by ICMP port-unreachable), lag (PktLag=50 PktLoss=5, about 100 ms round trip) 9, glshot 3, wine 5 (Linux client against the MinGW ucc.exe; the 67-line package-map tables of both ends identical), soak 4 (10 minutes, 2 clients + 2 bots, RSS flat after the first 2 minutes: server 38 MB, clients 83-99 MB), 0 Critical in every log; coordinator at the merge: 46 PASS. Seen only: the GL client rendering the other player (name label, lock-on reticle, scoreboard) in logs/net/glshot-NetA-sees-NetB.png. Not verified: everything listed under "never exercised".

Off switch. None.

#40.13 Lost handshake packets and the packet-id wrap (branch net-loss)

What the player/owner sees. A join no longer hangs for minutes when one handshake packet is lost; after about 4.5 minutes of play on a lossy link scores and other replicated values no longer go silently stale; a handshake that stops making progress ends after 20 seconds with the normal "connection failed" menu.

Where.

  • Source/Engine/Src/UnConn.cpp:33-40 PacketIdDelta (signed distance modulo MAX_PACKETID-1), :64-65 OutAckPacketId/InPacketId start at MAX_PACKETID-2 ("the id before 0", UT2004: -1), :513 and :548-565 ReceivedPacket order check and virtual-NAK loop use it (commit 84aea3e). Wire format unchanged.
  • Source/Engine/Src/UnPenLev.cpp:32 / :74 / :171 / :642-653 - UNetPendingLevel::Tick fails a pending connection that got no control message for InitialConnectTimeout (20 s, [IpDrv.TcpNetDriver]), measured from the last message received and checked after the tick's dispatch (commits 84aea3e, e3c66ac).
  • Test switches in UnConn.cpp/UnChan.cpp and UC2NetTest.cpp: NETDROP=, NETPKTID=, -NETOLDACKS (UnConn.cpp:18-31, 81-92, 379, 447; UnChan.cpp:689), all under Epic's DO_ENABLE_NET_TEST (which is 1).
  • ini/script/frontend: none.
  • Tests: net-test.sh handshake-loss (hello, challenge, netspeed, login, uses, welcome, join, afterjoin, wrap, wraponly, wrapclient), handshake-compat (6 cases), lag, soak with .views.

How the parts fit. UC2's reliability resends a lost bunch only on a NAK, and a NAK is deduced from an ack of a later packet (UnConn.cpp:548). Two defects made some loss unrecoverable: (1) OutAckPacketId started at 0 and the first packet a connection sends has id 0, so packet 0 is never inside a NAK range - if the server's CHALLENGE (its packet 0) is lost nothing resends it, and because the client keeps sending keep-alives and the server keeps acking them neither side times out: a silent hang (the coordinator's lag run: 3.5 minutes of nothing); (2) ids became _WORDs that wrap (UnConn.cpp:425) but the comparisons stayed a plain >, so after ~16,000 packets an ack of a small post-wrap id is never "greater" and no NAK is ever deduced again (WELCOME lost after the wrap = hang; in play, lost property updates never resent). The fix is local to the side that sends the lost packet, so each end's fix covers its own packets. The give-up is a defence for the case of an unfixed server.

Why. Coordinator's evidence logs/coord/net-lag-hang/; and "a lost handshake packet must not hang a join (it will matter on a WebRTC data channel)". Epic's UC2 code (both causes), not the port; on a LAN or Xbox system link loss is rare, over the internet 5 % loss on the first server packet hangs 1 join in 20.

Decisions and alternatives.

  • Fix the ack logic the way UT2004 does (signed ids + a "before 0" start) instead of adding a timer resend of packet 0.
  • Do not make a transport reliable to hide this (a WebRTC channel made reliable would add head-of-line blocking under the engine's own resends).
  • The first version of the give-up (measured from the connection start, before dispatch) fired wrongly after a long software-GL frame; it is now measured from the last message and after dispatch.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Each end's fix covers only the packets that end sends. Compatibility matrix (handshake-compat): a fixed server with an unfixed client joins when the server loses its CHALLENGE (2.2 s); an unfixed server with a fixed client does not join (the client gives up after 20 s and shows the connection-failed menu); an unfixed client whose JOIN is its packet 0 after a wrap does not join. Both ends of a game must therefore run the build (they do: one engine, one asset tree). PktLoss simulates at FlushNet, which equals a wire loss for the sender, but real links also reorder, duplicate and jitter. -NETOLDACKS leaves Epic's defective logic reachable in the shipped binary (test-only).

Status. Measured: every handshake-loss case joins in 2.0-2.6 s after the client starts (10/10 items in the coordinator's run); before the fix CHALLENGE-lost and WELCOME-after-wrap hung; compat 6/6 as the matrix above; lag 10/10 with the final harness, after a first series of 7/10 whose three failures were in the test (a client holding the melee weapon cannot hit at 450 units; a probe taken one second after a kill saw deaths=0), plus a PktLoss=20 run 9/9: all 21 runs joined, none hung; an 8-minute soak at PktLoss=5 PktLag=50 passes the wrap: clients' scores equal the server's with the fix, and with -NETOLDACKS two clients still showed stale scores 10 s after play stopped (the old logic's defect, reproduced); coordinator's full run: 89 PASS, 0 FAIL (logs/coord/a1-net.log). Observed, not chased: under simulated loss a player sometimes spawns holding the melee weapon or an empty energy weapon (server and client agree; not a desync; untested on a real lossy link).

Off switch. None (test-only -NETOLDACKS restores Epic's logic for one process).

#40.14 Network test tools and the native servers

What the owner sees. A scripted network test that can be re-run with one command; the dedicated and listen servers the game already had.

Where.

  • Source/Engine/Src/UC2NetTest.cpp (473 lines, ours, in Epic's Engine package; hooked by UnGame.cpp:447-455 (UGameEngine::Exec first lines) and :3528 (UGameEngine::Tick)): console commands NETPROBE <tag> (:78: a summary line, one per pawn and per PlayerReplicationInfo), NETPLACE <name> x y z yaw [pitch] (:134), NETPLACENEAR (:198), NETAIM <name> (:248); the command file -nettest=<file> (:315); NETDROP=<spec>[,<spec>] (:399-462); KeepServerMoveSpot (:179: records the placed spot in LastServerMoveLocation, otherwise UC2's ServerMove rewinds the teleported pawn). Commits 634d1b7, a4c33f6, 7bb4b53, 3047b6e, d27dfc6.
  • tools/dev/net-test.sh (52 lines) and tools/dev/net-test.py (893 lines): checks dedicated1 dedicated2 handshake-loss handshake-compat listen travel disconnect lag glshot soak wine; one bin/capped 6G scope, port 7850 (NET_PORT in 7850-7899), each process on its own Xvfb with its own ini/log (INI=, USERINI=, LOG= are Epic's appInit options), a virtual pad per client through SDL2Drv's -inputtest= channel. Decisions come from log and probe lines only. tools/dev/m12-wine-smoke.sh netjoin was upgraded from "handshake seen" to "joined and spawned".
  • Servers: ucc-bin server <map> (Epic's UServerCommandlet; GIsClient is 0, so no audio device and no movies) and uc2.bin <map>?Listen. Windows: ucc.exe/uc2.exe. The web has no dedicated server.
  • Other tools use these commands: NETPLACE LocalPlayer positions the camera for the renderer tests (m7-effects.sh).

How the parts fit. The commands are plain console commands, so any process (a browser page through ueExec, the web e2e spec) can use them.

Why. Network play has no oracle but itself; the owner's rule is that every visible feature has a repeatable test.

Decisions and alternatives. Everything compiled into the Engine package (not a test-only library), so web builds and all tools share the commands.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. The test commands are reachable by anyone with a console on a host or server: NETPLACE moves any player's pawn, NETPROBE prints state. Harmless for a normal player (the console key was made available by D-7) but it is cheat-capable surface in a listen server's host. DO_ENABLE_NET_TEST 1 is Epic's constant, so the packet-loss hooks are compiled in everywhere. The harness needed several fixes for its own weaknesses (placement that slid back, melee/empty weapons, a probe taken too early, GL client giving up).

Status. The suites above are the status. wine is opt-in and needs the Windows run tree.

Off switch. Not compiled out. Unused unless a command or -nettest= is given.

#40.15 WebRTC network driver for the browser (E-2)
