What the player/owner sees. In the browser, "Host a game" in the launcher starts a listen server; "Copy invite link" gives a link; a friend who opens it joins the match. Both play (spawn, move, shoot, score) and leave cleanly.

Where.

  • New code, Source/WebRTCDrv/ (web build only: its CMakeLists.txt returns early off Emscripten; commits 941326a, 738b6e9): Inc/WebRTCDrv.h (116 lines, the frozen C ABI and the two classes), Src/WebRTCNetDriver.cpp (340 lines, UWebRTCNetDriver : UNetDriver: InitConnect :82, InitListen :120, TickDispatch :147, LogStats :244, PushListenStatus :276, LowLevelDestroy :315, NormalizeConfigDefaults :47), Src/WebRTCConnection.cpp (93 lines, UWebRTCConnection : UNetConnection, 512-byte packets), Src/WebRTCDrv.cpp. Ported from engine-ut2004's driver (603 + 113 + 183 lines); the in-game server-browser file and the deferred-connect export were not ported.
  • Epic's code changed: Source/Engine/Src/UnGame.cpp:1426-1445 (UGameEngine::Browse, #ifdef __EMSCRIPTEN__): a LOCAL URL that carries the option connect and a map is the frontend's join key <room>_<serverSlug>; the URL's Host becomes that key, the map FURL::DefaultMap, and the connect option is removed (a partial travel copies every option, so leaving it would make a later real map name a host). Web-only; native builds do not compile it.
  • Launcher/build: Source/SDLLaunch/Src/SDLLaunch.cpp:50-51, 350-359, 527-528 (registers the classes; ReplaceMissingDrivers switches an Avaris.ini saved by an older web build from IpDrv.TcpNetDriver to the WebRTC driver); Source/SDLLaunch/CMakeLists.txt:40-43; Source/SDLLaunch/cmake/Web.cmake:106-115 adds --js-library vendor/glue/webrtc-shim.js; vendor/glue/webrtc-shim.js is the frontend's ABI-1 shim (byte-identical to engine-ut2004's, commit 547bd73); root CMakeLists.txt:412 lists the package.
  • ini: web Engine/Config/web/Default.ini:25 NetworkDevice=WebRTCDrv.WebRTCNetDriver, :153-164 [WebRTCDrv.WebRTCNetDriver] (AllowDownloads=True, ConnectionTimeout=60.0 (native 30), InitialConnectTimeout=300.0 (native 20: a guest streaming the map's files the first time sends nothing while it loads), KeepAliveTime=0.2, MaxClientRate=20000, RelevantTimeout=5.0, SpawnPrioritySeconds=1.0, ServerTravelPause=4.0, tick rates 60, DownloadManagers=Engine.ChannelDownload); generated by tools/dev/sync-web-ini.py (DELTAS + WEB_SECTIONS); README rows Engine/Config/README.md:76-77. The driver substitutes the web values for any config property that is not positive (an old Avaris.ini without the section would otherwise leave ConnectionTimeout=0, RelevantTimeout=0...).
  • Script: none. Frontend: targets/uc2 network block and notices, uc2.profile.js milestones, uc2-wasm-net.spec.js (40.16, 40.19). Test script: tools/dev/e2-web-net.sh.

How the parts fit. Division of labour: the engine's driver owns the engine connection (UNetDriver/UNetConnection: one engine packet per data-channel message); the frontend's glue owns signalling (the PeerServer), WebRTC peers and data channels; webrtc-shim.js forwards the nine webrtc_* calls the driver makes to the glue (send/receive through shared ring buffers on the engine's worker, the rest proxied to the main thread where RTCPeerConnection lives).

  • Host: InitListen builds "<room>/<server name>" (room from the room= option; server name from ?ServerName=, else the config value [Engine.GameReplicationInfo] ServerName, because UC2's PlayerProfile.SetServerName (Engine/Classes/PlayerProfile.uc:764) overwrites the class default with the profile's name ("Liandri Corporation") at start-up and never writes the ini) and calls webrtc_init_listen; the glue registers the peer uc2-<room>_<serverSlug>-srv. Each tick it accepts pipes (an ACCEPTC_Ignore pipe is parked and offered again, not refused, so a guest does not livelock during a travel), pumps packets, turns a pipe the glue reports closed into USOCK_Closed, and about once a second pushes name, map, players and game type for the glue's status replies.
  • Guest: the frontend starts the engine on <room>_<serverSlug>?connect; Browse turns it into a network URL; InitConnect dials uc2-<room>_<serverSlug>-srv. The client has exactly one transport slot, so a server travel (a second InitConnect while the old level's driver still ticks) detaches the old connection instead of closing it.
  • UC2 differences from the UT2004 driver (each for a UC2 reason): UpdateLastReceiveTime() on every received packet (UC2's "standby cheat" check fires NotifyStandbyHack after 1 s of silence otherwise); no per-tick emscripten_sleep (the engine has run on a worker since M4w; the template's sleep would cap a lone listen server at 10 fps); PreServerTravel(){} as UTcpNetDriver; the listen status from Notify->NotifyGetLevel(); peer prefix uc2.
  • Without a layer: without the .ini section the driver substitutes defaults; without the frontend (no Module.UE) the shim's calls throw [ue-abi] Module.UE.net not injected; natively none of this is compiled.

Why. PLAN Phase E-2 / owner decision (2026-10-04): "Net: WebRTCDrv ported; ProxyNetDrv not." and "P2P only (the owner's choice for uc2)".

Decisions and alternatives. Closing the driver's connection when the pipe closes (as ICMP does natively; otherwise only the timeout ends it). No in-game server browser: UC2's own System Link browser is UdpBeacon/ClientBeaconReceiver over UDP, which a browser cannot send; mapping beacons to the glue's server rows is "a task of its own". No proxy mode.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • A guest that vanishes (tab closed, crash) pauses the host's game. UC2's standby check (Epic's Xbox anti-cheat: no packet from an in-game client for 1 s) calls SetPause; the host is dropped only at ConnectionTimeout (60 s) or after the glue's 90 s reconnect grace, whichever is first. A guest that leaves through the game is dropped at once (the engine's channel close). Natively the closed socket's ICMP drops it quickly. Left as Epic's behaviour; recorded as an owner note.
  • The data channel the glue opens is reliable and ordered ([PIPE] rtc=ordered=true/maxRetx=null), not the unordered channel webrtc.md and WebRTCDrv.h describe: while the pipe is up a datagram is never lost, loss happens only while a pipe is dead and the session layer reconnects. The engine's resends run on top of that (the wrap and packet-0 fixes of 40.13 apply). Head-of-line blocking under bad networks is therefore possible; never measured on a real connection. (Superseded (2026-10-09/10): see section 60, 40.15.)
  • Not done: in-game discovery; two guests at once (the third page costs ~1.6 GB of browser memory; the spec has no such test); servertravel / map change over WebRTC (the one-slot detach logic is the template's and passes natively, but was not run in a browser); hosting from UC2's own in-game network menus (no room= then; the glue uses the persisted room); a host that vanishes (guest side); package download over WebRTC (every peer has the full tree; AllowDownloads=True with Engine.ChannelDownload); NETDROP/NETPKTID in the browser (they pass natively).
  • A browser has no UDP: the host logs UdpBeacon ... BindPort: socket failed (UC2 spawns its system-link beacon on a listen server); harmless.
  • Two Chromium instances need ~3.1 GB PSS and a 5.7 GB cgroup; the net spec needs a 16 GB machine budget.
  • STUN is the launcher's default (stun.l.google.com) and the broker the default PeerServer (unrealpeer-we.azurewebsites.net); two browsers behind strict NATs need a TURN server (launcher Advanced panel). Not tried across a real NAT.

Status. Measured, uc2-wasm-net.spec.js (2 tests; passes recorded in webrtc.md steps 3, 4, 6 and in the coordinator's run of 2026-10-08): the host's [WebRTC] InitListen: room ... server name ... and the registered peer id; the guest's join-key browse, pipe FULLY OPEN, Welcomed by server: LEVEL=DM-Deadbolt, level up, host Join succeeded; both spawn by a real click held 1 s (the console Fire spawns nobody) and the match starts; the guest holds W and the host sees its pawn move (651-901 units), rest positions agree to 0.0-0.4 units; the guest's Suicide counts on the host and replicates back; disconnect removes the guest's PRI on the host in under one probe (2.6 s); under UC2_E2E_NET_SIM='PktLoss=5&PktLag=50' on both ends: measured loss 3-8 % in, lag 166-183 ms, walk 668 units, agreement 0.0, but the leave took the host's 60 s timeout (the simulation's delay queue held the close); the launcher-form flow on a fresh install (server name generated Another Unreal Server - c12abc, room default-public-room, the engine listens under exactly those). On localhost the link carries ~28-30 packets/s each way at 0.5-1 KB/s, 0 % loss, 38-44 ms lag. Native regressions after the branch: net-test.sh dedicated1/dedicated2/listen/travel 32 PASS. Browser boot 4/4. Not verified: everything under "not done", real-world links.

Off switch. None for the driver itself: an ini that names IpDrv.TcpNetDriver is switched back to the WebRTC driver at launch (ReplaceMissingDrivers). Nothing goes online unless the player hosts or opens an invite link.

#The frontend (ufrontend, branch uc2, 42 commits 4affbe7..5654da2)

The frontend is a separate repository (the shared launcher, build and test shell for UT2004, UT99, Unreal 1 and now UC2). Everything below is on the local branch uc2; nothing was pushed. 42 files changed, +4,075 / -34 lines. Of those, 9 files are the new targets/uc2/ (data), 6 are new e2e files, and the other 27 touch shared code, shared tests or shared docs, listed in 40.17, 40.18 and 40.20 and again in the closing list. Input and profile features (D-3/D-4 name ownership, the saves and keys specs) are covered by section 30; here each gets one line.

#40.16 The uc2 target: descriptor, manifests, branding, precache

What the player/owner sees. A fourth game card in the launcher ("UC2 Web (experimental)", chip v2226), the Play panel with DeathMatch and Capture the Flag, 3 bots / 10 minutes toggles, a Multiplayer panel with P2P only, Advanced fields for the audio buffer, and a sound/graphics capability check.

Where. targets/uc2/ (all new; first commit 2d32173, later 591384d, d6554d4, 2ecfa57, 5785ebd, 341635a, 495a7f3, 4419272, 8510f59, 9e1f22b, 5c87870, 5654da2):

  • target.json (182 lines): id uc2, engineFamily UE2, abiVersion 1; publish (folder uc2, chip v2226, chipWarn: true, engine repo engine-uc2 branch main, preset dir .); assetMarkers (SHA-256 of System/Core.u, 72,051 bytes, package version 151; required files; forbidden XGame.u/BotPack.u so another game's tree is refused); storage (cacheBudgetMb 2048, memfsBudgetMb 1024); network (peer prefix uc2, public room, serverName read from Avaris.ini [Engine.GameReplicationInfo] ServerName with stock Another Unreal Server, modes P2P only, networkDevice, playerName with ownedBy); manifestExclude; userFiles ["System/Profiles/*.PRO"]; defaults (map DM-Deadbolt, game UCGameplay.UCDeathmatch, engine options -log); videoDrivers (GL4ES default, GL4ES without post-processing, null); travel (DeathMatch, CTF; 3 bots, 10 min; no presets, no mutators); capabilities (required: secure context, cross-origin isolation, SharedArrayBuffer, wasm threads and features, workers, Atomics.wait, OffscreenCanvas + WebGL on it, OPFS sync access; recommended: GPU acceleration, IndexedDB, webAudio, webrtc, pointerLock); audioBuffer {10 ms x 3}; notices (join-failed, join-refused, version-mismatch, host-failed, regexes on the engine's own lines); telemetry (LoadMap: and Critical: patterns).
  • file-manifest.json (1,224 entries, 1,991 MB at 5654da2) and dependency-manifest.json (208 packages, 1,804 import references, all resolved), both generated from the owner's local asset tree with generate-manifest.cjs and generate-deps.cjs (commands in the README).
  • precache.json: 17 System/*.u packages + Maps/Entry.unr + Maps/Menu.unr (10.5 MB).
  • branding/{theme.css,logo.svg,help.md,portal-notes.json}: neutral placeholders that imitate no game's or company's branding.
  • README.md (173 lines).

How the parts fit. The shared frontend reads the descriptor and builds the page, the file system manifest, the -ini: seeds (player name, server name, network device) and the engine command line; the engine reads the manifest-backed files on demand. The manifest has to match the asset tree the artifact is served with: after D-4, D-6, saveconfig and D-9 each rebuilt GUI.u, UCGameplay.u and UCMenu.u, a frontend commit regenerated the manifests (4419272, 8510f59, 9e1f22b, 5c87870).

Why. PLAN M11: "frontend targets/uc2/*, v151 support in generate-deps/manifest/travel, e2e boot/net specs, gates".

Decisions and alternatives. P2P only (owner's choice). Travel limited to the two game classes proven on this build (the CTF toggle is labelled "not yet verified on the web build"). The cooked sound and movies are in the manifest (asset tree v1) so they are served like packages; their by-products are excluded. Player files (System/Profiles/**, *.PRO, Avaris.ini, User.ini) are in manifestExclude so the site never serves one player's saves; userFiles makes the browser keep them.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • The manifests are generated from files that are not in any repository (assets/uc2 is never committed). Anyone without that tree cannot regenerate them or build the target; a rebuilt .u needs manifest, dependency manifest and engine to move together.
  • User-facing text is out of date: branding/help.md still says "null render device and null audio ... no sound ... no multiplayer yet" and branding/portal-notes.json says "Not playable yet. The web engine has no render, audio or network device, so the screen stays empty." and "must not be published as playable". The engine now renders, plays sound and plays online. The portal-notes file feeds the card on the published portal page (its own comment says so) and help.md is the target's help.
  • README.md still has the first-light statements (593 files / 1,094.7 MB; "what the engine needs next: ... a GL device, audio, a net driver"); the root README.md row says "boots to the menu with no rendering".
  • Sound and movies are not in precache.json ("not added yet" in the README): the cue tables, menu music and logos load on demand (3.1-3.6 s to first sound on a busy machine).
  • Total manifest 1,991 MB (1,899 MiB) against a cacheBudgetMb of 2048: a player who touches everything stays just below the cache budget, with no headroom for a second version of the assets.
  • The CTF travel entry and the 3 bots/10 min toggles were not run through the launcher in a spec (the net spec drives ?map=DM-Deadbolt&Game=UCGameplay.UCDeathmatch).
  • No language option for sound and movies: the game language comes from [Engine.Engine] Language in Avaris.ini (int by default); PLAN E-4 (a language option for every engine) is not done, so a web player cannot pick German voices from the launcher.
  • Chrome-based browsers only were exercised.

Status. Measured: yarn test 28/28 and yarn gates "all CI-tier gates green" at the E-2 commits (webrtc.md step 3); the boot, audio and net specs below. Not verified: other browsers; a clean-machine build (the owner's preview is built on the coordinator's machine).

Off switch. Not applicable (a target is data); remove targets/uc2 and its registrations to drop it.

#40.17 Package versions 145-151 in the shared glue (affects every target)

What the player/owner sees. Nothing; it lets the build tools read UC2's .u/.unr/.utx headers so the dependency prefetch and the manifest work.

Where. packages/glue/lib/unreal/reader.cjs (+99): new tableLayout(version), VER_INT_INDEX 145, VER_SHORT_RECORDS 150; from 145 every compact index is a plain i32, from 150 the import record's package index is an i16 and the export record starts with a 10-byte head of five i16 (class, super, package are words 0, 2, 4 on load); Cur.i16(), tableEnds. packages/glue/bin/generate-deps.cjs (+39) uses the same tableLayout. packages/glue/lib/unreal/property-codec.cjs (+26): assertCodecVersion makes the tagged-property decoder throw on a version >= 145 ("not supported here yet"). writer.cjs (+7, comment: it only writes v128). Tests packages/glue/test/unreal-package-versions.test.cjs (350 lines, 11 tests, synthesised v128/v147/v151 through both readers plus the real tree when .ufront/publish.local.json names assets.uc2), _target-fixtures.cjs, target-config.test.cjs. Commit c490326 (2026-10-04). Docs: glue CHANGELOG.md, README.md.

How the parts fit. Engine-side counterpart: UC2's UnLinker.cpp/UnObj.cpp (FCompactIndex<<) define the layouts. The two glue readers (build-time generate-deps, run-time reader.cjs for generators) share one function so they cannot disagree.

Why. Without it yarn deps cannot read a version-151 package (PLAN: "Frontend Node tools must learn package v151").

Decisions and alternatives. One place decides the layout; the tagged-property codec was not extended (it refuses instead of decoding plausible garbage; the v148+ tag layout is not implemented), so the QuickScreenshots generator does not work for uc2.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Shared with the other targets. Below version 145 the code path is the old one; the CHANGELOG states that on the four older targets' trees the reader's tables (SHA-256 over every package) and every dependency manifest are byte-identical before and after, and packages/e2e/tools/deps-validate.mjs passes unchanged. Not re-run for this report (no tests are run here); it rests on the commit's record. generate-manifest's GUID reader needed no change.

Status. Measured (commit record): all 208 packages of the UC2 tree end exactly where their own tables and export extents say, every import chain is rooted in a Core.Package, all 1,804 import references resolve; unit tests pass.

Off switch. None.

#40.18 Shared frontend code added for UC2 (name ownership, user files): one line each, details in section 30

  • network.playerName.ownedBy (D-4), commit 0b269e9 (+ target data 5785ebd, 341635a): the launcher's name field is disabled while the game's own identity names the player (UC2: User.ini [Profiles] LastProfile). Shared code: packages/frontend/src/components/NetworkPanel.vue, ProxyNetworkPanel.vue, stores/net.ts (nameOwner, resolveNameOwner, nameOwnerHint), config/network.ts, env.d.ts; packages/glue/src/config/ini-identity.js (readPersistedNameOwner) and src/index.js; docs docs/06-targets-and-branding.md, packages/frontend/README.md, packages/glue/CHANGELOG.md; tests config-name-owner.test.ts (107 lines), ini-identity.test.cjs (+93). A target without ownedBy never disables the field (UT2004, UT99, Unreal 1 are unaffected by design). Section 30 explains D-4.
  • Player name through -ini:, profiles as user files, player files never served (D-3/C-2), commit d6554d4: target data only.
  • Saves spec and keys spec (uc2-wasm-saves.spec.js, uc2-wasm-keys.spec.js, opt-in, 542 and 255 lines): section 30.

#40.19 The uc2 end-to-end specs

What the owner sees. Playwright specs that drive the real engine in Chromium and print evidence lines.

Where. packages/e2e/ (all under the memory guard tools/e2e-mem-guard.mjs; run through tools/dev/e2-web-net.sh in the engine repo, which also proves serve-web/ holds the artifact under test and mutes the output with a PulseAudio null sink):

  • lib/engines/uc2.profile.js (109 lines): the engine-specific data the shared harness needs: boot, render, host and client milestone regexes (the engine's log lines, which come WITHOUT the Log: tag with -log), bootSurface, netSurface, dmGameArgs.
  • uc2-wasm-boot.spec.js (785 lines, 4 tests, in the default set): menu with the GL device; DM-Deadbolt by URL with post-processing; keyboard + mouse play test (INSTANT ACTION, Fire, W, mouse look); mouse look linear (D-6). It launches with -nostartupmovies (125679e), so it never sees the logos.
  • uc2-wasm-audio.spec.js (392 lines, 3 tests, opt-in, commits a51b743, 1cd2925): (1) the menu plays its music through openal-soft on Web Audio and the output is not silent (log lines for the device, the 23 cue tables, a wave pack, the music; the AudioContext running; an AnalyserNode on the worklet node: audible share > 0.5); (2) the logos play with their sound and on time (-NULLMOVIES); (3) a key press ends the logos and the music follows. It builds its own browser context with --mute-audio (the suite's fixture ignores launchOptions; the first run proved the flag had no effect: only PULSE_SINK kept it off the speakers).
  • uc2-wasm-net.spec.js (587 lines, 2 tests, in the config's list): see 40.15.
  • uc2-wasm-saves.spec.js, uc2-wasm-keys.spec.js: section 30.
  • playwright.config.js: adds <target>-wasm-audio, -saves, -keys to the testMatch list (shared file; harmless for other targets, which have no such specs).
  • README.md (+39): the uc2 profile, boot spec and net spec.

How the parts fit. The specs read the engine's log lines: the engine and the profile regexes must change together.

Why. PLAN M11 gate "uc2 E2E green".

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. Not runnable without the local engine artifact and asset tree; hard-coded localhost:8080 and server ports (the runner refuses to start when another guard or port is busy); log text is the interface (a reworded engine line fails a spec); the audio check is "not silent", not "correct"; the audio spec needs the cooked files in the served tree (README); several checks are timing- or load-sensitive (an earlier audio-spec attempt was stopped by the memory guard at 12.1 GB while other work ran; LOG records reruns); the net spec asserts nothing about two guests, travel, or a real network; browser runs measure memory from outside the page and need a 16 GB budget.

Status. Measured (coordinator, 2026-10-08, logs/coord/a13/summary.txt): boot 4/4, net 2/2, audio 3/3; saves and keys passed on a rerun after the memory guard stopped the first attempt (saves reported a browser profile outliving the test: a teardown complaint).

Off switch. The audio, saves and keys specs are opt-in (named explicitly).

#40.20 Docs and CI-gate edits

Where (shared files). .ufront/gates/no-engine-specifics.sh (6 lines): the uc2 literal is now forbidden in generic glue/frontend source, like ut2004, ut99, unreal1 (commit 2d32173); README.md (one table row); docs/06-targets-and-branding.md (the ownedBy field); docs/migration/STATE.md (a 2026-10-04 "NEW TARGET uc2 (first light)" entry); test/skeleton.test.mjs (adds uc2 to the manifest-vs-engine-Paths= check and two captured console lines); packages/frontend/test/capabilities.test.ts (adds uc2).

Disadvantages, risks, deviations. The gate change is a new restriction on generic code, correct but a shared CI behaviour.

#Open issues in this area

Ordered by what most affects a player or a decision of the owner.

Not verified by a person, only measured

  1. Nobody has listened to the audio against a real Xbox. Mix level (+/- a dB or two: OpenAL Soft's centred gain is -1.10 / -1.52 dB, XACT's routing unknown), HRTF colour (not reproduced) and the zone reverb level (parameters carried 1:1, decay times measured, loudness not calibrated) are open. Captures were sent to the owner (LOG 2026-10-04); no verdict is recorded.
  2. The movie picture in a real browser has no assertion (the boot spec disables the logos; the audio spec checks sound and time only). The native ES 2 twin with LIBGL_NOBGRA=1 matched frame for frame, which is the nearest evidence.
  3. The cause of the owner's original "no sound, no movies, errors in the console" on the native build was never identified (the question to the owner in PLAN.md is unanswered); the web build, which had neither before O-2, is the likely source.
  4. Web audio latency (input to ear) and long-session stability were never measured; only Chromium was used.

Content and data 5. 19 source WAVs missing, 15 cues silent; whether to decode them from the shipped .xwb is an open owner decision (BLOCKERS.md). 6. Localised Sapphire taunts (and one Spanish tutorial line) play the wrong line, by decision (40.8). Movie language tracks 1-4 rest on Epic's code with no reference recording. 7. Inferred XACT semantics: priority direction, "Recurring = fade", unset-weight default, cue radius 4 for sounds with no 3D block (other players' non-3D cues are culled beyond 4 units).

Features not done 8. Animated loading movie, attract mode (UC2_Attract, A51), the Xbox legal and rating screens. 9. Never run: voice stealing, split-screen listener placement, pause/resume, three playlist types, outro cinematics, a MovieMaterial with sound (the menu's "View movies"), non-English movie tracks in game. 10. In-game server discovery on the web (UC2's System Link is UDP broadcast), two guests, travel over WebRTC, hosting from the in-game network menus, package download over WebRTC, any real network, a NAT without TURN. 11. Native network: team games/CTF online, package download, HTTP redirect download (class missing), split-screen connections, voice, LAN beacon, admin, match end by limit, more than 3 clients.

Risks that can bite 12. A guest that closes the tab pauses the host's game for up to 60 s (UC2's standby check; Epic's behaviour; a host-side mitigation such as disabling the check for the WebRTC driver was not made). 13. A teleporter moving a live player pawn online may be undone by UC2's ServerMove rewind (LastServerMoveLocation); only respawns and the test placement command were verified. 14. The packet-ack and package-map fixes need both ends on the build; an old peer can hang or desync. 15. OpenAL Soft is fetched by branch (ufront/web), not by commit, from GitHub at configure time; the 32-bit native game falls back from PipeWire to PulseAudio until libpipewire-0.3-modules:i386 is installed; real Windows output (WASAPI) untested. 16. Music is decoded on the game thread (16 ms hitch in the browser every ~0.74 s); wave packs are read whole and stay resident. 17. Test commands (NETPLACE, ...) and -NETOLDACKS are compiled into the shipped Engine and reachable from the console. 18. The WebRTC data channel is reliable/ordered while the driver's header and notebook say unordered; head-of-line blocking on a bad network is unmeasured.

Frontend 19. branding/help.md, branding/portal-notes.json, targets/uc2/README.md header, the root README.md row and STATE.md still describe the first-light build (no rendering, no audio, no network). portal-notes.json says "must not be published as playable". 20. Manifests (file-manifest.json, dependency-manifest.json) come from a local asset tree that is in no repository; they must be regenerated whenever a rebuilt .u changes; precache.json has no sound or movie file; 1,991 MB of manifest against a 2,048 MB cache budget; no language option (E-4). 21. The e2e specs depend on the engine's log text and on a local artifact, and are not in a CI run.

#50. Third-person character jitter (O-11), merged after the snapshot

Merged into main as c59c02d (branch jitter: 5f09fb4, c46bb34, 51d1ffc, cd4f20a, f874f28). Notebook: docs/porting/analysis/jitter.md. Line numbers below are main at c59c02d.

Summary:

  • The jitter you reported ("the camera moves forward, but the character stays still in place ... and then it updates") was real and came from our own change D-6 (the mouse's rigid camera, section 30), not from the renderer or the web build.
  • The fix is three small script changes in UCGameplay.u: the frame is now drawn from where the pawn is drawn. Nothing is smoothed and the pad's camera is not touched.
  • The C++ side only adds test tooling: a per-frame trace (FRAMETRACE) and repeatable uneven frame times (-framepattern=).
  • What is left on screen is the camera's walk bob (Epic's UCPawn.CheckBob), a smooth sway of about ±8 px at 1080p.

#The mouse camera is drawn from where the pawn is drawn

What you see. With the mouse owning the look, in third person, your character stays fixed on screen while it runs, at any frame rate and with uneven browser frame times. Before the fix it lurched ahead after a long frame and dropped back after a short one.

Where.

  • Script, Source/UCGameplay/Classes/UCPlayerController.uc (package UCGameplay.u, rebuilt):
    • MouseLookCamera (~3067): stamps MouseCameraTime = Level.TimeSeconds whenever it places the rigid camera.
    • new MouseCameraLeftBehind() (~3081): true when this frame's camera was the mouse's, the pawn is the view target, the mouse owns the look, and the pawn has moved since the view was computed (Pawn.Location != OldPawnLocation).
    • PlayerCalcView (~3162): one more condition in Epic's "compute the view again" test: || MouseCameraLeftBehind().
  • Script, Source/UCGameplay/Classes/UCPlayerInput.uc:97: var transient float MouseCameraTime. UCPlayerInput has no native C++ class, so no struct layout changes.
  • Engine (test tooling only, quiet by default):
    • Source/Engine/Src/UC2FrameTrace.cpp (new): console command FRAMETRACE <frames> [tag] and the switch -frametrace=. They log one line per drawn frame: the camera used, the pawn's location and velocity, the frame time, and the pawn's projected screen position.
    • Source/Engine/Src/UnGame.cpp (+8): the hooks for it in UGameEngine Exec and Draw.
    • Source/SDLLaunch/Src/SDLLaunch.cpp (~232-263): -framepattern=<ms>:<ms>:..., which plays back a fixed sequence of frame lengths, so a browser's uneven pacing can be repeated natively.
  • Tools:
    • tools/dev/jit-trace.sh: a native run (null renderer, 1920x1080, DM-Deadbolt at a fixed spot) in four 1.3 s segments (forward, back, strafe right, strafe left), for keyboard+mouse or pad, at 60 fps, uncapped or uneven.
    • jit-analyze.py: computes judder (each frame's distance from the line between its two neighbours) and the slope b (how far ahead the pawn sits, plotted against velocity × frame time).
    • jit-strip.sh / jit-strip.py: consecutive real GPU frames of the pawn, before and after.
    • tools/dev/script-build.sh now also accepts jit* trees.
  • Frontend: packages/e2e/uc2-wasm-jitter.spec.js (ufrontend 156d736). It is opt-in, has a line in playwright.config.js, and makes the same measurement in the browser from the FRAMETRACE lines.

How the parts fit.

  • The whole fix is in UCGameplay.u. Native code, data and the frontend are unchanged by it.
  • Without the rebuilt UCGameplay.u you get Epic's lazy camera everywhere (the shipped package has no D-6), which never showed this. With D-6's UCGameplay.u but without this fix, you get the jitter you saw.
  • UCGameplay is a server package, so a client and its server must carry the same build. Native trees, the web manifest and servers switch together (the web manifest's only changed entry is system/ucgameplay.u).

Why.

  • UC2 computes a frame's view early in the tick: PlayerWalking.PlayerMove → CalculateAimInfo → PlayerCalcView (UCGameplay.cpp:176-178). That runs before the pawn's physics, because the pawn ticks after its controller (UnLevTic.cpp:169-174). PlayerCalcView then hands that cached view to the frame's drawing.
  • Epic's lazy camera closes a share of the gap proportional to the frame time. Its lag is therefore v/k at any frame time, and drawing from the early view never shows.
  • D-6's rigid mouse camera sits exactly behind the pawn. So every frame drew the pawn its own move of that frame ahead of its rest spot. The trace fits ahead = 1.000 × v·dt with a residual of 0.00-0.01 units, in all four directions.
  • With even frames that is a constant offset and invisible. With uneven frames (any browser, or a native hitch) it changes every frame.

Decisions and alternatives.

  • We fixed the order of operations, not added smoothing. Rejected options:
    • Smooth the mouse camera's position the way the lazy camera does: this hides the cause and puts back the lag D-6 removed on purpose.
    • Move the camera calculation after physics for everyone: this changes Epic's aim order and the pad's feel.
  • The view is computed a second time only when it is the mouse's rigid camera and the pawn has moved. The aim, the pad's lazy camera and the end-of-round focused views keep Epic's single calculation.

Disadvantages, risks, deviations.

  • The view drawn can differ from the one the aim trace used in that tick, by up to v·dt (about 7-25 units). It is a parallel shift with the same rotation, and the next tick aims from the drawn view. The agent judged it negligible; hit rates were not measured.
  • The walk bob remains. The rigid camera carries all of it, about ±8 px of smooth sway at 1080p; the lazy camera passes about 60-70% of it. If you still call that sway jittery, the follow-up is to smooth or drop the bob under the mouse only (a decision for you).
  • The pad's strafe segments keep 0.6-2.2 px of judder at every frame rate. This is identical with the shipped scripts, and comes from the lazy camera trailing the pawn vertically on the test spot's ramp.

Status (measured). Repeated by the coordinator on main (logs/coord/a14, LOG.md entry O-11):

  • Native kb+mouse: uneven frames 0.53 px, 60 fps 0.26 px, uncapped 0.01 px (limit 1.0).
  • Pad: 1.87 px worst segment, identical with the pre-fix UCGameplay.u (also 1.87).
  • Browser jitter spec: judder 0.50-0.70 px.
  • Browser boot, keys and saves specs pass; m10-controls.sh 5/5.
  • d6-mouse.sh 67/2: the spawn-dependent lock check, and the pad walk check at a spawn by a wall. Rerun twice: 21/0, walk -353/+408 units.

The agent's own numbers:

Configuration Before (judder RMS per segment) After
Native kb+mouse, uneven 16.8-33.8 ms frames 10.5 / 11.0 / 20.4 / 20.7 px, b = 1.000 0.53 / 0.34 / 0.51 / 0.52 px, b = 0.000
Native kb+mouse, 60 fps up to 3.0 px (one 37.5 ms hitch: 20 px) 0.18-0.27 px
Native kb+mouse, uncapped 0.20-0.80 px 0.01 px
Native pad (lazy camera) 0.15-0.44 px unchanged
Browser kb+mouse, 1024x768, frames 20.8-57 ms 4.1 / 7.3 / 11.4 / 10.9 px, b ≈ 1 0.78 / 0.50 / 0.56 / 0.53 px, b ≈ 0
  • First person was never affected: b = 0.000 there before the fix too.
  • Regressions: verifyclasses OK; m10-controls.sh 5/5 pass; browser boot spec 4/4; d6-mouse.sh 68 pass, 1 fail. The failing check (lock-pulls-back-after-the-mouse-stops) depends on the random spawn. Pinned to that spawn it fails identically on main without the fix, and the new condition cannot act there because the pawn stands still.
  • Pictures: logs/jit/strip/strip-2.png (12 consecutive frames per row, before and after) and logs/jit/strip/strip-2.gif.
  • Not done: a physical pad; real Windows.

Off switch. None of its own. Using the shipped UCGameplay.u removes D-6 as a whole.

#00 Overview

  • "Start here" item 3: the mouse no longer needs UC2InputMigrate.cpp (deleted, F-1) nor the D-6 bindings; see 30.5. Item 5: the web build draws on WebGL 2 since F-3 (WebGL 1 is the fallback); see R-20.

Still open: 5 (the standby pause), 9 (the web profile layout, with the profiles revisit), 10 (GitHub: a private 10-p/engine-uc2 exists since 2026-10-10; CI after the merge).

#10. Platform, build and runtime

  • Feature 8, the web runtime (F-4a, F-4b step 3, F-4d, F-3). C++ exception catching is OFF for the engine on the web, with engine-ut2004's fail-soft loaders (Core/Src/UnObj.cpp, UnLinker.cpp, Engine/Src/UnGame.cpp, IpDrv/Src/TcpLink.cpp, UdpLink.cpp); only openal-soft keeps exceptions. The report's reason for real catching was wrong: Editor.TransBuffer never threw (VerifyImport's SafeReplace branch logs it). wasm -8%, Node census 58/58 maps, 0 aborts. Frame yield exactly as engine-ut2004 (one yield per frame, only when the limiter did not sleep; a hidden tab mutes the listener; ut2004's ASYNCIFY_IMPORTS); -sOFFSCREEN_FRAMEBUFFER kept on purpose. meta.json records the fetched libraries (cmake/ArtifactDeps.cmake, --dep-gl4es, --dep-openal-soft). The WebGL link limits are 1..2 since F-3 (Source/SDLLaunch/cmake/Web.cmake:121-123). Truth: platform-f4.md steps 2, 3, 5; LOG.md 2026-10-09.
  • Feature 9, -ini: (F-4b). The command line holds 4,096 characters (was 1,023, cut silently in SDLLaunch): a token that does not fit is dropped whole with a warning (SDLLaunch.cpp:524-526). Every test switch we had added is now a test key read ONCE with appReadTestKey/appReadTestFlag (Core/Inc/UnFile.h:319) and never kept in the ini; kept switches: -log, -ini:, -windowed, -defaultres, -usehomedir, the three renderer selectors, commandlet parameters. -ini: itself is unchanged. Truth: platform-f4.md "The switch table, final".
  • Feature 13, the inis (F-4d, F-4 follow-up, F-1). Default.ini, DefUser.ini and the web pair are LF; Epic's three reference copies (DefaultXbox.ini, DefUserXbox.ini, KeyBinds.ini) are Epic's bytes again (CRLF, 9d5683a); sync-web-ini.py refuses a CR in our inis and --check compares the three with Epic's. No migration in code any more: UC2InputMigrate.cpp is gone (F-1, owner: "the curated ini is the way"). New keys since: [Engine.Engine] FrameRateCap (0 = uncapped) and FpsLogSeconds, [UC2ALAudio.UC2ALAudioSubsystem] UseHRTF, WaveFile, UseEFX, [UC2GL4ESDrv.UC2GL4ESRenderDevice] GLESVersion=3, [UCGameplay.UCPlayerInput] bLazyMouseCamera; bKeysAsStick and MouseLockBreakAngle removed (hard-wired). Appendix C has them all.
  • Also in 10: the arm64 preset configured and built for the first time (F-4c: ucc-bin verifyclasses under qemu, 401 classes, 0 mismatches); "Never verified: an arm64 configure or build" no longer holds (no arm64 hardware run). UC2_ASSETS_DIR removed from CMake (F-4d). New in Engine: UC2Cheats.cpp (WHEREAMI, TELEPORT; NETPLACE/NETPROBE behind the same guard), UC2FpsLog.cpp (F-2), UC2FrameTrace.cpp (O-11).

#20. The renderer

  • R-10 DOF alpha (F-5, O-3). The compiled default of DOFShippedBlurAlpha is True (Source/UC2GLDrv/Src/UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:217), so an ini without the key also gets the shipped 65; ini and code agree. You confirmed 65 (it is an alpha, not a radius; no A/B). LOG.md's missing O-3 line: written for the coordinator in logs/d34/log-o3.md.
  • R-11 DistortionCullDistance (F-5). One source of truth: a config property of each device class, read from the device's own section ([UC2GLDrv.UC2GLRenderDevice] on the desktop, [UC2GL4ESDrv.UC2GL4ESRenderDevice] for GL4ES); the global GUC2GLDistortionCullDistance and the hard-coded section are gone; compiled default 15000 (UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:219). Measured: the twin reads 7000 from its own section while the desktop keeps 15000 (render-f5.md step 1). Open issue 13's "read only from the UC2GLDrv section" no longer holds.
  • R-18 anti-aliasing (F-3, F-4b). FXAA is removed (8adc4a1). OpenGL ES 3 / WebGL 2 (the web and the twin, through GL4ES' ES 3 back end) gets real 4x multisampling with resolve, as the desktop; OpenGL ES 2 / WebGL 1 has no anti-aliasing. The switches -glaa=N, -glnoaa, -glshotpresented are gone: AntiAliasing=<n> in the device section (set with -ini:, tools/dev/glini), test key ShotPresented. Quiet A/B: WebGL 1 + FXAA 27.5-35.4 ms vs WebGL 2 + MSAA 29.8-36.3 ms per frame (logs/coord/a16).
  • R-20 the web renderer (F-3). The device asks for WebGL 2 (GLESVersion=3, GLSL ES 3.00, LIBGL_ES=3 in our GL4ES fork 10-p/gl4es fix/cubemap-bind-active-unit) and falls back to WebGL 1. On WebGL 2 depth textures, gl_FragDepth, 32-bit indices and fragment highp are core, so the WEBGL_depth_texture gap of the fallbacks table closes there (still NOT implemented for a WebGL 1 context without the extension); 33 raw GLES calls behind GL4ES' back are gone on ES 3; the texture LOD bias is applied (the twin matches the desktop: 0.384 with, 0.871 without). The masks -glno* are the test key MaskCaps=<list>. Truth: webgl2.md; LOG.md 2026-10-09.
  • Open issue 12 (stale text): done by D-3 (UnRenDev.h:887-889, UC2GLRenderDevice.cpp:224-226), BLOCKERS by the audit of 2026-10-10. Also F-5: occlusion queries explored, not implemented (nothing visible depends on them); the shader inventory (every shader a material or effect can select is ported); the DM-Azure sky haze found and fixed (Mesa's fixed-function fog coordinate; sky difference vs the twin 46.75 -> 2.49); CTF-Aqueduct flicker not reproduced (your conditions wanted); shadow strength waits for your xemu shot.

#30. Input, UI, scripts, profiles and saves

  • 30.5 the mouse (F-1, O-11). Epic's shipped UCGameplay.u now plays with our inis: DefUser.ini binds the mouse as Epic's PC file and the retail disc do (MouseX=Count bXAxis | Axis aMouseX Speed=6.0, :101-102), so the shipped package turns the view through Epic's PC path (the stick's clamp and curve). The rebuilt UCGameplay.u takes the mouse's share out of aMouseX/aMouseY by the Count bytes and adds D-6's linear mouse on top, from the SAME ini (a D-6 User.ini with aTurn/aLookUp still works with it). UC2InputMigrate.cpp, its warning and -noinimigrate are gone; -maxfps became [Engine.Engine] FrameRateCap. New bLazyMouseCamera (Epic's lazy camera under the mouse, default off). Native: SDL's relative-mouse hints as engine-ut2004 (N counts in = N out at two speeds; no acceleration). The look keys: PageUp/PageDown are the look stick at 0.8 (Axis aMouseY Speed=+-4.0), the arrows strafe (Epic's PC layout). The rigid camera's jitter: section 50. Truth: vanilla.md steps 2-3, 6; LOG.md 2026-10-09 F-1.
  • 30.7 UC2InputMigrate: removed (F-1, owner: no auto-migration of user inis). The call in UnGame.cpp is gone; that part of UGameEngine::Init is Epic's again.
  • 30.8 keys as a stick (F-1). bKeysAsStick and MouseLockBreakAngle are hard-wired (the ini keys are gone; 25 degrees). The stick's walk on PC moved from the rebuilt script into the engine, for BOTH packages (Source/Engine/Src/UnIn.cpp:320-335, non-Xbox builds: Epic's script scales the stick only under IsXBox()). Measured: W, the left stick at 1.0/0.5/0.35 and Shift+W all run at 430 units/s (UC2 has no walk mode; you: leave it). O-14 closed by you.
  • 30.16 the KEYBOARD / MOUSE page (F-1). A key another action had is now CLEARED from that action (your D-9 decision), not swapped (UC2Settings_Keys.uc AssignKey); STRAFE LEFT/RIGHT list A, Left / D, Right; TURN LEFT/RIGHT have no key by default ("THE MOUSE TURNS"). The owner choices listed as open are decided (00 above).
  • 30.18 the .uc files and the rebuilt packages (O-11, F-1). UCPlayerController.uc (83 lines now) and UCPlayerInput.uc (221) changed again (the jitter fix, F-1's mouse share, bLazyMouseCamera, the hard-wired keys); the asset tree carries UCGameplay.u 4ed72f0b and UCMenu.u b9202a59 (SCRIPTS.txt). Reverting to the shipped packages now costs only the PC mouse, the player name without a profile and the KEYBOARD / MOUSE page: no ini to revert, no migration. Every suite runs both ways (d6-mouse.sh vanilla, m10-controls.sh on a shipped tree).
  • Open issues 2, 5, 6 decided (O-14 closed; the pad's turns stay; D-9 choices made). Item 12: the d6 lock checks were "flaky" because two PlayerStarts cannot show the pull-back; D-4 starts every movement scenario at a fixed spot (d34-docs-tests.md step 3).

  • Frontend (40.16-40.20). Since 5654da2 the frontend's uc2 branch has the F-1/F-6 manifests (165ce9d, ec41dfe) and the specs passing their test settings as -ini: keys (bd7c42b, the "without post-processing" launcher option removed).
